# Adjudicated finding catalog: ranks 1–144

This is a review artifact, not the exact-30 task submission. Ranks and scores are copied from the power-aware adjudication; the prose has been rewritten against each grounded source record and the live scoring rubric.

Rank: 1
Novelty Score: 5
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 5
Title: NAA20 perturbation couples recurrent CYP46A1 repression to induction of sterol-synthesis transcripts
Core Idea: In the atlas, Naa20 CRISPR perturbation lowered Cyp46a1 in thalamic, midbrain glutamatergic, and midbrain/pons/medulla GABAergic neurons, while sterol-synthesis transcripts rose in hypothalamic and midbrain/pons/medulla contexts. The biological interpretation is that impaired NatB function may reduce neuronal cholesterol elimination through CYP46A1 and elicit compensatory sterol synthesis; the co-occurrence of Cyp46a1 repression and Fdft1 induction in 191 MB P MY GABA makes this a testable coupling rather than an inference from cross-context absence.
Significance: If confirmed at the protein and metabolite levels, this would connect NAA20-associated neurodevelopmental disease to the principal route of brain cholesterol disposal, reframing NatB pathology around a NAA20–CYP46A1–24S-hydroxycholesterol axis. That would justify an unusually broad research direction spanning NatB substrate acetylation, neuronal sterol flux, circuit consequences, disease biomarkers, and therapeutic restoration of cholesterol turnover.
Prior expectation: Before viewing the atlas, impaired NAA20/NatB activity was expected to cause recessive neurodevelopmental disease based on [human genetics and biochemical evidence](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34230638/), while CYP46A1 was independently understood as the major route for converting brain cholesterol to 24S-hydroxycholesterol in a [review of brain cholesterol turnover](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27663182/). Those established facts did not predict that acute neuronal Naa20 perturbation would repeatedly repress Cyp46a1 or pair that response with sterol-synthesis induction.
Support: Naa20 perturbation reduced Cyp46a1 in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut (log2FC -0.39135, FDR 1.29e-13), 155 MB Glut (-0.76576, FDR 1.03e-10), and 191 MB P MY GABA (-1.03665, FDR 2.55e-6). Sterol-pathway increases included Cyp51 in 145 MH-LH TH Glut (+0.56858, FDR 0.0272), Mvk in the same class (+1.05342, FDR 0.0339), and Fdft1 in 191 MB P MY GABA (+1.02478, FDR 1.79e-5), where Cyp46a1 was also reduced. The major alternative is an indirect stress response to NAA20 perturbation rather than a sterol-regulatory role of NatB; the experiment measured neither N-terminal acetylation, CYP46A1 protein, 24S-hydroxycholesterol, nor cholesterol flux, and the partial sterol response was not identical across neuronal classes.
Research community: NatB/N-terminal-acetylation researchers, brain-cholesterol biologists, and investigators of NAA20-related neurodevelopmental disease would have the clearest reason to revise their working models. The pivotal test is neuron-resolved Naa20 editing coupled to CYP46A1 protein and isotope-based cholesterol-to-24S-hydroxycholesterol flux, with CRISPR-resistant wild-type and catalytically inactive NAA20 resolving NatB dependence from generic editing stress. A verified causal chain would establish an unusually broad agenda spanning the neuronal N-terminal acetylome, lipid turnover, circuit dysfunction, patient metabolite biomarkers, and CYP46A1-directed rescue.

Rank: 2
Novelty Score: 5
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: MATR3 perturbation induces an RBP state that active FUS and TDP-43 perturbations do not reproduce
Core Idea: Across cortical, thalamic, midbrain glutamatergic, and posterior GABAergic neurons, Matr3 perturbation repeatedly increased Atp9b, Ddhd1, E2f4, Kcnq2, Sugp2, Uggt2, and Zkscan3 while decreasing Rsrp1. FUS and TDP-43 perturbations generated substantial expression responses in matched classes but did not reproduce this signed MATR3 module. This observed divergence supports the interpretation that acute MATR3 reduction exposes an RNA-binding-protein liability state rather than a generic response to perturbing any ALS-linked RBP.
Significance: This relationship argues that MATR3-associated disease should not be subsumed under a single TDP-43/FUS stress model. If the module reflects direct MATR3-dependent RNA regulation, it would create a distinct mechanistic and biomarker framework for MATR3-linked ALS, frontotemporal degeneration, and distal myopathy, and would motivate comparative studies of why disease-associated RBPs converge in some settings yet diverge sharply in mature neurons.
Prior expectation: MATR3 was already known as a nuclear RNA-binding protein associated with neuromuscular disease and [selective neuronal vulnerability](https://doi.org/10.1172/jci.insight.143948), with additional evidence connecting its perturbation to [neurodegenerative RNA dysregulation](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-020-18949-w). Recent work also provided precedent for convergence among ALS-associated RNA-binding proteins on a [REST–UNC13A mechanism](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44318-025-00506-0), so a shared acute response would have been reasonable before the atlas; the recurrent, oppositely signed MATR3 module was not.
Support: In 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, Matr3 perturbation increased Atp9b (+1.06061, FDR 4.14e-12), Ddhd1 (+1.59396, FDR 4.23e-14), and E2f4 (+1.29225, FDR 4.72e-8) and decreased Rsrp1 (-0.35285, FDR 1.96e-4). The same directions were stronger in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut: Atp9b +1.48175 (FDR 3.53e-38), Ddhd1 +1.76597 (FDR 2.64e-49), E2f4 +1.73480 (FDR 1.46e-35), and Rsrp1 -0.69262 (FDR 1.26e-29). In 155 MB Glut, Ddhd1 rose +1.93699 (FDR 2.84e-28) and Rsrp1 fell -0.51681 (FDR 1.09e-14); in 191 MB P MY GABA, Ddhd1 (+1.89191, FDR 1.43e-11), E2f4 (+2.23139, FDR 1.42e-11), and Atp9b (+0.94914, FDR 0.00455) remained significant, although Rsrp1 did not (FDR 0.377). By comparison, Fus was strongly reduced in 151 (-1.40691, FDR 1.04e-45) yet Atp9b and Ddhd1 had FDR 1.0 and Rsrp1 moved oppositely upward (+0.42834, FDR 4.84e-29); Tardbp was likewise reduced (-1.41422, FDR 1.60e-39) while Rsrp1 increased (+0.15504, FDR 3.24e-5). The chief limitation is that guide efficiency and acute perturbation biology can generate apparent divergence; gene-level snRNA-seq does not measure the splicing, RNA export, binding occupancy, or aggregation mechanisms expected of these proteins.
Research community: ALS/FTD and neuromuscular-disease researchers—especially those studying MATR3 and comparative RBP biology—would be most likely to change direction. Matched-efficiency MATR3, FUS, and TARDBP editing in the same neuron types, followed by long-read RNA sequencing, MATR3 eCLIP, and CRISPR-resistant MATR3 rescue, would determine whether the signed module is a direct MATR3 regulon or a product of unequal perturbation or generic stress. Direct target recovery would justify MATR3-specific vulnerability maps, molecular biomarkers, and RNA-targeted interventions instead of a universal ALS-RBP model.

Rank: 3
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: PPP2R1A perturbation reverses sterol-transcription polarity across neuronal classes
Core Idea: Ppp2r1a perturbation induced multiple cholesterol-biosynthesis genes in midbrain and hypothalamic glutamatergic neurons but reduced Srebf2 and Hmgcr in thalamic and cortical glutamatergic neurons. The observation therefore supports a testable interpretation in which the PP2A A-alpha scaffold gates qualitatively different sterol-homeostatic states across mature neuronal classes rather than producing one uniform cholesterol response.
Significance: A validated polarity switch would make PP2A holoenzyme context an important determinant of neuronal lipid regulation, connecting phosphatase composition to opposing SREBP-pathway outputs. This could launch mechanistic studies of which PP2A complexes, phosphorylation events, and neuronal states determine sterol synthesis, and could alter how PPP2R1A-associated neurodevelopmental phenotypes or PP2A-directed interventions are interpreted.
Prior expectation: A [curated cholesterol-biosynthesis pathway](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-191273) places SREBP2/HMGCR control and downstream enzymes such as MSMO1, DHCR24, and SC5D within a coordinated sterol program. Before the atlas, a perturbation affecting that pathway would more naturally have been expected to shift the module in a common direction; prior knowledge did not predict opposite PPP2R1A-associated RNA outputs among these neuronal classes.
Support: After Ppp2r1a perturbation, 155 MB Glut neurons increased Msmo1 (+1.01390, FDR 2.95e-9), Insig1 (+0.93877, FDR 4.72e-7), Dhcr24 (+0.81862, FDR 7.79e-7), and Sc5d (+0.75365, FDR 1.10e-6). The 110 CNU-HYa HY MM Glut class showed the same direction for Insig1 (+0.93884, FDR 1.69e-5), Sc5d (+0.81201, FDR 1.42e-4), Dhcr24 (+0.76304, FDR 2.67e-4), and Msmo1 (+0.82738, FDR 6.80e-4). Conversely, 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut reduced Srebf2 (-0.53092, FDR 9.47e-8) and Hmgcr (-0.32009, FDR 0.0291), while 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut reduced Hmgcr (-0.59273, FDR 3.17e-5) and Srebf2 (-0.27908, FDR 0.0414). Because different genes mark the two arms, this is a pathway-level polarity interpretation rather than a literal sign reversal of every transcript; PP2A holoenzyme composition, phosphoproteins, SREBP2 cleavage, and sterol abundance were not measured, and unequal editing or baseline state remains an alternative.
Research community: PP2A signaling researchers, neuronal lipid biologists, and investigators of PPP2R1A-related developmental disorders are the natural audience. Their critical experiment is matched Ppp2r1a editing in purified representatives of the opposing neuronal classes, with phosphoproteomics, nuclear SREBP2 quantification, isotope-resolved sterol synthesis, and wild-type rescue. Opposite biochemical flux at comparable engagement would establish a phosphatase-dependent switch and motivate systematic mapping of PP2A holoenzymes to lipid-control nodes and neuronal physiology.

Rank: 4
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: AP2M1 and AP2S1 perturbations converge on TFRC induction and sterol repression while AP2S1 adds cortical activity-gene loss
Core Idea: Perturbing either of two physical AP-2 subunits induced Tfrc and reduced sterol-regulatory transcripts in matched thalamic and midbrain neuronal classes; Ap2s1 perturbation additionally repressed an Arc/Egr3/Bdnf/Vgf branch in cortical neurons. Together, these observations suggest that neuronal AP-2 dysfunction may couple impaired cargo endocytosis to an iron-uptake compensation signal and lipid regulation, with a further AP2S1-associated activity-output branch.
Significance: The convergence links a core endocytic complex to coordinated neuronal iron-receptor, sterol, and excitability-associated RNA outputs rather than treating AP-2 disease solely as defective vesicle trafficking. If validated, it would create a tractable iron–lipid–activity framework for AP2M1 developmental and epileptic encephalopathy and related AP-2 disorders, with distinct opportunities for cargo-level and neuronal-class interventions.
Prior expectation: AP2M1 and AP2S1 were already established as physical subunits of the [AP-2 clathrin adaptor](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P84091), and AP-2 depletion was known to [impair transferrin-receptor uptake](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12952941/). AP2M1 dysfunction is also linked to [developmental and epileptic encephalopathy](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31104773/). Those findings made defective endocytosis expected, but did not predict shared neuronal Tfrc induction with sterol repression or the AP2S1-associated cortical activity-gene branch.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Ap2m1 perturbation induced Tfrc (+0.83140, FDR 3.13e-6), and Ap2s1 perturbation induced it even more strongly (+0.98358, FDR 3.06e-24); both target transcripts were reduced in this class (Ap2m1 -0.57819, FDR 2.41e-8; Ap2s1 -0.53643, FDR 6.33e-5). In 155 MB Glut, Ap2m1 perturbation increased Tfrc (+0.96399, FDR 1.07e-5) while reducing Srebf2 (-0.78418, FDR 3.60e-5) and Dhcr24 (-0.83428, FDR 0.00323); Ap2s1 perturbation showed the same pattern for Tfrc (+0.85038, FDR 1.00e-7), Srebf2 (-0.49178, FDR 0.00229), and Dhcr24 (-0.55046, FDR 0.0146). The additional 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut Ap2s1 response included Egr3 -1.33292 (FDR 1.35e-13), Arc -0.89254 (FDR 2.56e-7), Bdnf -1.10465 (FDR 0.0274), and Vgf -0.63317 (FDR 2.52e-4). Convergence of two subunits argues against a single-guide artifact, but the assay did not measure coated pits, surface TFRC, iron uptake, cholesterol, or firing; generic membrane stress and unequal perturbation strength remain alternatives.
Research community: Endocytosis and membrane-trafficking researchers, neuronal iron and cholesterol biologists, and AP2M1 encephalopathy investigators have the strongest stake in this relationship. Comparing AP2M1- and AP2S1-edited neurons by live transferrin internalization, surface-TFRC imaging, labile-iron measurements, SREBP2 processing, lipidomics, and subunit-specific rescue would distinguish compensation for AP-2 cargo failure from a generic transcriptional stress state. The resulting cargo-to-state map could organize work on neuronal lipid balance, excitability, and genotype-specific therapeutic vulnerabilities.

Novelty Score: 4

Title: UBA5 and UFC1 perturbations converge on DDIT3 induction and ATP6V0C repression
Core Idea: Perturbation of either the UFMylation E1 enzyme Uba5 or E2 enzyme Ufc1 produced a shared Ddit3-high, Atp6v0c-low response in thalamic and midbrain glutamatergic neurons; Ufsp2 perturbation reproduced a narrower ATP6V0C/NDUFA2 branch. The proposed interpretation is a neuronal UFMylation-defect state with integrated-stress and V-ATPase-associated outputs, in which UFSP2 is only partly interchangeable with the E1/E2 arm.
Significance: A reproducible pathway-level RNA state would provide a mechanistic bridge from multiple UFMylation disorders to shared neuronal stress and organelle-acidification hypotheses. It could organize disease models around separable E1/E2 and de-UFMylation branches, supply pharmacodynamic markers, and motivate substantial work on how UFM1 conjugation controls neuronal proteostasis, lysosomal function, and circuit survival.
Prior expectation: UBA5 and UFC1 were established as the E1 and E2 enzymes of the UFM1 conjugation cascade in [curated protein records](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/search?query=accession:Q9GZZ9%20OR%20accession:Q8VE47%20OR%20accession:Q9Y3C8), and defects in either gene were linked to severe neurodevelopmental disease through [UFM1/UFC1 encephalopathy genetics](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29868776/) and [UBA5/UFC1 clinical genetics](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27545681/). Thus some E1/E2 convergence was expected before the atlas, but the specific combination of Ddit3 induction with Atp6v0c repression, and the narrower UFSP2 relationship, was not established.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Uba5 perturbation increased Ddit3 (+1.08824, FDR 9.78e-16) and decreased Atp6v0c (-0.48901, FDR 1.18e-19); Ufc1 perturbation produced the same directions for Ddit3 (+0.92264, FDR 1.14e-6) and Atp6v0c (-0.34908, FDR 2.19e-6). In 155 MB Glut, Uba5 again increased Ddit3 (+0.84461, FDR 0.00209) and reduced Atp6v0c (-0.71400, FDR 1.11e-18), while Ufc1 significantly reduced Atp6v0c (-0.42976, FDR 0.00658) but its Ddit3 increase did not pass FDR (+0.98507, FDR 0.118). Ufsp2 perturbation in 155 reduced Atp6v0c (-0.50407, FDR 0.00233) and increased Ndufa2 (+0.76887, FDR 0.0333), whereas Ddit3 was not FDR-supported (FDR 1.0). The strongest alternative is a generic integrated-stress response, especially because DDIT3 is not UFMylation-specific; UFM1 conjugates, V-ATPase abundance, organelle pH, and editing efficiency were not measured. Because 151 and 155 have the atlas's highest discovery yields, concentration there does not establish intrinsic neuronal-class restriction.
Research community: UFMylation, ER-proteostasis, lysosome, and UBA5/UFC1 neurodevelopmental-syndrome researchers have the greatest stake. Matched Uba5 and Ufc1 edits should be read out at the UFM1-conjugate proteome and organelle-pH levels, with catalytically active versus inactive rescue testing whether the DDIT3/ATP6V0C state persists only when UFMylation remains defective. Identifying the responsible UFMylated substrates would connect pathway chemistry to neuronal vulnerability, pharmacodynamic markers, and restoration strategies.

Novelty Score: 4

Title: CHP1 perturbation induces the sterol module that direct SREBF2 perturbation represses
Core Idea: Chp1 perturbation repeatedly increased canonical sterol-biosynthesis transcripts in thalamic and midbrain glutamatergic neurons, whereas direct Srebf2 perturbation decreased the same module in thalamic neurons. The sign-opposed observation is consistent with acute CHP1 disruption being sensed as lipid or ER homeostatic stress that activates an SREBP2-dependent cholesterol-demand response.
Significance: The opposite perturbation polarities turn a recurrent cholesterol signature into a mechanistically sharp hypothesis: CHP1 may sit upstream of a compensatory SREBP2 switch in mature neurons. Validation would connect CHP1's established membrane and glycerolipid functions to neuronal cholesterol control, opening studies of lipid balance, ion-exchanger maturation, and neurological phenotypes rather than treating the sterol response as an incidental stress marker.
Prior expectation: SREBP2 was already the canonical regulator of cholesterol-biosynthesis genes and sterol feedback in a [curated cholesterol-regulation pathway](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-1655829). CHP1 had prior links to [GPAT4-dependent ER glycerolipid synthesis](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.molcel.2019.01.037), and [endosome positioning](https://doi.org/10.1083/jcb.200811005) had precedent for influencing cholesterol sensing. These facts made a lipid-homeostasis effect plausible, but did not establish CHP1 as an upstream activator of the neuronal SREBP2 transcriptional response.
Support: Chp1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut increased Insig1 (+1.37, FDR 1.78e-31), Msmo1 (+1.30, FDR 2.56e-25), Fdft1 (+1.27, FDR 2.56e-16), Hmgcs1 (+0.759, FDR 2.45e-12), and Dhcr24 (+0.853, FDR 4.77e-13). In 155 MB Glut it again increased Insig1 (+0.912, FDR 2.46e-7) and Dhcr24 (+0.654, FDR 1.86e-4). Direct Srebf2 perturbation in 151 moved the same pathway oppositely, reducing Msmo1 (-1.14, FDR 4.0e-12) and Dhcr24 (-0.95, FDR 1.0e-11). This supports compensatory SREBP2 output but not a direct CHP1–SREBP2 molecular interaction; cholesterol, ER sterol transport, SREBP2 cleavage, GPAT4 activity, and CHP1 protein engagement were not measured, so generalized membrane or ER stress is the main alternative. The signal's concentration in high-yield 151/155 cannot establish restriction to those classes.
Research community: Neuronal lipid-homeostasis, SREBP, CHP1/NHE1, and glycerolipid researchers would be most likely to revise their models. Chp1 editing combined with ER-cholesterol sensing, nuclear SREBP2, isotope-resolved sterol synthesis, wild-type CHP1 rescue, and independent SREBF2 blockade can ask whether the transcriptional and flux responses require SREBP2 rather than generic stress. That dependency would connect CHP1-controlled membrane biogenesis and ion homeostasis to neuronal sterol demand and disease vulnerability.

Rank: 7
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: PGK1 and GAPDH perturbations converge on OGT and nuclear-speckle transcript loss
Core Idea: Independent Pgk1 and Gapdh perturbations reduced Ogt together with Pnisr/Rsrp1-associated RNA-processing transcripts in thalamic glutamatergic neurons, and Tpi1 perturbation also reduced Ogt in hypothalamic glutamatergic neurons. These observations motivate, but do not establish, a mechanism linking adjacent lower-glycolysis steps to O-GlcNAc regulation and nuclear-speckle or RNA-processing maintenance; the response is not demonstrated for every glycolytic enzyme.
Significance: This convergence offers a specific route by which glycolytic insufficiency could remodel neuronal RNA processing rather than merely lowering cellular energy. If flux, O-GlcNAc signaling, and splice regulation prove causally linked, the finding would support a substantial metabolism-to-RNA-biology program relevant to neuronal stress and metabolic neurological disease.
Prior expectation: GAPDH and PGK1 were known as adjacent enzymes in [cytosolic glycolysis](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-70171), and TPI1 as the enzyme interconverting DHAP and G3P in the [glycolytic pathway](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-70454). PNISR, RSRP1, SRRM2, and related readouts had [curated nuclear-speckle or spliceosome annotations](https://www.uniprot.org/). Before the atlas, these facts did not predict that distinct lower-glycolysis perturbations would converge on Ogt and a compact RNA-processing transcript set in neurons.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Pgk1 perturbation reduced its own transcript (-1.44092, FDR 4.43e-32), Pnisr (-0.22694, FDR 7.00e-11), and Ogt (-0.14565, FDR 4.998e-4). Gapdh perturbation in the same class independently reduced Pnisr (-0.25522, FDR 2.19e-5), Rsrp1 (-0.19735, FDR 1.49e-4), and Ogt (-0.17676, FDR 0.0220). Tpi1 perturbation reduced Ogt in 145 MH-LH TH Glut (-0.38744, FDR 0.0121), although its secondary 151 RNA-processing changes were only nominal. GAPDH itself was absent from the measured features, metabolic flux and UDP-GlcNAc/O-GlcNAc levels were not assayed, and the modest effect sizes leave shared energy stress or altered cell state as important alternatives to a specific glycolysis–OGT mechanism.
Research community: Neuro-metabolism, O-GlcNAc, and RNA-splicing communities—particularly investigators of metabolic control over nuclear organization—would pursue this connection. Isotope tracing, ATP and UDP-GlcNAc measurements, quantitative O-GlcNAc proteomics, live speckle imaging, and long-read RNA sequencing after matched PGK1 and GAPDH perturbations would locate the defect; restoration by a defined metabolite or OGT rescue would separate causal signaling from nonspecific stress. The larger opportunity is a map of metabolic nodes that govern neuronal RNA processing and create disease-selective liabilities.

Rank: 8
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: STXBP1 perturbation produces distinct cortical, midbrain, and Pvalb transcriptional outputs
Core Idea: Stxbp1 perturbation elicited a cortical Nrsn1/Pcsk1n-high and Grin2b-low response, a midbrain Cntnap1-low and Mt1/Fos-high response, and Slc6a1 repression in Pvalb GABA neurons. The biological interpretation is that chronic Munc18-1 insufficiency may be translated into different adaptive or stress states across neuronal classes rather than one transcriptomic consequence of presynaptic failure.
Significance: STXBP1 disorders are often framed around a common vesicle-release defect; these distinct positive RNA outputs nominate neuronal-class-specific routes from that shared lesion to circuit pathology. Validation could redirect disease studies toward differential cortical, midbrain, and inhibitory-neuron compensation and create class-specific biomarkers or therapeutic endpoints for STXBP1 encephalopathy.
Prior expectation: STXBP1/Munc18-1 was established as a [syntaxin-binding Sec1/Munc18 protein](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P61764) required for [neurotransmitter release](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.287.5454.864), and [SNARE-complex biology](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19164740/) predicted severe presynaptic dysfunction after its loss. That prior biology predicted a shared release defect, but not the distinct cortical neurosecretory, midbrain stress/adhesion, and Pvalb transporter RNA outputs observed here.
Support: In 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, Stxbp1 perturbation reduced Stxbp1 (-0.520, FDR 9.66e-11) and Grin2b (-0.231, FDR 0.00582) while increasing Nrsn1 (+0.724, FDR 3.98e-4) and Pcsk1n (+0.448, FDR 5.87e-4). In 155 MB Glut, Stxbp1 was reduced (-0.608, FDR 2.76e-9), Cntnap1 decreased (-0.526, FDR 3.71e-5), and Mt1 (+1.107, FDR 9.47e-4) and Fos (+1.390, FDR 0.0464) increased. In 052 Pvalb Gaba, Stxbp1 perturbation lowered Slc6a1 (-0.412, FDR 1.27e-8); direct Slc6a1 perturbation strongly reduced its own transcript (-1.018, FDR 1.39e-29) without establishing that the broader STXBP1 mechanism runs through SLC6A1. The atlas measured steady-state nuclear RNA rather than release, firing, survival, or network activity, so different activity histories, effective power, and perturbation strength could explain part of the apparent class divergence.
Research community: STXBP1 encephalopathy researchers, synaptic-release biologists, and circuit physiologists have the clearest incentive to follow this finding. In genetically identified cortical, midbrain, and Pvalb neurons, optical release measurements, patch clamp, time-resolved single-cell RNA, and STXBP1 restoration should determine whether the RNA branches persist at matched degrees of release failure. A true class-dependent adaptation would provide a framework linking distinct molecular responses to seizure, movement, and cognitive phenotypes and to tailored rescue strategies.

Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: PCYT2 and SELENOI perturbations converge on sterol induction while PCYT2 lowers peptide-processing genes
Core Idea: Within 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut neurons, Pcyt2 and Selenoi perturbations both induced an SREBP2-linked sterol module, whereas Pcyt2 perturbation additionally reduced Cpe and Pcsk2. The convergence suggests a shared membrane-lipid compensation state after disruption of two CDP-ethanolamine phosphatidylethanolamine-pathway enzymes, with a possible additional PCYT2 connection to secretory peptide processing.
Significance: The relationship connects phosphatidylethanolamine synthesis to cholesterol feedback and, for PCYT2, to the machinery that matures neuropeptides. Validation would open a mechanistic program linking membrane phospholipid composition, sterol sensing, and neuronal secretion, with direct relevance to PCYT2-associated complex hereditary spastic paraplegia.
Prior expectation: PCYT2 and SELENOI/EPT1 were established as endoplasmic-reticulum enzymes in [phosphatidylethanolamine synthesis](https://reactome.org/PathwayBrowser/#/R-HSA-1483213), and biallelic PCYT2 variants were known to disrupt etherlipids and cause [complex hereditary spastic paraplegia](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31637422/). CPE and PCSK2 were recognized as secretory peptide-processing proteins in [curated functional annotations](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/QuickGO/). These facts made membrane-lipid pathology plausible but did not predict convergence on an SREBP2-like sterol response or the selective PCYT2 peptide-processing branch.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Pcyt2 perturbation increased Insig1 (+1.00363, FDR 1.36e-11), Cyp51 (+0.68284, FDR 1.92e-9), Ldlr (+1.40824, FDR 5.76e-6), and Srebf2 (+0.27788, FDR 0.0206), while reducing Cpe (-0.31512, FDR 1.29e-5) and Pcsk2 (-0.31216, FDR 0.00364). Selenoi perturbation in the same class reduced its own transcript (-0.65198, FDR 0.00276) and independently increased Insig1 (+0.75848, FDR 3.76e-4). The convergence of two pathway enzymes supports a phosphatidylethanolamine-linked response, but Pcyt2 transcript itself did not pass FDR, and the experiment did not measure editing, phosphatidylethanolamine or sterol species, SREBP2 cleavage, neuropeptide abundance, or secretion; generic ER-membrane stress remains the strongest alternative. Because this relationship was resolved in the atlas's highest-yield context, it does not by itself demonstrate thalamic restriction.
Research community: Phospholipid and SREBP researchers, neuropeptide biologists, and hereditary-spastic-paraplegia investigators are positioned to resolve the two proposed branches. PCYT2- and SELENOI-edited neurons should be compared by isotope-resolved phosphatidylethanolamine synthesis, lipidomics, nuclear SREBP2, secreted-peptide mass spectrometry, and enzyme-specific rescue. Sterol activation proportional to phosphatidylethanolamine disruption, coupled to a PCYT2-selective peptide defect, would support a membrane-lipid-to-secretory-physiology program and testable lipid or SREBP correction strategies for PCYT2 disease.

Rank: 10
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: Acute TBR1 perturbation recurrently raises CPNE6 while splitting mature cortical projection-neuron outputs
Core Idea: Acute Tbr1 perturbation increased Cpne6 in several cortical projection-neuron classes, coupled to Dkk3/Epb41 decreases in L5/6 intratelencephalic neurons but to Cacna1g/C1qtnf4 increases in NP-CT-L6b-OB neurons. A plausible interpretation is that TBR1 retains a mature synaptic and membrane-associated regulon whose output depends on projection-neuron identity and is separable from its canonical neonatal fate-specification role.
Significance: A direct adult TBR1 regulon would extend TBR1 biology beyond developmental layer specification and provide a route from a neurodevelopmental transcription factor to ongoing projection-neuron physiology. This could launch focused studies of adult TBR1 chromatin occupancy, synaptic maintenance, and whether disease alleles perturb mature functions independently of early cortical wiring.
Prior expectation: Established work positioned TBR1 as a regulator of [cortical projection-neuron and layer-6 identity](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11239428/), showed continuing dosage-sensitive roles in [postnatal cortical neurons](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30318412/), and linked [TBR1 mutations to autism](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25232744/). Before viewing the atlas, one might therefore have expected acute postnatal perturbation either to reuse familiar developmental targets or to have little mature-neuron output; a recurrent Cpne6-centered, class-split response was not the standard prediction.
Support: In 001 L5-6 IT Glut, Tbr1 perturbation decreased Dkk3 (-1.17698, FDR 0.00229) and Epb41 (-1.54013, FDR 0.00724) while increasing Cpne6 (+0.91271, FDR 0.00748). In 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, Cpne6 again increased (+0.60600, FDR 0.0237) and Dkk3 decreased (-0.71992, FDR 0.0360). By contrast, the 027 NP-CT-L6b-OB Glut response was all upward among the reported rows, including Cacna1g +1.43196 (FDR 1.12e-5), Cpne6 +1.24500 (FDR 5.98e-5), and C1qtnf4 +0.47990 (FDR 0.0249). These are small expression programs from unequally sampled classes, and the assay did not measure TBR1 occupancy or direct transcription; residual lineage state, editing differences, or an indirect activity response could explain the split, while absence of neonatal targets is only a developmental comparison rather than a matched negative perturbation.
Research community: Cortical-development, projection-neuron, TBR1-syndrome, and activity-dependent-transcription communities would be most interested. Adult-neuron TBR1 CUT&RUN or CUT&Tag paired with nascent RNA after acute editing in the two responsive projection classes—and then CRISPR-resistant rescue—can distinguish direct occupancy at Cpne6, Dkk3/Epb41, or Cacna1g/C1qtnf4 from secondary state change. That result would let the field separate developmental from maintenance functions of TBR1 and test how patient variants alter adult cortical circuits.

Rank: 11
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: Zbtb11 perturbation recurrently lowers Rbm28, Stx16, Acsf3, and Ndufaf1 across subcortical neurons
Core Idea: A recurrent downregulated core followed Zbtb11 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, 155 MB Glut, and 191 MB P MY GABA neurons, spanning Acsf3 and Ndufaf1, the nucleolar factor Rbm28, the trafficking factor Stx16, and the GPI-biosynthesis gene Pigq. This observation raises the biological interpretation that functional ZBTB11 disruption couples nuclear control of mitochondrial capacity to nucleolar, glycan, and endomembrane maintenance rather than affecting mitochondrial transcripts alone.
Significance: If this coupling is real, it would give ZBTB11-associated intellectual disability a tractable multi-compartment neuronal mechanism and justify a substantial program linking mitochondrial output, ribosome biogenesis, membrane trafficking, and glycan biology. The atlas adds a recurrent neuronal RNA state and specific readouts for testing how ACSF3-related biochemistry fits into the broader syndrome.
Prior expectation: ZBTB11 was already expected to support nuclear mitochondrial gene expression through NRF-2/GABP, and biallelic variants were linked to [intellectual disability and altered ACSF3 biochemistry](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35104841/). Its [broad tissue expression](https://www.proteinatlas.org/ENSG00000066422-ZBTB11) made a narrowly mitochondrial effect plausible but did not predict the recurrent Rbm28-Stx16-Pigq branch.
Support: Zbtb11 perturbation yielded 48 FDR-significant genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, 17 in 191 MB P MY GABA, and 16 in 155 MB Glut. In 151 TH, Rbm28 decreased by log2FC -1.460 (FDR 3.57e-16), Stx16 by -1.320 (FDR 2.29e-14), Pigq by -1.260 (FDR 2.03e-13), and Acsf3 by -1.146 (FDR 7.68e-09); Stx16 also decreased in 155 MB Glut by -1.731 (FDR 1.50e-13), while Acsf3 and Ndufaf1 decreased in 191 MB P MY GABA by -1.897 (FDR 1.33e-06) and -2.459 (FDR 8.18e-04), respectively. The response burden covaries with perturbed-cell count, the relevant GABP partners were not perturbed, and RNA abundance alone cannot distinguish direct ZBTB11 targets from secondary mitochondrial or nucleolar stress.
Research community: Neurodevelopmental-disease, mitochondrial-gene-regulation, and nucleolar-stress researchers have the strongest reason to revise their models. Acute ZBTB11 protein depletion and rescue in matched neuronal cultures, measured alongside ZBTB11/GABP occupancy, nascent transcription, pre-rRNA synthesis, and respiration, would reveal whether the multi-compartment state begins with direct coordinated control or secondary stress. Early occupancy-linked effects would anchor a ZBTB11 disease program connecting genotype, ACSF3 metabolism, ribosome production, membrane trafficking, and circuit physiology.

Rank: 12
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: Pura perturbation raises Nefl while lowering Ogt and neighboring Igip in thalamic and midbrain glutamatergic neurons
Core Idea: Pura perturbation produced the same signed Igip-down, Ogt-down, and Nefl-up relationship in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut and 155 MB Glut neurons, with additional structural, ion-channel, and synaptic transcripts changing in each context. The trans-acting interpretation is that acute PURA disruption remodels neurofilament-associated RNA homeostasis; the competing interpretation is a local cis effect on nearby Igip.
Significance: A validated PURA-to-neurofilament and O-GlcNAc relationship would shift PURA syndrome work from broad RNA-binding dysfunction toward a measurable axonal-structural mechanism. It would support a substantial program asking whether altered neurofilament stoichiometry and OGT-dependent modification jointly drive neuronal dysfunction and whether they provide pharmacodynamic markers.
Prior expectation: Genetic inactivation of Pura was already known to [impair postnatal brain development](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12972605/), and PURA is an RNA-binding protein whose dysfunction causes [neurological disease](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33777106/). That biology predicted broad postnatal neuronal consequences, but not a reproducible Nefl-up/Ogt-down state or its separation from a possible local Pura-Igip locus effect.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Pura decreased by log2FC -0.466 (FDR 3.90e-05), Igip by -0.579 (FDR 7.20e-08), and Ogt by -0.132 (FDR 0.0144), while Nefl increased by +0.510 (FDR 3.90e-05); Cntnap1, Kcna6, and Lingo1 also increased by +0.342, +0.368, and +0.229 (FDR 1.36e-04, 3.05e-04, and 8.17e-04). In 155 MB Glut, Pura fell by -0.552 (FDR 7.54e-06), Igip by -0.611 (FDR 7.54e-06), and Ogt by -0.234 (FDR 0.0104), while Nefl rose by +0.568 (FDR 1.18e-05). The main limitation is that genomic editing cannot distinguish a trans PURA response from disruption of local regulation at Igip, and snRNA-seq does not establish neurofilament protein abundance, O-GlcNAcylation, or axonal structure. Recurrence in 151/155 is strong positive evidence but, given their superior effective power, does not establish restriction to those classes.
Research community: PURA-syndrome, neuronal RNA-biology, axonal-cytoskeleton, and O-GlcNAc researchers would be most affected. Locus-preserving acute PURA protein degradation, PURA rescue, and parallel measurement of Igip, Ogt, and neurofilament RNA and protein provide a clean discriminator: Nefl/Ogt changes after protein-only depletion support a trans mechanism, whereas Igip loss confined to genomic editing favors a cis effect. Resolving that split would support work on PURA-bound RNAs, neurofilament assembly, axonal transport, and genotype-specific therapeutic biomarkers.

Rank: 13
Novelty Score: 4
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 4
Title: Wdr26 perturbation recurrently lowers Srrm2 alongside neuronal excitability transcripts in cerebellar and subcortical neurons
Core Idea: Large responses to Wdr26 perturbation appeared in 308 CB GABA, 155 MB Glut, and 191 MB P MY GABA neurons, repeatedly lowering RNA-processing and neuronal-function transcripts while adding context-dependent outputs such as Col11a2 induction. The proposed mechanism is a CTLH-associated neuronal liability state rather than a simple phenocopy of loss of the assayed G-protein beta subunit Gnb1.
Significance: This relationship gives Skraban-Deardorff syndrome a concrete neuronal state that can be decomposed into CTLH-complex, RNA-processing, and excitability components. Establishing which WDR26 molecular interface produces the state could launch multiple disease-mechanism studies and identify molecular and electrophysiological readouts for variant classification.
Prior expectation: WDR26 haploinsufficiency was known to cause a [syndromic neurodevelopmental disorder](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28686853/), disease variants can impair [CTLH E3-complex assembly](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38575527/), and WDR26 can scaffold [G-protein signaling components](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22065575/). Those observations predicted neuronal vulnerability but did not specify whether CTLH disruption or G-protein scaffolding would dominate the neuronal response.
Support: Wdr26 perturbation produced 1,459 FDR-significant genes in 308 CB GABA, 491 in 155 MB Glut, 329 in 191 MB P MY GABA, and 43 in 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA. Srrm2 decreased in 308 CB GABA by log2FC -0.799 (FDR 1.95e-11) and in 155 MB Glut by -0.693 (FDR 7.09e-19); Cacna1g fell in 308 CB GABA by -1.743 (FDR 6.51e-10), Nat8l fell in 155 MB Glut by -0.903 (FDR 1.99e-15), and Col11a2 rose in 191 MB P MY GABA by +2.539 (FDR 1.67e-14). Eif4g3 also fell in 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA by -0.623 (FDR 0.00314). Gnb1 perturbation was nearly orthogonal to this state, but CTLH-core genes were not assayed as perturbations, functional protein engagement was not measured, and unequal cell counts or a general stress response remain alternatives.
Research community: Skraban-Deardorff syndrome, CTLH ubiquitin-ligase, neuronal RNA-processing, and ion-channel communities would be most motivated. A separation-of-function rescue with wild-type WDR26, a CTLH-assembly-defective variant, and a G beta-gamma-binding-defective variant, read out by both the RNA signature and excitability, can identify the responsible molecular interface. That classification would let disease researchers map patient variants onto CTLH versus G-protein dysfunction and relate each class to cerebellar and subcortical circuit phenotypes.

Title: Arid1a, Smarcb1, Smarcc2, and Smarce1 perturbations converge on Ptpn3-Reep2-Prkcd repression with Clstn2-Nexn induction
Core Idea: Within 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut neurons, perturbing four BAF subunits repeatedly lowered Ptpn3, Reep2, Prkcd, Begain, and Lrrc7 while raising Clstn2 or Nexn; Arid1b and the SMARCA ATPase perturbations did not reproduce the corrected multi-gene pattern. One interpretation is dependence on a particular mature-neuronal cBAF/core configuration, although unequal functional disruption could also create the apparent subunit boundary.
Significance: If the convergence reflects mature cBAF architecture, it would extend developmental BAF-switch models into maintenance of an adult neuronal identity and excitability program. That would create substantial follow-up directions for Coffin-Siris and related chromatin-remodeling disorders, including which subunits are mechanistically interchangeable after neuronal maturation and which outputs are reversible.
Prior expectation: Canonical [cBAF-complex assembly](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-9933937) places ARID1A, SMARCB1, SMARCC2, and SMARCE1 in related complexes, with ARID1A and ARID1B as alternative subunits. Neural BAF complexes were already known to undergo [developmental subunit switching](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17640523/) and to control [dendritic programs in postmitotic neurons](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17920018/). Some convergence was therefore expected, but the exact signed output and incomplete paralog phenocopy were not.
Support: Under Arid1a perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Begain fell by log2FC -0.455 (FDR 4.82e-06), Kcnq2 by -0.219 (FDR 1.51e-04), Ptpn3 by -0.187 (FDR 0.00302), and Lrrc7 by -0.128 (FDR 0.00463), while Clstn2 rose by +0.385 (FDR 4.82e-06). Smarcb1 perturbation lowered Ptpn3 by -0.952 (FDR 1.30e-31), Reep2 by -0.610 (FDR 5.54e-26), and Prkcd by -0.537 (FDR 2.45e-12), while increasing Nexn by +0.652 (FDR 4.94e-09). Smarcc2 produced the same signs for Ptpn3 (-0.982, FDR 6.88e-24), Reep2 (-0.681, FDR 2.02e-20), Prkcd (-0.575, FDR 1.55e-10), and Nexn (+0.644, FDR 2.71e-06); Smarce1 likewise lowered Ptpn3 (-0.340, FDR 1.23e-07), Reep2 (-0.247, FDR 1.23e-07), and Prkcd (-0.357, FDR 6.58e-07). Arid1b and Smarca2 transcripts were significantly reduced yet the same marker panel was not FDR-supported, but protein loss and BAF occupancy were unmeasured, so this boundary cannot by itself prove true subunit dispensability. This matched within-context convergence is informative, but observing it only in high-yield 151 does not establish cell-type specificity.
Research community: Neuronal chromatin-remodeling, BAF-complex, and Coffin-Siris syndrome researchers would be most likely to change their working models. Matched acute degradation of each endogenous subunit in one mature thalamic model, followed by rescue, complex proteomics, chromatin accessibility, and occupancy at the Ptpn3/Reep2/Prkcd and Clstn2/Nexn loci, would separate complex-architecture dependence from unequal editing. A defined cBAF output module would underpin studies of mature-neuron complex composition, circuit excitability, disease-variant stratification, and postnatal reversibility.

Title: Nipbl, Smc1a, and Stag2 perturbations share a Ptprm-Necab2-Smtn-Adamts2 repression core but diverge at Gabra1 and Brinp1
Core Idea: Nipbl, Smc1a, and Stag2 perturbations converged in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut neurons on strong downregulation of Ptprm, Necab2, Smtn, and Adamts2, while Stag2 increased Gabra1 and Brinp1 and Nipbl decreased them. The observed common core and signed divergence suggest that mature neurons read cohesin-loader and ring disruption through a shared projection or adhesion program plus subunit-dependent synaptic branches, not one interchangeable pan-cohesin response.
Significance: A validated common core with divergent branches would refine cohesinopathy models by separating shared mature-neuronal consequences from gene-specific disease mechanisms. This could launch programs linking cohesin topology to synaptic identity, explaining why NIPBL-, SMC1A-, and STAG2-associated syndromes overlap clinically yet differ in severity and neurological presentation.
Prior expectation: Cohesin was already known to [continuously support neuronal gene expression](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8131595/), while STAG1 and STAG2 had [distinct and overlapping gene-regulatory roles](https://doi.org/10.1186/s13072-020-00353-9). HDAC8 was also established in the cohesin acetylation cycle and [Cornelia de Lange syndrome](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature11316). Prior biology therefore predicted some convergence, but not this exact repression core or the opposite Gabra1/Brinp1 branches.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Smc1a perturbation lowered Ptprm by log2FC -0.783 (FDR 6.39e-07), Necab2 by -0.675 (FDR 3.53e-05), Smtn by -0.857 (FDR 9.52e-04), and Adamts2 by -0.876 (FDR 5.83e-04). Stag2 perturbation lowered the same genes by -0.884, -1.056, -1.577, and -0.936 (FDR 1.73e-18, 7.92e-18, 1.14e-16, and 5.43e-07) but increased Gabra1 by +0.329 (FDR 0.00453) and Brinp1 by +0.243 (FDR 0.0362). Nipbl perturbation lowered Ptprm, Necab2, Smtn, and Adamts2 by -0.985, -0.577, -0.974, and -0.749 (FDR 2.05e-07, 0.00403, 2.61e-04, and 0.0139) and lowered Gabra1 and Brinp1 by -0.523 and -0.561 (FDR 7.37e-04 and 2.79e-04). Stag2 and Nipbl self-transcript changes were not FDR-significant, Smc3 showed only nominal movement, and protein depletion and chromatin-loop changes were not measured; the Stag1, Pds5a, and Hdac8 comparisons had no FDR-supported core response and therefore define provisional boundaries rather than proving non-function. The single high-yield context supports the relational comparison but not intrinsic thalamic specificity.
Research community: Cohesinopathy, chromosome-architecture, and neuronal-adhesion researchers would be most engaged. Rapid matched depletion of NIPBL, SMC1A, and STAG2 in postmitotic thalamic neurons, coupled to Micro-C, nascent RNA, and loader- or ring-separation-mutant rescue, can test whether the common genes lose specific contacts and whether Gabra1/Brinp1 divergence reflects distinct chromatin mechanisms. This would create a multi-gene cohesinopathy framework connecting three-dimensional genome organization to synaptic physiology and patient-specific therapeutic windows.

Title: Setdb1 perturbation induces Atf7ip, Tasor, and clustered gamma-protocadherins without an equivalent Ehmt1 response
Core Idea: Setdb1 perturbation increased the HUSH-associated factors Atf7ip and Tasor together with multiple Pcdhga and Pcdhgb genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut and 155 MB Glut neurons, with a narrower protocadherin response in 052 Pvalb Gaba. The interpretation to test is that SETDB1 maintains a HUSH-linked clustered-protocadherin repression state in postnatal neurons that is distinct from the measured Ehmt1 response.
Significance: If SETDB1 directly maintains this module, it would connect heterochromatin machinery to ongoing neuronal identity and cell-adhesion control after development. That would support a substantial program on how HUSH and H3K9 methylation preserve protocadherin choice, wiring, and circuit stability, with implications for SETDB1- and HUSH-related neurodevelopmental phenotypes.
Prior expectation: SETDB1 was already implicated in repression of a [neuron-specific clustered-protocadherin domain](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28671686/), and HUSH components were known to preserve [brain architecture and protocadherin fidelity](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36332029/). The prior therefore predicted protocadherin derepression, but not the acute Atf7ip/Tasor increase, its recurrence in these neuronal contexts, or its apparent separation from Ehmt1 perturbation.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Setdb1 perturbation increased Atf7ip by log2FC +0.706 (FDR 3.37e-04), Tasor by +0.730 (FDR 1.01e-04), Pcdhga8 by +0.999 (FDR 5.90e-04), and Pcdhga11 by +0.928 (FDR 0.00724). In 155 MB Glut, Atf7ip and Tasor rose by +0.916 and +0.790 (both FDR 0.00894), while Pcdhga11 and Pcdhgb5 rose by +1.169 and +1.131 (both FDR 0.01925). In 052 Pvalb Gaba, Pcdhga11 increased by +1.381 (FDR 5.25e-04) and Pcdhgb4 by +1.204 (FDR 0.0109). Ehmt1 perturbation in 151 TH increased Abhd12b (+1.484, FDR 5.41e-14) and Smtn (+0.552, FDR 0.0181) but not Atf7ip, Tasor, or the listed protocadherins; however, neither Setdb1 nor Ehmt1 self-transcript was FDR-reduced there, no HUSH core node was directly perturbed, and protein loss, H3K9me3, and locus occupancy were not measured.
Research community: Neuronal epigenetics, HUSH-complex, protocadherin, and circuit-assembly communities would be most motivated. Time-resolved SETDB1/TASOR occupancy, H3K9me3 profiling, and long-read protocadherin-choice measurements after acute SETDB1 protein degradation—with catalytic-dead and HUSH-binding-defective rescue—would distinguish locus-directed HUSH control from generic heterochromatin stress. Demonstrating direct control would support substantial work linking postnatal chromatin maintenance to neuronal self-recognition, wiring, and disease-associated circuit instability.

Title: Ranbp2 perturbation produces Srrm2-down/Rbm15-up cortical and Grm1-Epha4-Itpr1-up cerebellar responses
Core Idea: Ranbp2 perturbation produced a mixed RNA and nuclear-protein abundance response in 022 L5 ET CTX Glut neurons but a distinct Grm1-Epha4-Itpr1 calcium and synaptic induction in 308 CB GABA neurons. The cell-state interpretation is that the same RANBP2 nuclear-pore/SUMO lesion may be translated into different downstream liabilities by cortical projection neurons and cerebellar inhibitory neurons.
Significance: A validated bifurcation would change RANBP2 models from a uniform nuclear-transport defect to a neuron-state-dependent mechanism with separable RNA and calcium consequences. It would create substantial opportunities to connect nuclear-pore SUMOylation to projection-neuron maintenance, cerebellar signaling, and the selective neurological manifestations of RANBP2 dysfunction.
Prior expectation: RANBP2 was already known as a nuclear-envelope component of the [RanBP2–RanGAP1–SUMO1–Ubc9 complex](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-3108232) with [SUMO E3-ligase activity](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22464730/), so nuclear and RNA-related effects were plausible. A cerebellar calcium and synaptic induction was not an obvious generic consequence of that established function.
Support: In 022 L5 ET CTX Glut, Ranbp2 itself decreased by log2FC -0.536 (FDR 0.00653), Gpr162 decreased by -0.487 (FDR 0.00778), Rbm15 increased by +0.751 (FDR 0.0194), and Srrm2 decreased by -0.373 (FDR 0.0350). In 308 CB GABA, Grm1 increased by +0.829 (FDR 0.00872), Epha4 by +1.084 (FDR 0.00872), and Itpr1 by +1.412 (FDR 0.0261). The two positive responses occur outside the highest-yield contexts, but the atlas measures RNA rather than SUMOylation, transport, or calcium physiology; unequal protein disruption or baseline state could therefore create two apparent branches without one common causal RANBP2 mechanism.
Research community: Nuclear-transport, SUMOylation, cortical-projection-neuron, and cerebellar-physiology researchers would be most affected. Matched acute RANBP2 protein depletion in L5 ET-like and cerebellar GABA neurons, with quantitative SUMO proteomics and transport assays in both and calcium imaging plus electrophysiology in the latter, would test for equal proximal disruption followed by divergent distal phenotypes. Such state-dependent decoding would motivate cell-class-specific models of RANBP2 circuit dysfunction and selective rescue.

Title: Rb1 and E2f4 perturbations release distinct replication and chromosome-repair arms in thalamic neurons
Core Idea: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut neurons, Rb1 perturbation induced a Dnmt1-Tipin-Prim1-Lig1 DNA-replication arm, whereas E2f4 perturbation induced an Aaas-Rad18-Tacc3-Blm-Ddx11-Polq chromosome and repair arm. The observed separation supports a model in which postmitotic neurons retain distinct RB1-E2F and DREAM-E2F4 brakes, making ectopic cell-cycle transcription a set of replication-licensing and chromosome-maintenance routes rather than one undifferentiated re-entry program.
Significance: Resolving these arms could materially sharpen models of abortive neuronal cell-cycle re-entry in degeneration and injury. It would support substantial work on which branch causes DNA damage, apoptosis, or survival and whether selective suppression of one arm can protect mature neurons without broadly blocking cell-cycle machinery elsewhere.
Prior expectation: Acute Rb inactivation was already known to trigger [ectopic cell-cycle proteins and neuronal loss](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23077065/), while RB–E2F and DREAM–E2F4 complexes were known to repress [related but separable cell-cycle programs](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35835684/) and E2F4 was established as a [p130–DREAM repressor component](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17531812/). Thus derepression was expected, while the clean replication-versus-repair partition in this neuronal state was not.
Support: After Rb1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Dnmt1 increased by log2FC +1.514 (FDR 9.33e-10), Tipin by +1.518 (FDR 2.93e-06), Prim1 by +2.724 (FDR 5.87e-05), and Lig1 by +1.942 (FDR 1.63e-04); Dnmt1 also rose in 155 MB Glut by +1.158 (FDR 7.69e-04). After E2f4 perturbation in 151 TH, Aaas increased by +1.331 (FDR 3.65e-14), Rad18 by +1.635 (FDR 5.31e-06), and Polq by +2.120 (FDR 0.00763). E2f1 and E2f5 did not reproduce these active states, but their negative comparisons do not establish functional equivalence of editing. Rb1 self-RNA was not FDR-reduced, and the assay does not measure RB1 protein, DREAM occupancy, DNA synthesis, DNA damage, apoptosis, or actual cell-cycle progression. The within-151 contrast is valid, but its high effective power precludes an inference that these arms are thalamus-specific.
Research community: Neuronal cell-cycle, DNA-damage, and neurodegeneration communities would be most motivated. Matched acute RB1 and E2F4 protein depletion in mature neurons should combine nascent RNA and RB/E2F4/p130 occupancy with EdU, replication-fork markers, DNA-damage imaging, and survival. Distinguishing transcriptional derepression from abortive S phase—and assigning different physiological outcomes to the two arms—would support branch-selective intervention across neurodegenerative and acute neuronal-injury models.

Title: Tardbp and Fus perturbations create opposite Snrnp70 responses within separable recurrent RNA-processing states
Core Idea: Across 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, 155 MB Glut, and 191 MB P MY GABA neurons, Tardbp perturbation recurrently induced Ppp6c, Fam135a, Gabpa, and Usp15 while lowering Snrnp70; Fus perturbation instead induced Rsrp1 and Atxn2l and raised Snrnp70. These opposing, recurrent observations support distinct acute TDP-43 and FUS RNA-processing abundance states rather than one generic ALS-gene stress response.
Significance: The signed divergence offers a way to separate TDP-43 and FUS mechanisms in matched neurons and could yield state-specific biomarkers or modifiers for two major RNA-binding-protein disease axes. Because the signal recurs in several neuronal contexts, confirmation could sustain substantial programs on how shared RNA-processing machinery generates distinct downstream toxicity.
Prior expectation: TDP-43 was already established as a [repressor of cryptic exons](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26250685/) with [cell-type-dependent RNA targets](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28153034/), and FUS was known to bind brain RNAs and [regulate alternative splicing](https://doi.org/10.1038/srep00603). These priors predicted RNA-processing defects but not the recurrent opposite Snrnp70 direction or the exact Tardbp-versus-Fus abundance programs.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Tardbp decreased by log2FC -1.414 (FDR 1.60e-39), while Ppp6c, Fam135a, and Gabpa increased by +1.985, +2.132, and +2.248 (FDR 2.45e-41, 4.16e-35, and 1.74e-38) and Snrnp70 decreased by -0.587 (FDR 5.10e-20). The signs recurred in 155 MB Glut for Ppp6c (+2.264, FDR 5.11e-30) and Snrnp70 (-0.667, FDR 2.80e-14), and in 191 MB P MY GABA for Ppp6c (+2.463, FDR 3.90e-17) and Snrnp70 (-0.589, FDR 1.53e-06). Fus perturbation in 151 TH lowered Fus by -1.407 (FDR 1.04e-45) but increased Rsrp1 by +0.428 (FDR 4.84e-29), Atxn2l by +0.411 (FDR 3.84e-14), and Snrnp70 by +0.261 (FDR 5.74e-05); Rsrp1 and Snrnp70 were also both positive in 155 MB and 191 MB. The 3-prime abundance assay cannot observe cryptic-exon inclusion, Stmn2 was not FDR-significant in the 151 Tardbp comparison, Unc13a abundance increased rather than recapitulating human cryptic-exon-associated depletion, and weak negative ALS comparators do not exclude their disease mechanisms.
Research community: ALS/FTD, RNA-splicing, and neuronal RNP communities would be most engaged. Isoform-resolved long-read or junction-level single-nucleus sequencing after matched acute TDP-43 and FUS depletion, combined with eCLIP and RNA-binding-mutant rescue, can separate direct isoform regulation from secondary abundance compensation at Snrnp70 and companion genes. Mechanistic separation would enable comparative biomarkers, modifier screens, and therapy testing tailored to TDP-43 versus FUS states.

Title: Elp1-Elp2 and Bdp1 perturbations converge on aminoacyl-tRNA-synthetase and ATF4 stress in thalamic neurons
Core Idea: Elp1 and Elp2 perturbations induced a strong amino-acid-stress state in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut neurons, where Bdp1 perturbation converged on Ddit3 plus six cytosolic aminoacyl-tRNA synthetases; a smaller Elp1 state also recurred in midbrain, hypothalamic-thalamic, and GABAergic neurons. The convergence suggests that impaired Elongator-dependent tRNA modification and RNA polymerase III initiation meet at a neuronal tRNA-demand and translation-stress checkpoint, rather than marking every tRNA-modification perturbation.
Significance: This convergence links two different ways of compromising tRNA biology to one measurable neuronal stress axis. If validated, it would support substantial programs on codon-specific translation failure in familial dysautonomia and related neurodevelopmental disease, while testing whether GCN2-ATF4 or downstream amino-acid transport and charging pathways are therapeutically adjustable.
Prior expectation: ELP1 was known as the Elongator scaffold, with Elongator controlling [tRNA modification and codon-biased neuronal protein output](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-018-03221-z). BDP1 was established as a TFIIIB component required for [RNA polymerase III initiation](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-76066), while amino-acid limitation could induce cytosolic aminoacyl-tRNA synthetases through [ATF4-responsive elements](https://doi.org/10.1093/nar/gkw667). These facts made the direction coherent but did not predict convergence of the two perturbation classes in mature neurons.
Support: Elp1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut produced 645 FDR-significant genes, including Cars +1.12 (FDR 1.53e-28), Chac1 +3.43 (FDR 2.28e-28), Asns +1.46 (FDR 1.50e-27), Ddit3 +1.57 (FDR 3.13e-20), Atf4 +1.57 (FDR 4.66e-09), and Srsf5 -0.543 (FDR 4.79e-14). Elp2 perturbation produced 150 FDR-significant genes in the same context and closely matched the Elp1 signature. Bdp1 perturbation increased Ddit3 by +1.031 (FDR 0.00572), Mars1 by +0.605 (FDR 0.00572), Iars by +0.734 (FDR 0.0120), Lars by +0.663 (FDR 0.0120), Aars by +0.624 (FDR 0.0120), Cars by +0.683 (FDR 0.0151), and Yars by +0.706 (FDR 0.0429). Elp1 recurrence included Cars +0.709 (FDR 1.23e-05) and Chac1 +1.75 (FDR 0.00517) in 155 MB Glut, and Cars +0.957 (FDR 3.43e-07) in 191 MB P MY GABA. Trmt1 did not reproduce the 151 markers, but its functional modification activity was not measured; Bdp1 was FDR-responsive only in 151, and the atlas lacks tRNA abundance, modification, charging, ribosome occupancy, and protein-synthesis measurements, so generic integrated stress remains the strongest alternative.
Research community: Familial-dysautonomia, tRNA-modification, RNA polymerase III, translation-control, and integrated-stress researchers would be most motivated. After matched acute ELP1, ELP2, and BDP1 depletion, tRNA modification and charging, Pol III tRNA output, codon-resolved ribosome occupancy, and GCN2/ATF4 dependence should be ordered in time. A shared proximal tRNA defect and codon-pausing pattern preceding the RNA state would support a neuronal codon-vulnerability program spanning disease variants, cell-state demand, ISR modulation, and restoration of tRNA supply or modification.

Title: Proteasome-core and assembly perturbations couple proteasome-gene induction to loss of neuronal ion-pump and glutamate-receptor transcripts
Core Idea: Perturbing Psmb4, Psmc1, Psmc5, or Pomp repeatedly increased proteasome or proteostasis transcripts while lowering Atp1a3, Atp1b1, or Gria2 in responsive midbrain glutamatergic, Pvalb, and hypothalamic GABA neurons. The interpretation is not that proteasome function was restored, but that compensatory transcription coexists with erosion of a mature-neuronal expression state.
Significance: If the two arms are causally linked, proteasome stress in neurons is not adequately described by the familiar recovery response: the same cells may restore proteasome-component RNA while losing molecular support for membrane potential and glutamatergic signaling. That would justify a substantial program to define when proteasome compensation preserves neurons and when it marks entry into a physiologically compromised state relevant to neurodegeneration.
Prior expectation: Proteasome impairment was expected to engage recovery pathways because POMP was established as a [proteasome-assembly factor](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17948026/), neuronal loss of PSMC1 was known to cause [proteasome failure and neurodegeneration](https://www.jneurosci.org/content/28/33/8189), and NFE2L1 had been shown to mediate [proteasome recovery after inhibition](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20385086/). Those sources do not predict the accompanying loss of ion-pump and glutamate-receptor RNA.
Support: In 155 MB Glut, Pomp perturbation lowered Atp1a3 (log2FC -1.075, FDR 2.98e-11) and Gria2 (-0.997, FDR 1.16e-6) while increasing Ddit3 (+1.674, FDR 3.04e-5) and Psmc5 (+0.530, FDR 0.00268). In the same class, Psmc1 perturbation lowered Atp1a3 (-1.432, FDR 1.62e-23) and Atp1b1 (-1.556, FDR 1.98e-25), increased Ddit3 (+2.122, FDR 1.32e-9) and Psmd4 (+0.921, FDR 1.01e-7); Psmc5 perturbation likewise lowered Atp1a3 (-1.247, FDR 8.07e-13) and Atp1b1 (-1.094, FDR 1.78e-8) while increasing Psma5 (+0.891, FDR 0.00244). In 052 Pvalb Gaba, Psmb4 perturbation lowered Atp1a3 (-1.001, FDR 3.49e-4) and Gria2 (-0.886, FDR 1.20e-5) while increasing Psmc6 (+0.766, FDR 2.39e-6), Psmd4 (+0.960, FDR 5.34e-5), and Psme4 (+0.713, FDR 2.28e-4); the same directions recurred in 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA. The principal limitation is that RNA abundance does not measure proteasome activity, recovery, neuronal firing, or survival; shared integrated stress or a general state shift could generate both arms, and functional editing was not independently quantified.
Research community: Neuronal proteostasis, proteasome-recovery, and neurodegeneration researchers need to distinguish compensatory RNA induction from physiological rescue. Matched editing of several proteasome nodes in defined neurons, with proteasome-activity reporters, longitudinal survival, and patch-clamp measurement of Na+/K+-pump and glutamatergic function, would reveal whether recovery-gene induction can be uncoupled from decline. That distinction could guide a subunit-by-neuron vulnerability program and interventions that preserve excitability during proteasome stress.

Title: ANK3 perturbation lowers calcium-handling, receptor, and active-zone transcripts without a matched Cntn1, Cntnap1, or Sptan1 response
Core Idea: ANK3-targeted thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c glutamatergic neurons lost a broad set of calcium-handling, synaptic, and receptor transcripts, whereas targeting the nodal or cytoskeletal partners Cntn1, Cntnap1, and Sptan1 did not reproduce that RNA state in the same context. This matched observation supports, without proving, a model in which ankyrin-G maintains a transcriptionally coupled excitability program beyond generic node or axon-initial-segment structural disruption.
Significance: A validated scaffold-to-transcription connection would extend ankyrin-G biology from channel localization to maintenance of the molecular state that supports synaptic and calcium physiology. Because ANK3 is central to neuronal excitability and psychiatric genetics, separating this program from general axonal-structure failure could motivate multiple studies of how membrane scaffolds communicate with the nucleus.
Prior expectation: Established work predicted that ankyrin-G organizes [axon-initial-segment and nodal membrane domains](https://doi.org/10.1083/jcb.200612012), while SPTAN1 studies defined an [ankyrin–spectrin assembly hierarchy](https://www.jneurosci.org/content/37/47/11311) and linked spectrin dysfunction to [neurological disease](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36697767/). It did not specifically predict coordinated regulation of Atp2a2, Atp2b1, Erc2, Vgf, and neurotransmitter-receptor transcripts.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Ank3 perturbation lowered Ank3 itself (log2FC -0.256, FDR 8.42e-5), Opn3 (-1.053, FDR 9.24e-11), Atp2a2 (-0.360, FDR 9.24e-11), Atp2b1 (-0.337, FDR 3.81e-10), Erc2 (-0.545, FDR 1.75e-8), Vgf (-0.852, FDR 2.40e-8), Ache (-0.688, FDR 2.58e-8), Cacna2d1 (-0.227, FDR 0.00184), Gabra4 (-0.191, FDR 0.00226), Grm1 (-0.214, FDR 0.00770), and Gria4 (-0.205, FDR 0.0167), while increasing Pcsk1n (+0.552, FDR 1.10e-8). Cntn1, Cntnap1, and Sptan1 were each strongly depleted in the same class (respectively log2FC -0.880, -0.822, and -0.529; FDR 4.36e-29, 1.09e-11, and 2.03e-13), yet their Atp2a2, Opn3, Atp2b1, Pcsk1n, and Erc2 rows all had FDR 1.0. The alternative is unequal functional editing, protein turnover, or a perturbation-specific stress response rather than an ankyrin-G-to-nucleus pathway; axon-initial-segment structure and electrical function were not measured, and the positive state was resolved in one high-yield context.
Research community: Axon-initial-segment, ankyrin/spectrin, ion-channel, and psychiatric-neurogenetics communities would be most affected. ANK3 and structural comparators should be depleted to matched protein levels, then ANK3 rescued with constructs that retain or lack defined channel/scaffold-binding domains while axon-initial-segment architecture, nascent RNA, and excitability are measured. Domain-selective transcript rescue would distinguish signaling from generic structural injury and connect ANK3 risk alleles to scaffold-dependent transcriptional homeostasis.

Title: CALM1 perturbation induces a Rasgrp2/Peg3 module opposite to CAMTA1 and not reported after CALM3 targeting
Core Idea: CALM1-targeted thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c glutamatergic neurons increased Rasgrp2, Peg3, Aifm3, Resp18, and Coro6 while lowering Atp2b1; CAMTA1 perturbation moved five induced markers oppositely, and the source report did not observe the module after CALM3 targeting. A CALM1-locus or calmodulin-paralog dependency is one interpretation despite identical encoded proteins, but unequal editing or locus-level regulation remains a strong alternative.
Significance: A genuine CALM1-versus-CALM3 functional difference would change how calmodulin genes are modeled in neurons: dosage, transcript localization, untranslated regions, or locus-specific regulation could matter even when protein sequence does not. That result would create a focused program spanning calmodulinopathy, calcium-dependent transcription, and the design of gene-specific rescue strategies.
Prior expectation: [Curated CALM1 protein records](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P0DP26) support the identity and conservation of the calmodulin core, while a [curated calcium-signaling pathway](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-111997) places calmodulin upstream of CaM-dependent enzymes, NMDA-receptor feedback, and calcium-responsive transcription. CAMTA1 is an established calmodulin-binding regulator required for [long-term memory](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27194798/). This biology predicts extensive protein-level redundancy but not the signed CALM1/CAMTA1 relationship.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Calm1 perturbation depleted Calm1 (log2FC -0.924, FDR 1.67e-34) and increased Rasgrp2 (+0.862, FDR 9.26e-9), Peg3 (+0.216, FDR 2.38e-6), Aifm3 (+0.328, FDR 0.00321), Resp18 (+0.366, FDR 0.00660), and Coro6 (+0.271, FDR 0.0182), while lowering Atp2b1 (-0.136, FDR 0.0326). Camta1 perturbation drove Coro6 (-1.094, FDR 2.81e-35), Peg3 (-0.770, FDR 1.40e-33), Aifm3 (-1.102, FDR 8.68e-26), Resp18 (-0.874, FDR 8.96e-12), and Rasgrp2 (-0.958, FDR 4.34e-5) in the opposite direction. The reported CALM3 non-phenocopy is not accompanied here by exact comparator rows or a protein-level engagement measure, and Calm2 was weakly engaged; guide, locus, expression, or sampling differences therefore prevent a firm paralog conclusion. The RNA readout also does not measure CaM binding, calcium dynamics, CaMK, calcineurin, or channel gating. Because the positive module was resolved only in high-yield 151, no broader cell-context specificity can be inferred.
Research community: Calmodulin signaling, calmodulinopathy, calcium-channel, and synaptic-plasticity researchers would reconsider the assumption that CALM loci are interchangeable. Defined loss-of-function alleles at Calm1 and Calm3, quantitative editing and CaM protein measurements, and reciprocal coding-sequence/UTR rescue during calcium imaging and Rasgrp2/Peg3 readout would separate RNA architecture from protein identity. A locus- or UTR-dependent rescue pattern could establish a broader program on gene-specific control of identical calcium-sensor proteins and locus-tailored therapies.

Priority Score: 4
Title: ADARB1 perturbation recurrently lowers Flnb across cortical, subcortical, and cerebellar neuronal classes
Core Idea: Flnb RNA decreased directionally after Adarb1 perturbation in every tested neuronal class and reached FDR significance in 12 of 22, with large effects across thalamic, midbrain, hypothalamic, Pvalb, cortical, and cerebellar populations. Flnb is therefore a robust neuronal readout of ADAR2 loss, but its interpretation remains open among FLNB editing, another ADAR2-dependent RNA process, and an indirect cytoskeletal-state response.
Significance: The result redirects attention from canonical glutamate-receptor substrates toward a recurrent filamin/actin-associated consequence of ADAR2 disruption. If the ADARB1-FLNB connection proves causal, RNA-editing researchers could pursue a new route by which ADAR2 influences neuronal structure, mechanics, or transport, with implications for disease phenotypes not explained by GRIA2 alone.
Prior expectation: ADAR2 was already known to edit Q/R sites in FLNA and FLNB, and a study of [ADAR2-deficient mouse heart](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0200968) examined FLNB editing and expression. That precedent makes a filamin connection biologically plausible, but it did not establish a recurrent neuronal Flnb-abundance decrease across these brain populations.
Support: Adarb1 perturbation lowered Flnb in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut (log2FC -1.479, FDR 6.67e-44), 155 MB Glut (-1.654, FDR 1.57e-38), 191 MB P MY GABA (-1.477, FDR 7.81e-16), 145 MH-LH TH Glut (-1.198, FDR 6.89e-15), 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA (-0.913, FDR 6.36e-11), and 052 Pvalb Gaba (-0.797, FDR 2.44e-8). Additional significant decreases occurred in 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut (-0.693, FDR 2.52e-5), 054 CNU-MGE GABA (-0.850, FDR 1.38e-4), 110 CNU-HYa HY MM Glut (-0.912, FDR 1.57e-4), 012 MEA LA CA1 DG Glut (-0.890, FDR 0.0276), and 308 CB GABA (-0.738, FDR 0.0218). Adarb1 itself was depleted in 151 (-0.555, FDR 2.04e-9), 066 (-0.816, FDR 0.0189), and 052 (-0.616, FDR 0.0401). The strongest alternative is an editing-dependent effect on FLNB RNA stability rather than an editing-independent regulatory link; editing sites, FLNB protein, cytoskeletal function, and matched Adar or Gria2 functional engagement were not measured.
Research community: RNA-editing, neuronal-cytoskeleton, and ADAR-linked-disease communities would gain a common mechanistic question. Targeted long-read RNA sequencing and site-resolved editing after ADARB1 loss, with wild-type versus catalytically inactive ADAR2 rescue and editing-mimetic FLNB constructs, can separate editing-dependent transcript stability from indirect or editing-independent regulation. The answer would support systematic mapping from ADAR2-dependent structural RNA targets to neuronal morphology and circuit phenotypes.

Title: MAX perturbation derepresses STAG3 and circadian E-box genes while MGA shares only the STAG3 branch
Core Idea: MAX targeting strongly induced Stag3 in thalamic and midbrain glutamatergic neurons and induced Per2 and Cry1 in the thalamic class; MGA targeting also induced Stag3 and Obscn but shared little whole-transcriptome similarity with MAX. The pattern suggests separable MAX-containing repressor branches in adult neurons—an MGA-associated germline/meiotic branch and a distinct circadian E-box branch—rather than a unitary loss of MYC-driven biosynthesis.
Significance: Confirming separable MAX repressor outputs would revise how the MYC/MAX network is organized in mature neurons and connect chromatin repression of normally silent meiotic genes to neuronal clock control. It would support substantial work on how different MAX partners partition repression, whether inappropriate germline-gene activation harms neurons, and whether clock disruption is mechanistically linked or parallel.
Prior expectation: MAX is the shared bHLHZip partner for activating MYC complexes and several repressive MXD/MNT/MGA complexes. Prior work supports MAX/MGA repression of [germline genes](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29381691/) and [meiotic genes](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27025988/), as well as MAX-dependent repression at [circadian E-box promoters](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32225100/). The unexpected feature is that these branches separate within the same neuronal perturbation map rather than moving as one generic MAX program.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Max perturbation increased Stag3 (log2FC +3.324, FDR 1.90e-11), Per2 (+1.108, FDR 1.64e-10), and Cry1 (+0.654, FDR 0.0275); in 155 MB Glut it increased Stag3 (+3.047, FDR 5.03e-6). Mga perturbation in 151 increased Stag3 (+3.606, FDR 4.83e-5) and Obscn (+4.435, FDR 1.73e-8). The source found 167 FDR-significant Max gene-by-cell rows across six tested contexts, but MAX and MGA had weak whole-transcriptome similarity. Max RNA itself was not FDR-depleted and had mixed directions, so the result is a Max-locus perturbation phenotype, not demonstrated MAX protein loss. The reported absence of Stag3 induction after MYC/MYCN/E2F-DP targeting is not decisive without matched functional engagement, and direct occupancy or ncPRC1.6 activity was not measured. Concentration of the strongest positive rows in 151/155 is compatible with their high effective power and does not establish restriction to those classes.
Research community: MYC/MAX-network, ncPRC1.6, neuronal-chromatin, and circadian-transcription researchers need to determine whether the two branches occupy distinct regulatory complexes. Time-resolved MAX and MGA CUT&RUN with nascent RNA after independently validated editing would test direct, partner-specific control at Stag3 and Per2/Cry1. Separating those branches would motivate substantial work on mature-neuron repressor-complex partitioning, ectopic germline programs, and clock-linked vulnerability.

Title: ATP6V0C and PITPNA perturbations converge on a calcium, PKC, and secretory-transcript state in thalamic neurons
Core Idea: ATP6V0C and PITPNA targeting in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c glutamatergic neurons both lowered Atp2a2, Prkcg, Ramp3, and Tubb3 while increasing Scg2; ATP6V0C targeting additionally produced broad stress, ion-channel, and secretory responses in thalamic and Pvalb neurons. One interpretation is convergence of vesicle or endolysosomal acidification and phosphatidylinositol transfer on a calcium/PKC-sensitive state, but shared downstream stress or ATP6V0C-driven Pitpna reduction is equally plausible.
Significance: A causal ATP6V0C-PITPNA connection would join proton-pump function to phosphoinositide supply and calcium handling, two processes already implicated separately in synaptic physiology and neurodegeneration. Establishing the order of these events could create a major line of work on how organelle acidification and membrane-lipid signaling jointly determine neuronal resilience.
Prior expectation: ATP6V0C is the [proton-conducting V0 pore component](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P63082) on synaptic-vesicle and lysosomal membranes. PITPNA supports phosphatidylinositol supply for [PLC-linked signaling](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15322105/), and reduced Pitpna causes [neurodegeneration in the vibrator mouse](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9182797/). These priors make interaction plausible but do not predict the shared signed neuronal RNA state.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Atp6v0c perturbation depleted Atp6v0c (log2FC -1.433, FDR 1.04e-34), lowered Atp2a2 (-0.577, FDR 3.09e-20), Prkcg (-0.856, FDR 1.56e-16), Ramp3 (-0.744, FDR 1.54e-9), Tubb3 (-0.706, FDR 1.35e-10), Kcnc1 (-0.234, FDR 0.0402), and Gria2 (-0.226, FDR 0.0394), and increased Scg2 (+0.682, FDR 1.57e-10), Ptprn (+0.333, FDR 6.74e-5), Dusp1 (+1.133, FDR 2.03e-6), and Crem (+1.104, FDR 7.82e-8). Pitpna perturbation in the same class depleted Pitpna (-0.788, FDR 2.48e-16), lowered Atp2a2 (-0.226, FDR 7.06e-4), Prkcg (-0.376, FDR 5.90e-5), Ramp3 (-0.337, FDR 0.00579), and Tubb3 (-0.260, FDR 0.0356), and increased Scg2 (+0.358, FDR 0.0142). ATP6V0C targeting also lowered Pitpna itself (-0.328, FDR 0.00712), which could account for part of the overlap. In 052 Pvalb Gaba, Atp6v0c perturbation induced Atf3 (+6.345, FDR 0.00149), Crem (+1.268, FDR 8.63e-6), and Scg2 (+0.893, FDR 1.97e-5) while lowering Kcnc1 (-0.451, FDR 3.24e-6) and Gria2 (-0.488, FDR 2.16e-4). Organelle pH, vesicle filling, phosphoinositides, calcium, and flux were unmeasured; uneven protein depletion among V-ATPase subunits also prevents a subunit-selective vulnerability conclusion.
Research community: V-ATPase, membrane-lipid-signaling, synaptic-vesicle, and neurodegeneration researchers are positioned to resolve the connection. Matched ATP6V0C and PITPNA editing with simultaneous lysosomal/synaptic-vesicle pH, phosphoinositide, and calcium imaging, followed by PITPNA-restoration or re-acidification epistasis, would order the pathway if only one intervention rescues the shared RNA state. This could establish a sustained program on organelle-lipid coupling across neuronal subtypes and disease alleles.
