Title: GRIN2A raises an L2/3 NPTX2-BDNF-PTGS2 program while GRIN2B trends oppositely and significantly lowers Vgf and Kcnv1
Core Idea: GRIN2A targeting increased an activity/plasticity-associated RNA set in upper-layer cortical excitatory neurons, whereas GRIN2B targeting in L2/3 moved the same markers downward, with FDR support strongest for Vgf and Kcnv1 on the GRIN2B side. Opposite homeostatic states after chronic loss of GluN2A- and GluN2B-containing receptors are a plausible interpretation, but asymmetry in statistical and on-target support makes subunit non-equivalence provisional.
Significance: Validated sign-opposed responses would add a chronic transcriptional dimension to GluN2A/GluN2B specialization and could change how subunit-selective NMDAR interventions are expected to remodel cortical circuits over time. This would seed work connecting receptor composition to homeostatic plasticity, neurotrophin signaling, and long-term drug effects rather than treating both subunits as interchangeable reductions in NMDAR current.
Prior expectation: Established work predicted [distinct GluN2A and GluN2B disease and circuit roles](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31807283/), while [NPTX2/Narp homeostatic synaptic scaling](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20729843/) and [NMDA-receptor-dependent neuronal PTGS2 induction](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35354047/) provided marker-level precedents. It did not specifically predict opposite chronic regulation of the Nptx2/Bdnf/Ptgs2/Vgf group in superficial cortical neurons.
Support: In 007 L2-3 IT CTX Glut, Grin2a perturbation increased Nptx2 (log2FC +1.777, FDR 6.09e-4), Bdnf (+1.249, FDR 0.0272), Ptgs2 (+1.264, FDR 0.0353), and Cckbr (+0.832, FDR 0.00117); Vgf also rose (+0.650) but had FDR 0.395. In 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, the most secure component was Nptx2 induction (+1.401, FDR 7.94e-6), with Grin2a depletion at -0.349, FDR 0.0239. In 007, Grin2b perturbation depleted Grin2b (-0.471, FDR 0.00107), lowered Kcnv1 (-1.108, FDR 2.89e-5) and Vgf (-0.987, FDR 0.0194), and moved Bdnf (-1.156, FDR 0.184), Cckbr (-0.831, FDR 0.0707), Nptx2 (-1.032, FDR 0.617), and Ptgs2 (-1.453, FDR 0.286) in the opposite direction without FDR support. Thus the multigene reversal rests partly on nominal evidence, and Grin2a itself was not FDR-depleted in L2/3 (log2FC -0.401, FDR 0.274). Unequal editing, receptor turnover, baseline activity, or sampled cell state could explain the contrast; receptor currents and network activity were not measured.
Research community: NMDAR-subunit, cortical-plasticity, and neuropsychiatric-pharmacology communities would be most motivated by a verified sign reversal. Independently validated, matched Grin2a and Grin2b edits in identified L2/3 neurons should be followed longitudinally through subunit currents, network activity, and RNA trajectories, with subunit-specific rescue as the causal test. Reversible divergence would create a research direction on chronic subunit-selective homeostasis and delayed responses to NMDAR-modulating drugs.

Title: EEF2 targeting induces a large Vgf, Nptx2, and Cx3cl1 trophic state in superficial cortical neurons
Core Idea: EEF2 targeting generated a 575-gene response in L2/3 intratelencephalic cortical neurons, led by strong Vgf, Nptx2, and Cx3cl1 induction and Uchl1 repression, with smaller Vgf- or Ranbp2-positive responses in other neuronal classes. Impaired elongation may provoke a superficial-cortical trophic and neuron-to-glia signaling state, but unchanged Eef2 RNA and the absence of direct translation measurements leave guide-linked or secondary state effects unresolved.
Significance: If EEF2 protein disruption causes this state, translational stress in cortical neurons would have an unexpectedly strong secretory and trophic output rather than a simple uniform loss of protein synthesis. That could support major work on how vulnerable excitatory neurons signal adaptation or distress to surrounding cells and whether VGF, NPTX2, or CX3CL1 promotes recovery versus pathology.
Prior expectation: EEF2 is the [conserved ribosomal translocation factor](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P58252), and neuronal eEF2 phosphorylation is established as a [regulator of dendritic translation](https://doi.org/10.3389/fncel.2014.00035). These priors predict translation-related consequences but not a large Vgf/Nptx2/Cx3cl1 abundance state centered in superficial cortical neurons.
Support: In 007 L2-3 IT CTX Glut, Eef2 perturbation increased Vgf (log2FC +2.149, FDR 1.01e-8), Nptx2 (+2.946, FDR 3.02e-8), and Cx3cl1 (+0.864, FDR 4.93e-8), while lowering Uchl1 (-0.712, FDR 5.35e-5); the full response contained 575 FDR-significant genes. Smaller echoes included Vgf induction in 052 Pvalb Gaba (+1.424, FDR 1.35e-5) and Ranbp2 induction in 155 MB Glut (+0.827, FDR 2.60e-5). Eef2 RNA itself was not FDR-depleted in any tested class, and effective protein disruption, elongation rate, ribosome occupancy, cell survival, and secreted peptide levels were not measured. Independent-guide effects, selective survival, or a pre-existing state imbalance are therefore major alternatives, and the stronger L2/3 yield does not by itself establish intrinsic cell-type specificity.
Research community: Translation-control, cortical-circuit, neurotrophin, and neuron-glia-signaling researchers would pursue whether this trophic state is adaptive. Independent Eef2 alleles, protein quantification, ribosome profiling or nascent-protein labeling, and time-resolved single-cell RNA would first distinguish elongation stress from guide-linked selection; VGF/CX3CL1 neutralization could then test effects on edited and neighboring cells. Those results would support a program on secretory adaptation to translational stress across cortical layers and disease models.

Title: BRAF and MAP2K1 perturbations repress, whereas NF1 perturbation induces, a neuronal VGF-PTPN5-ETV5 MAPK module
Core Idea: In thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c glutamatergic neurons, BRAF and MAP2K1 targeting lowered a shared Vgf/Ptpn5/Unc13a axis, NF1 targeting moved Vgf, Ptpn5, Atp2a2, and Etv5 upward, and SHOC2 targeting also lowered Vgf; NF1-associated Vgf induction recurred in midbrain glutamatergic and midbrain/pons/myelencephalon GABA neurons. These signed RNA responses support a neuronal RAF-MEK branch opposite the NF1 Ras brake, while weaker paralog and LZTR1 comparisons remain boundaries rather than evidence of biochemical dispensability.
Significance: The expected pathway polarity becomes valuable here because it is attached to a concrete neuronal output containing VGF, STEP/PTPN5, ETV factors, calcium handling, and release machinery. Validating that output could give RASopathy and neurofibromatosis research a cell-resolved bridge from pathway genotype to neuronal physiology and enable branch-selective biomarkers for interventions.
Prior expectation: BRAF is an [upstream kinase for MAP2K1 and MAPK activation](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P15056/entry). NF1 is a Ras GTPase-activating negative regulator whose loss activates the [RAS/RAF/MAPK pathway](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-6802957), whereas SHOC2 supports [RAF activation dynamics](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1902658116). The expected sign is therefore NF1 loss opposite BRAF/MEK loss; the new element is the specific mature-neuronal RNA module and its recurrence.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Map2k1 perturbation lowered Vgf (log2FC -0.785, FDR 7.85e-8), Ptpn5 (-0.436, FDR 9.37e-4), Unc13a (-0.325, FDR 0.0119), and Etv5 (-0.631, FDR 0.0119). Braf perturbation lowered Vgf (-0.795, FDR 1.39e-4), Ptpn5 (-0.635, FDR 1.39e-4), Atp2a2 (-0.263, FDR 1.39e-4), and Unc13a (-0.315, FDR 0.00393). Nf1 perturbation reversed the axis: Vgf +0.848 (FDR 1.36e-11), Ptpn5 +0.550 (FDR 1.64e-8), Atp2a2 +0.260 (FDR 7.30e-5), and Etv5 +0.541 (FDR 0.0127); Shoc2 perturbation lowered Vgf (-0.660, FDR 0.00794). NF1-associated Vgf induction recurred in 155 MB Glut (+2.192, FDR 1.75e-16) and 191 MB P MY GABA (+1.871, FDR 1.78e-9), where Scg2 and Unc13a also rose (+0.856 and +0.482, both FDR 0.00820). Braf RNA depletion was not FDR-significant, Map2k1 self-depletion was not FDR-significant, and direct MAPK phosphorylation or activity was not measured; differential editing, activity-state changes, and Vgf neurotrophin responsiveness could explain part of the polarity. Nominal LZTR1 rows and weak RAF1/MAPK1 negatives do not establish node-specific independence.
Research community: RASopathy, neurofibromatosis, neuronal-MAPK, and synaptic-plasticity communities could use this as a candidate functional map of pathway dosage. A matched NF1, BRAF, MAP2K1, and SHOC2 allelic series in identified neuronal classes, with time-resolved phospho-ERK and genetic epistasis, should test whether ERK normalization restores VGF/PTPN5/ETV5 and electrical phenotypes in the predicted order. Causal ordering would support stratification of RAS/MAPK disorders by neuronal branch output and tests of state-selective pathway correction.

Title: SIN3A targeting induces cilium and cholesterol genes while repressing spliceosome transcripts in thalamic and midbrain neurons
Core Idea: SIN3A targeting produced a large thalamic response with induction of cilium/centrosome, Hcn3, Cyp46a1, and Grn transcripts alongside repression of Srrm2, Snrnp70, Rnpc3, Ddx17, and other RNA-processing genes; a smaller cilium/cholesterol and spliceosome pattern recurred in midbrain and inhibitory neurons. A SIN3A-dependent chromatin state coordinating ciliary or sterol biology with RNA processing is one interpretation, but the upward Sin3a RNA shift and response breadth leave direct repression, allelic consequence, and causal unity unresolved.
Significance: SIN3A-related disease is usually framed through cortical development and intellectual disability. A validated mature-neuronal program joining cilia, brain cholesterol turnover, and spliceosome abundance would broaden that model and create several mechanistically linked directions for Witteveen-Kolk syndrome, synaptic plasticity, and neuronal chromatin research.
Prior expectation: SIN3A was expected to act as an [HDAC-associated transcriptional scaffold](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bbagrm.2009.05.007), and haploinsufficiency is established in a [neurodevelopmental syndrome with altered cortical development](https://doi.org/10.1038/ng.3619). Postnatal excitatory-neuron deletion also affects [hippocampal plasticity through Homer1/mGluR5 signaling](https://doi.org/10.1172/jci.insight.92385). These sources do not predict the observed cilium/cholesterol-plus-spliceosome relationship.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Sin3a targeting increased Odf2 (log2FC +0.820, FDR 1.71e-26), Hcn3 (+2.192, FDR 3.99e-23), Dzip1 (+0.840, FDR 4.54e-21), Cyp46a1 (+0.299, FDR 1.30e-10), and Grn (+0.423, FDR 0.00419), while lowering Srrm2 (-0.476, FDR 1.60e-19), Snhg11 (-0.236, FDR 5.84e-19), Snrnp70 (-0.392, FDR 2.39e-11), Rnpc3 (-0.593, FDR 2.76e-11), and Ddx17 (-0.352, FDR 1.05e-9). In 155 MB Glut, the grounded summary reports Cyp46a1 induction with score 7.553 and FDR 1.85e-10, Odf2 induction with score 7.119 and FDR 2.70e-9, Snhg11 repression with score -9.925 and FDR 5.67e-19, and Srrm2 repression with score -7.924 and FDR 1.34e-11. Sin3a RNA itself increased in 151 (+1.354, FDR 2.36e-17) and in four other responsive classes, so its direction cannot be read as measured loss; protein disruption, chromatin occupancy, splicing, ciliary structure, cholesterol flux, and neuronal physiology were not assessed. A broad stress response or guide-specific allele effect could unite the signatures, and weak REST or other chromatin-comparator responses do not prove SIN3A specificity. The dominance of 151/155 may reflect their greater effective power, so it cannot establish preferential biological sensitivity.
Research community: SIN3A/Witteveen-Kolk, neuronal-chromatin, cilia, cholesterol, and RNA-processing communities need to determine whether this is one regulatory state or several secondary consequences. Defined Sin3a alleles with SIN3A protein quantification, CUT&RUN/ATAC, and long-read RNA in the same purified neurons would test direct occupancy and splicing, while ciliary imaging and sterol measurements separate downstream branches. Coherent causal ordering would establish a mature-neuron SIN3A program connecting chromatin dosage to organelle, lipid, and RNA-processing phenotypes.

Title: MEF2C and MEF2A perturbations resolve different cortical and thalamic neuronal outputs
Core Idea: Observation: Mef2c perturbation produced a shared synaptic and membrane-associated response in L4/5 and L2/3 intratelencephalic cortical neurons, whereas the FDR-supported Mef2a response was a compact Gpr4/Ogt/Prkcg/Sez6 set in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons. Interpretation: acute MEF2 paralog loss may engage non-interchangeable mature-neuronal programs, but the different cell contexts and engagement profiles prevent a clean intrinsic paralog comparison.
Significance: If matched perturbations reproduce the split, MEF2 dosage disorders would need to be understood through paralog-by-neuronal-state interactions rather than a single generic MEF2 transcriptional program. That could support a substantial comparative program on how MEF2A and MEF2C maintain adult cortical and thalamic physiology.
Prior expectation: MEF2 factors were already expected to regulate activity-dependent neuronal transcription and synapse development, and MEF2C dosage was known to be neurodevelopmentally important ([MEF2C control of cortical synapses](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.20059); [MEF2 signaling review](https://doi.org/10.3389/fncel.2024.1465821)). Those priors did not specify the signed cortical MEF2C program or establish a MEF2A-versus-MEF2C boundary in these mature neuronal classes.
Support: After Mef2c perturbation, 007 L2-3 IT CTX Glut increased Stac2 (log2FC +2.066, FDR 1.51e-5), Serinc2 (+1.781, FDR 0.00355), Chgb (+0.819, FDR 2.56e-7), and Gria2 (+0.174, FDR 0.0221), while lowering Slit2 (-1.587, FDR 2.38e-5); the same directions recurred in 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, including Stac2 +1.479 (FDR 2.94e-4) and Chgb +0.481 (FDR 5.17e-5). Mef2c itself fell in both classes (-0.547, FDR 1.31e-4; -0.391, FDR 0.00242). In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Mef2a perturbation lowered Gpr4 (-1.348, FDR 0.00430), Ogt (-0.175, FDR 0.00571), and Prkcg (-0.256, FDR 0.00571) and increased Sez6 (+0.476, FDR 0.0205), while Mef2a RNA was only nominally lower (-0.427, FDR 0.0574). The strongest alternative is context or editing-quality dependence rather than paralog identity; the atlas supplies no matched cell class in which both perturbations produced comparably engaged, directly comparable programs.
Research community: MEF2, activity-dependent transcription, cortical-circuit, and neurodevelopmental-disorder researchers would find the paralog boundary useful. The decisive experiment is matched Mef2a and Mef2c editing in the same purified cortical and thalamic neuronal types, with protein-level engagement, CUT&RUN, nascent RNA, and reciprocal paralog rescue. Confirmation would justify a focused program mapping paralog-specific occupancy and physiological outputs across mature neuronal identities.

Title: ESRRA and PPARGC1B perturbations converge weakly on a neuronal mitochondrial axis while retaining distinct outputs
Core Idea: Observation: Esrra perturbation produced a broad, mostly negative thalamic program containing metabolic, membrane, and synaptic genes, while Ppargc1b perturbation produced a much narrower mitochondrial signal and shared a restricted negative gene set with Esrra. Interpretation: ERRα and PGC-1β may participate in a mature-neuronal oxidative transcriptional axis, but the small cross-perturbation correlation and weak Ppargc1b engagement make functional convergence provisional.
Significance: A validated ERRα–PGC-1β dependency would extend mitochondrial coactivator biology into defined mature neuronal states and clarify whether PGC-1 family paralogs provide separable routes to respiratory maintenance. Because the present overlap is restricted, this is a high-value mechanistic lead rather than evidence for a complete shared mitochondrial program.
Prior expectation: ERRα was already implicated in PGC-1-dependent mitochondrial and synaptic gene expression in mouse brain ([McMeekin et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuroscience.2021.10.007)), and PGC-1β was known to bind ERR proteins and regulate mitochondrial capacity ([Kamei et al. 2003](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/14530391/); [Wareski et al. 2009](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19542216/)). Prior work therefore predicted some relationship, but not its restricted overlap or separation from PGC-1α and PPAR perturbations in these neurons.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Esrra perturbation yielded 57 FDR genes, including Gas6 (log2FC -0.556, FDR 1.56e-9), Bcan (-0.937, FDR 2.38e-6), Nceh1 (-0.376, FDR 8.11e-6), Mdh1 (-0.250, FDR 1.53e-4), and Atp1b1 (-0.209, FDR 1.53e-4), while Esrra itself fell -0.481 (FDR 2.07e-4). In Ppargc1b-targeted 155 MB Glut, Atp5b was the sole FDR hit (-0.515, FDR 0.00754); in 151, Ppargc1b and Esrra shared score<-2 calls including Idh3b (Ppargc1b -0.340, FDR 0.225; Esrra -0.250, FDR 0.0288) and Uqcrc1 (-0.226, FDR 0.282; -0.197, FDR 0.0725). Esrra was Ppargc1b's nearest positive perturbation neighbor in 151, but the whole-transcriptome correlation was only r=0.0946. Ppargc1b self RNA never reached FDR<0.05 in 21 contexts, and Ppargc1a/PPAR comparisons were near zero; unequal functional editing, a shared stress state, and the absence of respiratory-flux measurements remain major alternatives.
Research community: Neuronal bioenergetics, nuclear-receptor, PGC-1, and neurodegeneration communities would be the natural audience. Matched ESRRA and PPARGC1B depletion with protein quantification, mitochondrial respiration, isotope tracing, and reciprocal coactivator rescue in thalamic and midbrain neurons would distinguish a shared oxidative module from weak correlated stress. If confirmed, the result would support targeted work on paralog-specific mitochondrial maintenance rather than a broad PGC-1 replacement strategy.

Title: SRRM2 and several RNA-processing perturbations expose recurrent but nonidentical nuclear-speckle transcript responses
Core Idea: Observation: Srrm2 loss and perturbations of Nop56, Huwe1, Snrnp70, and U2af2 changed overlapping nuclear-speckle or splice-regulator transcripts, especially Rsrp1, but sometimes in opposite directions. Interpretation: selected neuronal states may share an abundance-level response to RNA-processing stress, although the heterogeneous perturbations do not establish a single pathway or direct epistasis.
Significance: The useful result is a comparative liability map rather than a unitary spliceosome program: different RNA-processing lesions may converge on a small reporter set while retaining mechanism-specific signs and outputs. If validated at the level of splicing and speckle organization, this could motivate a substantial effort to stratify neuronal RNA-processing disorders by state and lesion.
Prior expectation: SRRM2 was already established as a nuclear-speckle scaffold and disease-relevant RNA-processing factor ([Ilik et al. 2020](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.60579); [Xu et al. 2022](https://doi.org/10.1093/nar/gkac669)), while transport and splice-factor work supported sensitivity of the same machinery ([Lai et al. 2001](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11517331/)). These sources predict RNA-processing consequences, but not one shared signed response across all six perturbations.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Srrm2 perturbation strongly lowered Srrm2 (log2FC -1.565, FDR 8.05e-38) and Rsrp1 (-0.281, FDR 3.54e-5); in 155 MB Glut it lowered Leng8 (-0.848, FDR 1.88e-22). In the same thalamic class, Nop56 perturbation lowered Rsrp1 (-0.233, FDR 1.73e-6), U2af2 perturbation lowered it (-0.983, FDR 9.01e-29), but Huwe1 perturbation increased it (+0.462, FDR 2.07e-17); Snrnp70 perturbation lowered Srsf5 (-0.361, FDR 5.58e-5). Thus the positive evidence is a recurrent RNA-processing neighborhood, not a uniform direction. Several cortical and Pvalb contexts had target-RNA shifts without corrected downstream genes, but unequal power and functional engagement make those nulls non-diagnostic; total RNA abundance also cannot establish altered splice isoforms, speckle morphology, or a common causal mechanism.
Research community: Nuclear-speckle, spliceosome, RNA-binding-protein disease, and neuronal-vulnerability researchers would benefit most. The discriminating study would perturb each factor at matched protein loss in the same neuronal types and jointly measure long-read isoforms, nascent RNA, speckle morphology, and RSRP1 localization; shared functional defects despite opposite Rsrp1 RNA signs would support a higher-order stress state. Confirmation would enable mechanism-based grouping of neuronal RNA-processing disorders and lesion-specific biomarker development.

Title: GRIN2B and DLGAP4 perturbations independently lower Vgf in cortical projection neurons
Core Idea: Observation: Grin2b perturbation recurrently lowered Vgf in L2/3, L4/5, and L5 cortical projection neurons, and Dlgap4 perturbation independently lowered Vgf, with Scg2 joining in L5 ET neurons. Interpretation: an NMDAR–SAPAP4 neighborhood may couple postsynaptic state to regulated neuropeptide transcripts, but sparse Dlgap4 outputs and small signature correlations do not establish direct receptor-to-scaffold signaling.
Significance: If VGF is a functional readout of NMDAR/SAPAP4 state, it would provide a tractable bridge from postsynaptic architecture to neuropeptide and trophic signaling in projection neurons. The modest response breadth keeps this as a focused cortical mechanism rather than a broad new synaptic program.
Prior expectation: GluN2 subunits were known to shape NMDAR signaling and plasticity ([Paoletti et al. 2013](https://doi.org/10.1038/nrn3504)), and SAPAP4 was established as a PSD95–SAPAP–SHANK scaffold component ([Welch et al. 2004](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15024750/); [SAPAP review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/36497075/)). Those priors make a shared postsynaptic neighborhood plausible but do not predict Vgf as the recurring signed output.
Support: After Grin2b perturbation, 007 L2-3 IT CTX Glut had 47 FDR genes, including Kcnv1 (log2FC -1.108, FDR 2.89e-5), Vgf (-0.987, FDR 0.0194), and Adamts2 (+2.102, FDR 0.0118); 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut lowered Vgf (-0.752, FDR 5.52e-4), Kcnv1 (-0.728, FDR 5.78e-4), Nr4a1 (-0.768, FDR 0.0110), and Rph3a (-0.615, FDR 0.0110). In 022 L5 ET CTX Glut, Grin2b perturbation again lowered Vgf (-0.755, FDR 0.0347) and increased Adamts2 (+1.098, FDR 0.0347), although Grin2b self RNA was FDR 0.105. Dlgap4 perturbation lowered Vgf in 151 TH (-0.409, FDR 0.0289) and lowered Scg2 (-1.145, FDR 0.00438) and Vgf (-0.566, FDR 0.0111) in L5 ET. Dlgap4–Grin2b score correlations were only r=0.1395 in L5 ET and r=0.0916 in L4/5; common activity loss, unequal editing, or convergent secondary state change could explain the marker without a direct molecular axis.
Research community: NMDAR, postsynaptic-density, cortical projection-neuron, and neuropeptide researchers would be positioned to test the link. Matched GRIN2B and DLGAP4 depletion with receptor currents, PSD proteomics, Vgf nascent transcription and secretion, plus scaffold-domain rescue would distinguish shared postsynaptic signaling from generic reduced activity. A confirmed causal relationship would support a bounded program on synapse-to-neuropeptide coupling in cortical projection classes.

Title: TSC2 perturbation induces a cerebellar GABA synaptic and calcium-transcript state
Core Idea: Observation: Tsc2 perturbation in cerebellar GABA neurons increased a 37-gene set containing Shank3, Cacna1d, Stxbp1, Grm8, Cabp1, and Slc8a3. Interpretation: TSC2 loss may engage a noncanonical synaptic/calcium branch in these neurons, but silent Tsc1 and Mtor RNA responses cannot establish a TSC2-specific mechanism without matched functional engagement.
Significance: If the branch is reproducible and separable from canonical mTORC1 output, it could broaden TSC2 neuronal biology toward synaptic calcium regulation in cerebellar inhibitory cells. The comparator uncertainty means this should begin as a targeted mechanism study rather than a general revision of TSC-complex biology.
Prior expectation: Curated signaling places the TSC1–TSC2 complex upstream of RHEB and mTORC1 ([Reactome mTORC1 signaling](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-166208)); enrichment of the observed gene set for synaptic compartments is consistent with, but does not mechanistically explain, the branch ([STRING](https://string-db.org/)). Established biology would therefore predict shared TSC-complex effects more readily than an isolated TSC2 output.
Support: In 308 CB GABA, Tsc2 perturbation increased Shank3 (log2FC +1.108, FDR 0.00171), Cacna1d (+0.862, FDR 0.0204), Stxbp1 (+0.604, FDR 0.0204), Grm8 (+1.406, FDR 0.0280), Cabp1 (+1.342, FDR 0.0280), and Slc8a3 (+1.187, FDR 0.0419). The Tsc2 self row was only -0.277 with FDR 0.811. Tsc1 perturbation produced no FDR genes in the class, and its Shank3, Cacna1d, Stxbp1, and Cabp1 rows all had FDR 1.0; Mtor perturbation likewise did not reproduce them. Those nulls are not matched protein-level loss or kinase-output controls, so guide efficacy, mosaic editing, and secondary neuronal-state changes remain strong alternatives; mTOR activity, calcium dynamics, and synaptic physiology were not measured.
Research community: Tuberous-sclerosis, cerebellar-circuit, mTOR, and synaptic-calcium communities would be most interested. The decisive experiment is matched TSC1, TSC2, and MTOR editing with protein and phospho-S6 calibration in identified cerebellar GABA neurons, followed by calcium imaging, electrophysiology, and TSC2-domain rescue. Confirmation would support a focused program on mTOR-dependent versus TSC2-specific control of inhibitory cerebellar synapses.

Title: KMT2B perturbation produces a mostly repressive thalamic neuronal state with a smaller midbrain core
Core Idea: Observation: Kmt2b perturbation generated a 46-gene, almost entirely downregulated state in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons and a four-gene negative core in midbrain glutamatergic neurons. Interpretation: acute KMT2B loss may be especially consequential for maintenance of this neuronal expression state, but similar self-RNA shifts without downstream responses elsewhere do not prove intrinsic thalamic vulnerability.
Significance: The response links a dystonia-associated chromatin regulator to a concrete mature-neuronal maintenance program rather than only developmental gene regulation. If matched editing confirms the state dependence, it could support substantial work on which KMT2B-controlled genes create postnatal circuit vulnerability.
Prior expectation: KMT2B was already known as an H3K4 methyltransferase and dystonia gene ([UniProt](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9UMN6)); adult neuronal deletion affects plasticity and memory ([Kerimoglu et al. 2013](https://www.jneurosci.org/content/33/8/3452)), and KMT2B is required for neuronal maturation programs ([Barbagiovanni et al. 2018](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30355503/)). These priors predict neuronal transcriptional dependence but not the exact Stxbp2/Rasgrp2/Csf2ra/Fah state.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, 44 of 46 FDR genes decreased after Kmt2b perturbation: Stxbp2 log2FC -1.578 (FDR 9.53e-25), Rasgrp2 -1.659 (FDR 8.24e-15), Csf2ra -0.869 (FDR 3.25e-8), Adamts3 -0.800 (FDR 8.01e-8), Echdc2 -1.355 (FDR 4.53e-7), and Fah -0.690 (FDR 5.36e-7); Kmt2b itself fell -0.659 (FDR 1.72e-7). In 155 MB Glut, Ebf4 (-1.416, FDR 1.17e-6), Csf2ra (-0.903, FDR 1.16e-4), Fah (-1.017, FDR 0.00210), and Kmt2b (-0.584, FDR 0.0332) were the only FDR genes. Similar Kmt2b RNA trends in other contexts are not functional engagement controls, and the assay did not measure H3K4 methylation, occupancy, cell physiology, or survival; differential power and protein turnover remain alternatives to selective vulnerability.
Research community: KMT2B-dystonia, neuronal chromatin, thalamic-circuit, and epigenetic-therapy researchers would be most motivated. Matched Kmt2b editing across neuronal classes with protein depletion, H3K4me3 profiling, CUT&RUN, and rescue by catalytic versus scaffold mutants would test whether the state is directly chromatin-dependent. Validation would enable a cell-state-focused program connecting KMT2B target genes to dystonia circuit physiology.

Title: VPS35 perturbation remodels a thalamic synaptic-receptor and excitability transcript set
Core Idea: Observation: Vps35 perturbation in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons lowered Grin2b, Rims3, L1cam, and Kcnc2 while increasing Ache, without transcriptionally replacing core retromer, sorting-neixin, WASH, or TBC1D5 components. Research community: Retromer, Parkinson disease, endosomal-trafficking, and synaptic-excitability researchers would be the main audience. Title: GRIN1 perturbation induces a broad thalamic growth-factor and synaptic-remodeling state
Core Idea: Observation: Grin1 perturbation in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons increased Scg2, Stmn4, Kctd16, Gpm6a, Igf1, and Jun while lowering Erc2, Homer2, Kcnab2, Gabrd, and Prkcd. Research community: NMDAR, thalamic-circuit, homeostatic-plasticity, and neuropsychiatric communities would be most interested. Matched functional loss of GRIN1 and selected subunits with receptor-current measurements, calcium imaging, and GluN1 rescue would determine whether response magnitude follows total NMDAR loss rather than guide quality. Confirmation would support a comparative program on full-receptor versus subunit-selective homeostatic states.

Title: CELF2 perturbation collapses a thalamic CELF-family state and recurrently induces Snhg11
Core Idea: Observation: Celf2 perturbation lowered Celf1, Celf3, and Celf5 in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons and increased Snhg11 across most tested neuronal classes despite large differences in overall response breadth. Interpretation: CELF2 may maintain a local CELF-regulator network, while Snhg11 is a robust abundance reporter of its loss rather than an established downstream effector.

Prior expectation: CELF proteins were already known to regulate splicing, stability, and translation ([Dasgupta and Ladd 2012](https://doi.org/10.1002/wrna.107)), and CELF2 has documented roles in neural progenitor fate and neurodevelopmental disease ([MacPherson et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.celrep.2021.109226)). Those priors did not predict coordinated loss of other CELF-family transcripts or Snhg11 as a recurrent mature-neuronal reporter.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Celf2 perturbation produced 507 FDR genes: Snhg11 increased log2FC +0.411 (FDR 1.30e-52), while Celf3 fell -0.466 (FDR 3.79e-9), Celf5 -0.647 (FDR 9.42e-8), Celf1 -0.481 (FDR 4.13e-4), and Celf2 -0.185 (FDR 0.00472); Kcnc2 and Syt7 also fell (-0.505, FDR 2.05e-25; -0.641, FDR 3.76e-25) and Vamp1 rose (+0.791, FDR 9.03e-18). Snhg11 was significantly increased in 18 of 22 contexts, with significant log2FC +0.182 to +0.469. Smaller classes retained different outputs—for example 052 Pvalb Gaba increased Arhgef1 +0.941 (FDR 7.07e-16), while 155 MB Glut increased Tmem132a +0.849 (FDR 7.07e-12). Snhg11 may mark shared perturbation stress rather than mediate CELF2 biology, and total RNA cannot establish altered splicing or translation.
Research community: Neuronal RNA-processing, CELF-family, long-noncoding-RNA, and neurodevelopmental-disorder researchers would find the reporter useful. Long-read RNA-seq and CLIP after matched CELF2 loss, followed by CELF paralog and Snhg11 rescue or depletion, would distinguish direct network regulation from a correlated stress marker. Confirmation would support a bounded program using Snhg11 to track CELF2 dysfunction while identifying the consequential isoform changes.

Title: ZC3H14 perturbation recurrently induces Zcchc18 while lowering polyadenylation-factor transcripts
Core Idea: Observation: Zc3h14 perturbation increased Zcchc18 in thalamic and midbrain neurons and lowered Pabpn1, with Cstf2 repression joining in midbrain glutamatergic neurons. Interpretation: loss of the poly(A)-binding protein ZC3H14 produces a compact RNA-processing abundance state in which Zcchc18 is the most reproducible positive reporter.
Significance: The relationship gives the ZC3H14 intellectual-disability field a tractable mature-neuronal readout and nominates a specific response neighborhood. Its consequence is presently bounded because poly(A)-tail control, isoforms, and neuronal physiology were not measured.
Prior expectation: ZC3H14 loss was already linked to intellectual disability ([Pak et al. 2011](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21734151/)) and poly(A)-tail regulation ([Kelly et al. 2014](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24671764/)), with mouse brain phenotypes supporting neuronal relevance ([Rha et al. 2017](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28666327/)). That biology predicts RNA-processing defects but not Zcchc18 induction or the Pabpn1/Cstf2 combination.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Zc3h14 perturbation increased Zcchc18 (log2FC +0.515, FDR 7.98e-10) and Tra2a (+0.427, FDR 0.00402) and lowered Pabpn1 (-0.264, FDR 9.51e-4). In 155 MB Glut, Zc3h14 itself fell -0.739 (FDR 0.00543), Zcchc18 increased +0.558 (FDR 2.07e-6), and Pabpn1 and Cstf2 fell (-0.280, FDR 0.0141; -0.592, FDR 0.0283). Zcchc18 also increased in 191 MB P MY GABA (+0.566, FDR 0.0116). Only 155 had FDR-level self-RNA loss, but self RNA is not a functional engagement gate; unequal editing, power, and secondary stress still constrain conclusions about context breadth, and no poly(A)-tail measurement links these abundance changes mechanistically.
Research community: Polyadenylation, neuronal RNA-binding-protein, and intellectual-disability researchers would be the primary audience. Direct RNA sequencing with poly(A)-tail measurements, ZC3H14 CLIP, and rescue by RNA-binding-deficient versus wild-type ZC3H14 would test whether Pabpn1/Cstf2 and Zcchc18 are direct consequences of altered poly(A) control. A positive result would support a focused program on ZC3H14-dependent RNA maturation in mature neurons.

Title: QRICH1 perturbation recurrently lowers a Scn2b-Celf3-Ldhd module in thalamic and caudal neurons
Core Idea: Observation: Qrich1 perturbation strongly lowered Scn2b, Celf3, Ldhd, and Map1a in thalamic neurons, with a smaller Celf3/Ldhd/Tlcd3b core recurring in midbrain and posterior neuronal populations. Interpretation: QRICH1 supports a coherent ion-channel, RNA-binding, and metabolic expression module in selected mature neurons, although the molecular connection among those outputs is unknown.
Significance: The result turns a poorly characterized, broadly expressed factor into a concrete neuronal maintenance hypothesis. Its present impact is moderate: it provides a strong marker set for mechanistic work but does not yet identify a unifying biochemical function.
Prior expectation: QRICH1 was broadly expressed and nucleoplasmic rather than known for a restricted neuronal program ([Human Protein Atlas QRICH1](https://www.proteinatlas.org/ENSG00000198218-QRICH1)). The focused Scn2b/Celf3/Ldhd/Tlcd3b response was therefore not a straightforward consequence of prior annotation.
Support: Qrich1 perturbation produced 75 FDR genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, led by Scn2b (log2FC -1.277, FDR 6.86e-37), Celf3 (-1.353, FDR 1.24e-30), Ldhd (-1.981, FDR 3.67e-22), and Map1a (-0.686, FDR 2.58e-8). In 155 MB Glut, Scn2b fell -0.922 (FDR 9.15e-15), Celf3 -1.171 (FDR 7.20e-10), and Tlcd3b -0.864 (FDR 7.42e-7); 191 MB P MY GABA reproduced a narrower Celf3/Map1a/Ldhd core. Eleven tested contexts had no FDR downstream row, but those nulls do not establish intrinsic state restriction under unequal power and engagement. The assay provides no QRICH1 occupancy, protein-interaction, or functional evidence linking the ion-channel, RNA, and metabolic components.
Research community: Rare-disease genetics, neuronal transcription, sodium-channel, and RNA-metabolism researchers would find the module actionable. Endogenous-tag proteomics and chromatin/RNA occupancy assays after QRICH1 loss, followed by rescue and electrophysiology, would determine whether the genes form one direct module or several secondary responses. Confirmation would support a proportionate program defining QRICH1's molecular role and its most informative neuronal biomarkers.

Title: MARCHF6 and TXNDC15 perturbations converge on a narrow neuronal SREBP2 sterol-feedback module
Core Idea: Observation: Marchf6 and Txndc15 perturbations both induced Insig1, Hmgcs1, Ldlr, Srebf2, Fdft1, and Dhcr24 in responsive thalamic, midbrain, or posterior neurons, whereas Amfr supplied only nominal opposite-skew evidence. Interpretation: two ER-associated factors may converge on neuronal sterol feedback, with MARCHF6 producing the broader response and TXNDC15 a narrower pathway-level phenocopy.
Significance: The result extends an established MARCHF6 sterol-control line by nominating TXNDC15 as a neuronal entry point into the same feedback module. It is likely to deepen an existing sterol-homeostasis program rather than open an entirely new field.
Prior expectation: MARCHF6 was already known to control SQLE, HMGCR, CYP51A1, and DHCR24 ([Zelcer et al. 2014](https://doi.org/10.1128/MCB.01140-13); [Scott et al. 2020](https://doi.org/10.1042/BCJ20190647)), and SREBP–SCAP feedback was established ([Brown and Goldstein 2009](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18974038/)). The unanticipated element is TXNDC15's signed convergence in mature neurons, not MARCHF6-dependent sterol feedback itself.
Support: Marchf6 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut increased Insig1 (log2FC +1.002, FDR 1.03e-7), Dhcr24 (+0.880, FDR 7.54e-7), and Cyp51 (+0.728, FDR 9.42e-7), while Marchf6 fell -0.688 (FDR 1.33e-6). In 155 MB Glut, Txndc15 fell -1.070 (FDR 1.89e-17) and sterol genes increased, including Insig1 +1.384 (FDR 2.56e-8), Msmo1 +1.286 (FDR 4.76e-7), and Ldlr +1.870 (FDR 1.37e-6); Txndc15 also fell in 191 MB P MY GABA (-1.100, FDR 3.63e-7) with a weaker Insig1-led response. Whole-transcriptome correlations between Txndc15, Marchf6, and Srebf2 were weak, so the evidence is pathway-level rather than a shared global state. Sterol abundance and flux were not measured, and Amfr's proposed opposite branch is only nominal.
Research community: ER quality-control, cholesterol-homeostasis, and neuronal-lipid researchers would be the main community. Matched MARCHF6 and TXNDC15 editing with SREBP2 cleavage, sterol lipidomics, isotope flux, and double-perturbation epistasis would determine whether TXNDC15 enters the same biochemical feedback loop. Confirmation would support a focused extension of neuronal sterol-control work around TXNDC15.

Title: XPO1 targeting produces an RNA-gain state with Arrdc3-Slc16a6 induction and RNA-processing repression
Core Idea: Observation: Xpo1 targeting increased Xpo1 RNA in the strongest responsive neurons, induced Arrdc3, Slc16a6, Vamp2, and Sun2, and lowered Snrnp70, Arglu1, Pabpn1, and Srsf-family transcripts. Interpretation: this is an Xpo1-gain-associated neuronal state that is directionally different from the expected effects of XPO1 inhibition, not evidence that the guides phenocopied selinexor.
Significance: The finding is useful because it separates target direction from target identity: XPO1 gain and pharmacological inhibition should not be treated as interchangeable perturbations. It provides a defined neuronal gain-state signature for moderate follow-up on nuclear-export dosage.
Prior expectation: XPO1/CRM1 was already known as the Ran-dependent nuclear export receptor and the target of selinexor ([ChEMBL Exportin-1 records](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chembl/); [XPO1 inhibition study](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0093709)). Prior inhibitor biology would predict altered cargo localization, not necessarily this Xpo1-transcript gain with Arrdc3/Slc16a6 induction.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Xpo1 targeting lowered Snrnp70 (log2FC -0.896, FDR 1.65e-21) and induced Arrdc3 (+2.103, FDR 8.36e-18), Slc16a6 (+1.516, FDR 7.72e-15), and Xpo1 itself (+0.520, FDR 3.76e-5). In 155 MB Glut, Vamp2 increased +0.759 (FDR 3.40e-10); in 191 MB P MY GABA, Sun2 increased +1.123 (FDR 4.10e-4); and in 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA, Arglu1 fell -0.481 (FDR 0.00451). A WNT/TGF-β/p53 sentinel panel produced no FDR rows across five responsive contexts, but that limited panel cannot exclude inhibitor-like protein relocalization. Two Arrdc3-only responses lacked detectable Xpo1 RNA gain, and RNA abundance does not measure XPO1 protein, nuclear export, or cargo localization.
Research community: Nuclear-transport, RNA-processing, and XPO1 pharmacology researchers would be most interested. The decisive experiment is to compare validated XPO1 overexpression, loss, and selinexor exposure in the same neurons using live export reporters, nuclear/cytoplasmic proteomics, and the Arrdc3/Slc16a6 signature. Confirmation would support a bounded program on dosage-direction effects and neuronal liabilities of XPO1 modulation.

Title: NALCN perturbation lowers a compact Opn3-Slc6a17-Tspan33 module in thalamic neurons
Core Idea: Observation: Nalcn perturbation lowered Opn3, Slc6a17, and Tspan33 together with Nalcn in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons, whereas these markers did not respond in midbrain glutamatergic neurons despite Nalcn RNA loss. Interpretation: the three-gene set is a candidate state-dependent readout of NALCN disruption, not yet a resolved ionic or transcriptional mechanism.
Significance: The compact marker set could make NALCN dosage effects experimentally tractable in a defined neuronal population. Its modest breadth and unknown function keep it as a focused follow-up rather than a field-changing program.
Prior expectation: NALCN was already established as a neuronal sodium-leak channel relevant to resting excitability ([mouse NALCN annotation](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8BXR5); [NALCN channel discovery](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17448995/); [NALCN disease biology](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24904279/)). That function makes downstream state changes plausible but does not predict the Opn3/Slc6a17/Tspan33 combination.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Nalcn perturbation lowered Nalcn (log2FC -0.897, FDR 1.19e-9), Opn3 (-0.753, FDR 1.17e-5), Slc6a17 (-0.218, FDR 0.0288), and Tspan33 (-0.370, FDR 0.0288). In 155 MB Glut, Nalcn fell even more strongly (-0.955, FDR 7.05e-7), but Opn3 (+0.071), Slc6a17 (-0.076), and Tspan33 (-0.285) all had FDR 1.0. That matched RNA contrast strengthens state dependence but does not establish equivalent functional channel loss; protein turnover, guide outcomes, neuronal activity, and effective power could differ. Leak current, membrane potential, and direct transcriptional control were not measured.
Research community: NALCN channel, thalamic-excitability, and channelopathy researchers would be the most relevant community. Matched protein depletion and whole-cell recordings in thalamic and midbrain neurons, followed by electrical rescue or NALCN re-expression and marker measurement, would test whether the module follows loss of leak conductance. Confirmation would support a small program using the markers to connect NALCN dosage to neuronal state.

Title: ATXN1 and CIC perturbations converge almost perfectly on a thalamic mixed-direction state
Core Idea: Observation: Atxn1 perturbation produced a 187-gene thalamic response, and Cic was its closest non-self perturbation neighbor, with every shared FDR gene changing in the same direction. Interpretation: the atlas recovers an adult-neuronal ATXN1–CIC complex output and supplies a compact reporter set, while leaving direct CIC occupancy and complex disruption unmeasured.
Significance: The relationship is strongly precedented but unusually clean in this assay, making it valuable for validating and dissecting an established disease complex in mature thalamic neurons. It is more likely to refine SCA1 and CIC biology than to launch an unrelated new field.
Prior expectation: Disruption of the ATXN1–CIC complex was already known to cause neurobehavioral phenotypes ([Lu et al. 2017](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28288114/)) and to contribute substantially to SCA1 pathology ([Rousseaux et al. 2018](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29526553/)). Strong convergence was therefore expected; the atlas adds a cell-resolved adult reporter state.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Atxn1 perturbation increased Dclk1 (log2FC +0.601, FDR 2.93e-12), Aldh1l2 (+4.440, FDR 4.25e-8), and Ankrd55 (+1.459, FDR 2.59e-7), while lowering Lrrc7 (-0.274, FDR 7.19e-11), Trim9 (-0.362, FDR 1.83e-7), and Grin2c (-0.281, FDR 0.00409). Across 17,259 genes, Cic was the closest non-Atxn1 neighbor at Pearson r=0.543, and all 130 genes significant under both perturbations agreed in sign. Atxn1 self RNA did not reach FDR significance, but self RNA is not an engagement gate; the main remaining alternatives are shared downstream state or perturbation artifacts rather than demonstrated loss of the protein complex. CIC occupancy, ATXN1–CIC binding, and neuronal physiology were not measured.
Research community: SCA1, CIC transcription, and thalamic-circuit researchers would find the state immediately useful. CIC CUT&RUN and ATXN1–CIC proteomics after validated perturbation, combined with complex-preserving and complex-disrupting rescue alleles, would determine which reporter genes require the physical complex. Confirmation would support a focused program using this neuronal state to separate complex-dependent disease mechanisms from other ATXN1 functions.

Title: GFPT1 perturbation couples Ogt repression to ER and integrated-stress induction
Core Idea: Observation: Gfpt1 perturbation repeatedly lowered Ogt and induced Ddit3, Chac1, Asns, Hspa5, and other ER or integrated-stress transcripts in responsive neurons. Interpretation: reduced hexosamine-pathway input may create a neuronal UDP-GlcNAc liability that engages both O-GlcNAc and ER-folding responses, although metabolites and glycosylation were not measured.
Significance: The response supplies a coherent neuronal readout of hexosamine-pathway insufficiency and connects two established downstream arms. It is a useful extension of existing HBP–ER-stress biology, with moderate priority for defining which glycosylation deficit drives the state.
Prior expectation: GFPT1 was already the rate-limiting entry into UDP-GlcNAc synthesis ([Reactome HBP](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-446210)), and HBP deficiency had precedent for causing ER stress ([Wang et al. 2014](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3959665/)). The atlas refines that expectation by resolving recurrent Ogt repression and neuronal context.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Gfpt1 perturbation produced 311 FDR genes, including Ogt log2FC -0.960 (FDR 8.10e-17), Ddit3 +1.897 (FDR 2.94e-12), and Chac1 +3.077 (FDR 1.48e-9). In 145 MH-LH TH Glut, Asns increased +1.492 (FDR 3.39e-6); in 155 MB Glut, Hspa5 increased +1.422 (FDR 2.56e-5); and in 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, Ogt fell -0.552 (FDR 0.00665). Several cited cortical or hippocampal contexts had zero FDR genes, but those nulls cannot establish buffering under unequal power and engagement. UDP-GlcNAc, O-GlcNAcylation, N-glycosylation, ER function, and cell viability were not measured, so generic nutrient or integrated stress remains an alternative to the proposed coupling.
Research community: Hexosamine-pathway, O-GlcNAc, ER-stress, and neuromuscular/neuronal-metabolism researchers would be the main audience. Metabolomics and glycoproteomics after matched GFPT1 loss, with GlcNAc supplementation and selective OGT or N-glycosylation rescue, would distinguish the causal arm. Confirmation would support a focused program on neuronal thresholds for UDP-GlcNAc insufficiency.

Title: TUBA1A perturbation induces asymmetric beta-tubulin and TUBA4A compensation
Core Idea: Observation: Tuba1a loss induced Tubb3, Tubb2a, Tuba4a, Tubb4a, Tubb4b, and Tubb5 in thalamic neurons, with Tubb2a/Tuba4a/Tubb3 induction recurring in Pvalb neurons; direct Actg-style reciprocity was absent, while tubulin-folding cofactors induced Tuba1a. Interpretation: neuronal tubulin homeostasis is directionally asymmetric across isotypes and folding machinery.
Significance: The response gives a cell-resolved view of established tubulin autoregulation and nonredundancy and could help interpret tubulinopathy compensation. Its likely contribution is refinement of an active field rather than a wholly new mechanism.
Prior expectation: Tubulin mRNA autoregulation was already mechanistically established ([Lin et al. 2020](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aaz4352)), tubulin isotypes were known to have specialized functions ([Janke and Magiera 2020](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41580-020-0214-3)), and TBCD/TBCE were placed in post-chaperonin heterodimer folding ([Reactome](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-389977)). The atlas adds neuronal isotype direction and context.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Tuba1a perturbation lowered Tuba1a (log2FC -0.967, FDR 2.78e-20) and increased Tubb3 (+0.576, FDR 6.37e-9), Tubb2a (+0.523, FDR 2.68e-7), and Tuba4a (+0.696, FDR 1.14e-5). In 052 Pvalb Gaba, Tubb2a increased +0.614 (FDR 7.58e-4) alongside the same directional isotype response. Conversely, Tbcd perturbation increased Tuba1a in 155 MB Glut (+0.931, FDR 3.78e-17), and Tbce perturbation increased it in 151 (+0.630, FDR 2.51e-7). Direct Tubb2a perturbation produced a much narrower response and should not be interpreted as a symmetric inverse. The assay measures mRNA, not soluble tubulin, microtubule polymer, autoregulatory decay, or neuronal morphology.
Research community: Tubulin-autoregulation, microtubule, axonal-transport, and tubulinopathy researchers would find the asymmetric map useful. Isotype-specific protein and nascent-RNA measurements with TTC5 perturbation, microtubule imaging, and reciprocal isotype rescue would determine whether the responses are canonical autoregulation or cell-state-specific compensation. Confirmation would support a focused neuronal tubulin-homeostasis program.

Title: CHD8 perturbation produces a recurrent caudal Pnisr-Usp11-Dus3l repression state
Core Idea: Observation: Chd8 perturbation lowered Pnisr, Usp11, and Dus3l and increased Csf2ra and Aaas most strongly in thalamic neurons, with the negative core recurring in midbrain glutamatergic and posterior GABAergic neurons. Interpretation: acute postnatal CHD8 loss reveals a caudal RNA-abundance state distinct from the developmental cortical programs emphasized in prior work.
Significance: The finding refines an important autism gene by identifying a reproducible mature-neuronal output outside the usual developmental framing. Because no splice-junction or direct chromatin evidence is available, it is a moderate-priority extension rather than a new CHD8 mechanism.
Prior expectation: CHD8 haploinsufficiency was already strongly linked to autism ([Bernier et al. 2014](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24998929/)), causal mouse phenotypes ([Katayama et al. 2016](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27602517/)), and neurodevelopmental transcriptional networks ([Cotney et al. 2015](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25752243/)). Those priors focused on developmental chromatin regulation, not the recurrent mature caudal Pnisr/Usp11/Dus3l state.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Chd8 perturbation lowered Pnisr (log2FC -0.594, FDR 1.30e-34), Chd8 (-0.532, FDR 1.02e-9), Usp11 (-1.125, FDR 5.15e-7), and Dus3l (-0.466, FDR 3.36e-6), while increasing Csf2ra (+0.666, FDR 1.25e-6) and Aaas (+0.805, FDR 0.00132). Chd8 also fell in 191 MB P MY GABA (-0.546, FDR 4.13e-4) and 155 MB Glut (-0.385, FDR 0.00821), where the Pnisr/Usp11/Dus3l-down core recurred at smaller breadth. Response magnitude covaried partly with engagement and cell counts, and the RNA-abundance pattern does not demonstrate altered splicing, direct CHD8 occupancy, or a caudal-specific intrinsic dependency.
Research community: CHD8/autism, neuronal chromatin, and RNA-processing researchers would be the primary audience. CHD8 CUT&RUN and nascent/long-read RNA profiling after matched acute loss in cortical and caudal neurons would distinguish direct transcriptional regulation from secondary RNA-processing stress. Confirmation would support a bounded postnatal CHD8 program complementary to developmental disease models.

Title: ATP1A3 perturbation induces ion-pump and synaptic remodeling distinct from ATP2A2 ER stress
Core Idea: Observation: Atp1a3 perturbation in thalamic neurons lowered Atp2a2, Atp2b1, Prkcg, Kcnj9, and Kcnj3 while increasing Ache, Vgf, Ptpn5, Pcsk1n, Grin2a, and Slc17a6; direct Atp2a2 perturbation instead induced Xbp1, Hspa5, Ddit3, Chac1, Cars, and Asns. Interpretation: loss of the neuronal Na+/K+-pump α3 subunit and loss of SERCA2 produce separable homeostatic states rather than a single ion-pump stress response.
Significance: The signed contrast helps distinguish ATP1A3 channelopathy-related adaptation from calcium-store ER stress and provides concrete markers for functional studies. It is a strong validation and refinement of known pump nonredundancy, with moderate rather than agenda-setting impact.
Prior expectation: ATP1A3 was already known as a neuron-enriched α3 rescue pump with nonredundant sodium-clearance kinetics ([Azarias et al. 2013](https://doi.org/10.1074/jbc.M112.425785)) and a broad neurological disease spectrum ([Salles et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.3389/fneur.2021.637890)). Those priors predict a distinct ATP1A3 phenotype but not the exact divergence from ATP2A2 at this transcript endpoint.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Atp1a3 perturbation strongly lowered Atp1a3 (log2FC -1.674, FDR 5.70e-63), Atp2a2 (-0.409, FDR 1.33e-14), and Prkcg (-0.407, FDR 9.48e-9), while increasing Ache (+0.586, FDR 1.96e-12). Direct Atp2a2 perturbation lowered Atp2a2 -1.256 (FDR 1.78e-19) but increased Cars (+0.916, FDR 1.12e-9), Ddit3 (+1.604, FDR 2.06e-8), Chac1 (+3.014, FDR 6.61e-8), Xbp1 (+0.785, FDR 3.00e-7), and Hspa5 (+0.749, FDR 1.95e-6). Atp1a2 was a weak neuronal comparator, so the data do not exhaust Na+/K+-pump isoform redundancy. Pump activity, sodium, membrane potential, ER calcium, and firing were not measured, leaving differential ionic stress versus secondary transcription as alternatives.
Research community: ATP1A3 channelopathy, ion-homeostasis, ER-stress, and thalamic-circuit researchers would find the contrast directly useful. Side-by-side pump-current, sodium, calcium, and membrane-potential measurements after matched ATP1A3 and ATP2A2 loss, with isoform-specific rescue, would test whether the RNA states follow distinct ionic defects. Confirmation would support a focused physiology-to-transcription program for ATP1A3 disorders.

Title: KMT2A perturbation reveals a mature thalamic Nr2f1-Pcdh7-Lhx9 maintenance state
Core Idea: Observation: Kmt2a perturbation lowered Nr2f1, Pcdh7, Lhx9, Camk2d, Pcdh10, and Grik2 and increased Igfbp5 and Cx3cl1 in thalamic neurons, while other neuronal classes showed only singletons or a tiny midbrain branch. Prior expectation: Neuronal KMT2A was already required for prefrontal plasticity and working memory ([Jakovcevski et al. 2015](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25834037/)), postnatal neural-stem-cell neurogenesis and Dlx2 activation ([Lim et al. 2009](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature07726)), and human KMT2A mutations cause Wiedemann-Steiner syndrome ([Jones et al. 2012](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22795537/)). The atlas adds a postmitotic thalamic output distinct from those known settings.

In 155 MB Glut, Fnbp1l (-0.749, FDR 0.0214) and Kmt2a were the only FDR rows, and three additional glutamatergic classes yielded isolated Ptprk, Aff4, or Nap1l2 hits. Las1l did not phenocopy Kmt2a, but most other 5FMC components were not perturbed, so complex-level exclusion is incomplete. KMT2A protein, H3K4 methylation, occupancy, and neuronal function were not measured.
Research community: KMT2A/Wiedemann-Steiner, neuronal chromatin, and thalamic-development researchers would be most interested. Matched acute KMT2A loss with CUT&RUN, H3K4 profiling, nascent RNA, and rescue by catalytic versus scaffold mutants would distinguish direct mature-neuronal targets from secondary state change. Confirmation would support a focused program on postmitotic KMT2A maintenance rather than broad developmental reanalysis.

Title: YLPM1 perturbation lowers Srsf6-Srsf5 and Inha in midbrain-linked neurons
Core Idea: Observation: Ylpm1 RNA was reduced across several neuronal classes, but midbrain glutamatergic neurons additionally lowered Srsf6, Srsf5, and Inha, with Inha recurring in posterior GABAergic neurons. Interpretation: YLPM1 loss may stress nuclear-speckle or post-transcriptional machinery in these neurons, although the branch is primarily supported by one high-yield midbrain context.
Significance: The result supplies a concrete neuronal output for a poorly characterized nuclear protein and a small set of markers for mechanistic follow-up. It is likely to extend RNA-processing work rather than establish a broad new disease program.
Prior expectation: YLPM1 was annotated as a nuclear protein associated with speckle-related compartments ([UniProt YLPM1](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q9R0I7); [Human Protein Atlas](https://www.proteinatlas.org/ENSG00000119596-YLPM1)), and nuclear speckles are defined by RNA-processing factors ([QuickGO GO:0016607](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/QuickGO/term/GO:0016607)). These priors make SRSF changes plausible but do not predict their neuronal context.
Support: In 155 MB Glut, Ylpm1 perturbation lowered Ylpm1 (log2FC -0.652, FDR 6.93e-4), Srsf6 (-0.610, FDR 9.35e-4), Srsf5 (-0.440, FDR 0.00429), and Inha (-0.501, FDR 0.0232), while increasing Shprh (+0.787, FDR 9.35e-4), Jakmip2 (+0.875, FDR 0.0231), and Atxn2l (+0.362, FDR 0.0232). In 191 MB P MY GABA, Ylpm1 and Inha both fell about -0.833 (FDR 5.92e-4). Ylpm1 also fell in 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA (-1.173, FDR 7.09e-4) without a broad companion program. The latter null does not prove state-selective coupling because effective power and protein loss were unmeasured; speckle morphology, splice isoforms, and direct YLPM1 interactions were not assayed.
Research community: Nuclear-speckle, splice-regulator, and YLPM1 rare-disease researchers would be the relevant audience. Imaging speckle organization and long-read RNA after matched YLPM1 protein depletion in midbrain and comparator neurons, followed by YLPM1 rescue, would test whether the SRSF changes report genuine speckle dysfunction. Confirmation would support a bounded mechanistic study of YLPM1-dependent RNA processing.

Rank: 52
Novelty Score: 3
Support Score: 4
Priority Score: 3
Title: BMAL1-CLOCK and REV-ERB perturbations recover an asymmetric neuronal circadian feedback loop
Core Idea: Observation: Bmal1 and Clock perturbations lowered Dbp, Nr1d2, and related clock outputs, whereas Nr1d1 or Nr1d2 perturbations induced portions of the positive clock limb and a synaptic branch in thalamic neurons. Interpretation: the atlas recovers expected circadian feedback but shows asymmetric paralog and endpoint behavior that should be treated as a single-timepoint basal state, not a phase-resolved oscillator.
Significance: The finding validates that pooled postnatal perturbation can recover a neuronal clock circuit and identifies a tractable thalamic synaptic output. Its priority is moderate because the central feedback logic is established and the experiment lacks time resolution.
Prior expectation: CLOCK:BMAL1 activation of core clock genes and the PER/CRY and REV-ERB feedback loops are established ([Reactome circadian clock](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-9909396); [Trott and Menet 2018](https://journals.plos.org/plosgenetics/article?id=10.1371/journal.pgen.1007156)), and REV-ERB paralogs are known to coordinate circadian output ([Ikeda et al. 2019](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6629614/)). The atlas primarily adds neuronal context and signed asymmetry.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Bmal1 perturbation lowered Dbp (log2FC -1.601, FDR 3.07e-22), Nr1d2 (-1.083, FDR 1.19e-18), and Nr1d1 (-1.074, FDR 4.70e-18), while increasing Erc2 (+0.628, FDR 1.32e-12); 103 genes passed FDR<0.05. Clock perturbation in the same class lowered Dbp -1.030 (FDR 1.36e-11) and Nr1d2 -0.378 (FDR 2.25e-4), and in 155 MB Glut lowered Nr1d2 -0.833 (FDR 1.30e-13) and Dbp -1.290 (FDR 2.05e-6). Nr1d2 perturbation increased Nr1d1 +0.520 in 151 (FDR 1.44e-5), while Nr1d1 perturbation increased Dbp +0.407 (FDR 0.00243) and generated a 116-gene branch. A single harvest time can invert or attenuate feedback depending on phase, and neither circadian period nor rhythmic amplitude was measured.
Research community: Circadian-neurobiology and thalamic-circuit researchers would use the result mainly as a starting map. Time-resolved perturbation across circadian phase, with bioluminescent clock reporters and electrophysiology, would distinguish altered oscillator dynamics from static phase displacement. Confirmation would support a focused program on how the neuronal clock gates synaptic-state genes in defined thalamic cells.

Novelty Score: 3

Priority Score: 3
Title: HSPA8 perturbation induces a compact HSP70-HSP90 compensatory transcript arm
Core Idea: Observation: Hspa8 perturbation increased Dnaja1, Hspa4l, Hsp90aa1, and Cct6a in thalamic neurons, with Dnaja1 or Hsp90aa1 induction recurring in cortical and hypothalamic classes. Interpretation: loss of constitutive HSPA8 releases a compensatory folding response, although correlation with proteasome perturbations does not prove altered folding flux.
Significance: The response gives a concise neuronal measure of HSPA8 insufficiency and supports comparison of constitutive versus inducible chaperone networks. It is a well-grounded extension of established feedback biology with moderate experimental value.
Prior expectation: HSPA8 was established as a constitutive HSP70 chaperone ([UniProt HSPA8](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P11142)), DNAJ proteins as HSP70 specificity factors ([Kampinga and Craig 2010](https://doi.org/10.1038/nrm2941)), and HSP70–HSF1 negative feedback as a mechanism for compensatory chaperone induction ([Krakowiak et al. 2018](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.31668)). The observed arm therefore has strong precedent.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Hspa8 perturbation increased Dnaja1 (log2FC +0.496, FDR 7.23e-4), Hspa4l (+0.394, FDR 7.23e-4), Hsp90aa1 (+0.383, FDR 0.00252), and Cct6a (+0.700, FDR 0.0203). Dnaja1 also increased in 009 L2-3 IT PIR AON ENT Glut (+0.581, FDR 0.0196), and Hsp90aa1 increased in 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA (+0.720, FDR 0.0198). Nominal positive calls for Hsph1, Dnaja1, Dnajb1, Hsp90aa1, Stip1, and Hspa4l recurred across the 21 tested classes. Protein folding, HSF1 activity, aggregation, and proteasome flux were not measured, so the interpretation remains an abundance-level compensatory response rather than demonstrated proteostasis recovery.
Research community: Neuronal proteostasis, HSP70/HSP90, and chaperone-therapy researchers would find the marker arm useful. HSF1 reporter assays, folding sensors, proteomics, and wild-type versus ATPase-deficient HSPA8 rescue would determine whether transcript induction restores functional proteostasis. Confirmation would support a focused neuronal chaperone-feedback study rather than a broad new program.

Title: PPP3CA and PPP3R1 perturbations expose a noncanonical calcineurin transcript output opposite AP-2 adaptor states
Core Idea: Observation: Ppp3ca and Ppp3r1 perturbations produced compact, partially overlapping thalamic responses centered on Pcsk1n and selected calcium, synaptic, and receptor genes, while AP-2 adaptor perturbations moved the restricted Ppp3r1 signature oppositely. Interpretation: calcineurin loss is captured here by an endocytic and excitability-related RNA module rather than a canonical NFAT/RCAN abundance reporter.
Significance: The response suggests a tractable transcriptional interface between calcineurin and synaptic endocytosis, but it does not yet establish phosphatase-to-AP-2 epistasis. It is a moderate-priority refinement of well-known calcineurin biology.
Prior expectation: Calcineurin was already established as a calcium-dependent phosphatase that activates NFAT ([Reactome](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-2025928)) and dephosphorylates endocytic proteins during synaptic-vesicle recycling ([Cousin and Robinson 2001](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11672811/)). Those priors make both NFAT and endocytic outputs plausible; the atlas instead emphasizes a compact non-NFAT RNA state.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Ppp3ca perturbation lowered Ppp3ca (log2FC -0.483, FDR 6.35e-12) and Iyd (-1.085, FDR 0.0112) while changing Ptpn3 (-0.224, FDR 0.0239) and Pik3r1 (+0.488, FDR 0.0239). Ppp3r1 perturbation lowered Ppp3r1 (-0.703, FDR 1.89e-5), Atp2a2 (-0.327, FDR 8.08e-5), Coro6 (-0.472, FDR 5.99e-4), and Slc6a17 (-0.286, FDR 0.0496), while increasing Pcsk1n (+0.710, FDR 2.21e-7) and Ptprs (+0.356, FDR 4.87e-7). Ap2s1 and Ap2m1 were the strongest negative signature neighbors; for example, Ap2s1 perturbation increased Atp2a2 and Slc6a17 while lowering Ptprs and Ndrg2. That anticorrelation is not double-perturbation epistasis, and NFAT localization, phosphatase activity, endocytosis, and calcium were not measured.
Research community: Calcineurin, synaptic-vesicle endocytosis, and calcium-signaling researchers would be most interested. Matched calcineurin-subunit and AP-2 perturbations with phosphatase assays, endocytic imaging, NFAT localization, and double-perturbation rescue would distinguish a causal countermodule from correlated neuronal states. Confirmation would support a focused study of calcineurin–endocytosis coupling in mature thalamic neurons.

Title: ACTB perturbation recurrently induces ACTG1 and FLNA without reciprocal ACTG1 compensation
Core Idea: Observation: Actb perturbation increased Actg1 and Flna across multiple neuronal classes, whereas Actg1 perturbation did not produce a broad reciprocal Actb-like state and yielded only a narrow Flna response. Interpretation: cytoplasmic actin compensation is directionally asymmetric and includes a filamin-associated remodeling arm.
Significance: The pattern gives neuronal context to established β- versus γ-actin nonredundancy and could help interpret cytoskeletal disease alleles. It is robust but closely aligned with known actin biology, so follow-up should remain proportionate.
Prior expectation: β- and γ-cytoplasmic actins were already known to encode nearly identical proteins with nonredundant gene-level functions ([Vedula et al. 2017](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29244021/); [Patrinostro et al. 2018](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30012594/)), and ACTB/ACTG1 variants cause a shared developmental syndrome ([Rivière et al. 2012](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22366783/)). Asymmetric compensation was therefore plausible.
Support: After Actb perturbation, Actg1 increased in 9 of 22 contexts and Flna in 13 of 22 at FDR<0.05. In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Actg1 rose log2FC +0.695 (FDR 1.32e-16) and Flna +0.802 (FDR 2.54e-13); in 027 NP-CT-L6b-OB Glut, Actg1 rose +0.651 (FDR 0.00376) and Flna +1.415 (FDR 7.93e-15); in 155 MB Glut, the changes were +0.869 (FDR 3.33e-19) and +0.669 (FDR 0.0212). Direct Actg1 perturbation strongly lowered Actg1 in 151 (-0.824, FDR 9.85e-16) but did not significantly induce Flna there; its only non-self FDR hit was Flna in 027 (+0.779, FDR 0.0132). Actb itself was absent from response rows, and mRNA does not measure actin protein stoichiometry, polymerization, or morphology.
Research community: Actin-autoregulation, neuronal-cytoskeleton, migration, and Baraitser-Winter researchers would find the asymmetry useful. Isotype-specific protein quantification, polymerization imaging, and reciprocal coding-sequence/UTR rescue after defined Actb and Actg1 alleles would distinguish locus regulation from protein demand. Confirmation would support a focused neuronal study of actin-isotype compensation.

Title: CAML perturbation lowers receptor, ion-pump, and synaptic-vesicle transcripts
Core Idea: Observation: Caml perturbation lowered receptor and membrane genes in thalamic neurons and vesicle, chloride-transporter, ion-pump, and receptor genes in midbrain glutamatergic neurons. Interpretation: loss of the WRB–CAML tail-anchored-protein insertion machinery creates a neuronal membrane-protein liability reflected at the RNA level, although insertion and localization were not measured.
Significance: The response extends established ER insertion biology into mature neuronal receptor and vesicle maintenance and supplies concrete readouts for mechanistic work. Its expected contribution is moderate because the pathway itself is known and the transcript changes could be secondary compensation.
Prior expectation: CAML/CAMLG was already part of the mammalian WRB receptor for ER insertion of tail-anchored proteins ([Yamamoto and Sakisaka 2012](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23041287/); [Vilardi et al. 2014](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24392163/); [Reactome WRB–CAMLG docking](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-9609899)). A membrane-protein response was plausible, but the neuronal receptor and vesicle transcript set was not directly predicted.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Caml perturbation lowered Lingo1 (log2FC -0.327, FDR 3.88e-4), Gabrd (-0.281, FDR 0.00239), Gabra1 (-0.286, FDR 0.0134), Syp (-0.207, FDR 0.0154), and Caml (-0.375, FDR 0.0285); 28 genes passed FDR. In 155 MB Glut, it lowered Sv2a (-0.420, FDR 0.00303), Caml (-0.553, FDR 0.00336), Syp (-0.246, FDR 0.00669), Slc12a5 (-0.397, FDR 0.00849), Atp1a3 (-0.227, FDR 0.00857), and Gabbr1 (-0.295, FDR 0.0208); 18 genes passed FDR. Most other contexts had no corrected downstream response, but those nulls do not establish intrinsic restriction. Direct tail-anchor insertion, receptor surface abundance, ER stress, and synaptic function were not assayed.
Research community: Tail-anchored-protein insertion, synaptic-membrane trafficking, and neuronal proteostasis researchers would be the main audience. Surface proteomics and insertion reporters after matched CAML and WRB loss, with CAML rescue, would determine whether the RNA state follows failed insertion of specific neuronal cargos. Confirmation would support a focused program on how ER insertion capacity maintains synaptic membrane composition.

Title: SRSF1 perturbation rewires splice-regulator abundance instead of inducing SRSF2 compensation
Core Idea: Observation: Srsf1 perturbation increased Ptbp2, Rbm28, Dazap1, and Psip1 while lowering Srsf2, Hnrnpdl, Clk4, and Clk1 in responsive neurons. Interpretation: SRSF1 loss destabilizes a broader RNA-regulator network rather than eliciting simple SRSF2 or CLK-family transcriptional compensation.
Significance: The response identifies a concrete abundance-level network reaction to loss of a core SR protein and can guide isoform-focused experiments. It is a moderate-priority extension because direct splicing outcomes and functional compensation remain unknown.
Prior expectation: SRSF1 was already established as an essential SR-family splicing regulator ([UniProt SRSF1](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q6PDM2)), and interaction/enrichment resources place the response genes in a shared RNA-processing neighborhood ([STRING](https://string-db.org/)). The signed collapse of Srsf2 and CLK transcripts rather than compensatory induction was not guaranteed by those priors.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Srsf1 perturbation strongly lowered Srsf1 (log2FC -1.832, FDR 7.05e-65) and Srsf2 (-1.222, FDR 1.09e-44), while increasing Ptbp2 (+1.194, FDR 2.99e-72), Rbm28 (+1.248, FDR 4.09e-67), Dazap1 (+1.260, FDR 9.95e-65), and Psip1 (+0.999, FDR 4.94e-64). Related, uneven RNA-processing responses occurred in CA3/CA2/DG glutamatergic and hypothalamic or striatal GABAergic contexts. The very broad thalamic response may include secondary stress, and total RNA abundance neither measures splice isoforms nor demonstrates loss of functional SRSF2 compensation.
Research community: Spliceosome, SR-protein, neuronal RNA-processing, and RBP-disease researchers would be most interested. Long-read RNA-seq, phospho-SR proteomics, and SRSF2 or CLK rescue after matched SRSF1 depletion would distinguish failed compensation from downstream network remodeling. Confirmation would support a focused study of SR-network homeostasis in neurons.

Title: HSF1 perturbation lowers a basal HSPH1-HSP90 support module
Core Idea: Observation: Hsf1 perturbation lowered Hsph1, Hsp90aa1, Hsp90ab1, Ahsa2, Stip1, and Rsrp1 in selected neuronal classes. Interpretation: HSF1 maintains a compact constitutive chaperone support layer in mature neurons, distinct from the sparse response to direct Hsp90ab1 loss.
Significance: The result provides cell-resolved evidence for basal, not merely heat-induced, HSF1 control of neuronal chaperone abundance. It is a useful validation and refinement of established feedback biology with moderate follow-up value.
Prior expectation: HSF1 was already the central heat-shock transcription factor ([Reactome HSF1 activation](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-3371511); [Gomez-Pastor et al. 2018](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28852220/)), and HSP90 regulation was well characterized ([Prodromou 2016](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4980810/)). Basal maintenance of a compact chaperone module was therefore plausible.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Hsf1 perturbation lowered Hsph1 (log2FC -0.484, FDR 1.69e-8), Hsp90aa1 (-0.282, FDR 2.85e-4), Rsrp1 (-0.175, FDR 8.06e-4), Hsp90ab1 (-0.345, FDR 8.96e-4), Mat2a (-0.234, FDR 0.00737), and Ahsa2 (-0.294, FDR 0.00827). In 155 MB Glut, Hsph1 fell -0.599 (FDR 2.50e-4), Rsrp1 -0.161 (FDR 0.00169), and Stip1 -0.388 (FDR 0.00583); 052 Pvalb Gaba lowered Rsrp1 and Ahsa2, and 110 CNU-HYa HY MM Glut lowered Hsp90aa1 -0.439 (FDR 0.0275). Hsf1 self RNA never passed FDR despite predominantly negative trends, which is non-diagnostic for functional editing. Protein abundance, heat-shock inducibility, aggregation, and survival were not measured.
Research community: Heat-shock, neuronal proteostasis, and chaperone-therapy researchers would be the natural audience. Matched HSF1 depletion with chromatin occupancy, basal and induced chaperone proteomics, heat-stress survival, and HSF1 rescue would test direct maintenance of the module. Confirmation would support a focused study of basal HSF1 dependence across neuronal states.

Title: CTCF perturbation robustly represses clustered protocadherins in thalamic and midbrain neurons
Core Idea: Observation: Ctcf perturbation coordinately lowered multiple α-, β-, and γ-clustered protocadherins in thalamic and midbrain glutamatergic neurons, alongside a recurrent Zcwpw2-up and Dennd6b/Abhd14b-down branch. Interpretation: the atlas recovers established CTCF control of clustered protocadherin loci and adds a cell-resolved mature-neuronal abundance profile.
Significance: This is a strong positive control for relational recovery and a useful map of adult neuronal response, but the central CTCF–protocadherin relationship is already established. Follow-up should focus on the less-explained companion branch and cell-state consequences rather than present a new locus mechanism.
Prior expectation: Neuronal CTCF was already required for stochastic clustered-protocadherin expression, dendritic development, and synapse formation ([Hirayama et al. 2012](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22854024/)), and for experience-dependent neuronal gene regulation and chromatin structure ([Sams et al. 2016](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27880914/)). Coordinated Pcdh-cluster repression was therefore expected.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Ctcf perturbation lowered Pcdhb20 (log2FC -1.521, FDR 1.47e-7), Pcdhb17 (-1.618, FDR 2.53e-7), Pcdhb16 (-1.910, FDR 2.55e-7), Pcdhac1 (-1.386, FDR 3.28e-7), Pcdhb18 (-1.402, FDR 2.08e-5), and Pcdha10 (-1.106, FDR 1.00e-4). In 155 MB Glut, it lowered Pcdhgb2 (-2.114, FDR 4.17e-5), Pcdhga12 (-1.745, FDR 7.36e-5), Pcdhb20 (-1.933, FDR 7.36e-5), Pcdhb18 (-2.478, FDR 3.51e-5), and Pcdha10 (-1.726, FDR 2.82e-5). The same two classes also showed Zcwpw2 induction and Dennd6b/Abhd14b repression. The assay did not measure CTCF occupancy, chromatin loops, or single-cell protocadherin choice, and the companion genes could reflect a broader state change.
Research community: Chromatin-architecture, protocadherin, and neuronal-identity researchers would use this chiefly as an in vivo perturbation map. CTCF CUT&RUN, locus-conformation assays, and single-cell long-read protocadherin profiling after matched loss would verify whether abundance changes reflect altered promoter choice and looping. The proportionate follow-up is to define the unexplained Zcwpw2/Dennd6b/Abhd14b branch and its relationship to the known locus phenotype.
