Title: APC, CTNNB1, and TCF7L2 perturbations recover opposing Wnt-node polarity with neuronal-state outputs
Core Idea: Observation: Apc perturbation induced canonical Wnt feedback genes and lowered neuronal subtype markers, while Ctnnb1 and Tcf7l2 perturbations moved many shared genes in the opposite direction and added synaptic or excitability outputs in thalamic neurons. Interpretation: the atlas recovers expected APC–β-catenin–TCF polarity while showing that this established signaling node is embedded in a mature neuronal identity program.
Significance: The result is a strong biological validation and a useful source of neuronal Wnt readouts, but its core direction is well precedented. The most valuable follow-up is to separate canonical feedback from TCF7L2-dependent maintenance of thalamic excitability.
Prior expectation: APC was already the central β-catenin destruction-complex component ([Reactome APC signaling](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-4839744)), Axin2 and Nkd1 were established Wnt feedback targets ([Jho et al. 2002](https://doi.org/10.1128/MCB.22.4.1172-1183.2002); [Larraguibel et al. 2015](https://doi.org/10.1091/mbc.E14-12-1648)), and TCF7L2 was known to control postmitotic thalamic differentiation and excitability ([Lipiec et al. 2020](https://doi.org/10.1242/dev.190181)). Opposing pathway polarity was therefore expected.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Apc perturbation produced 303 FDR genes and increased Axin2 (log2FC +1.248, FDR 2.43e-16), Nkd1 (+2.455, FDR 2.14e-13), and Notum (+1.562, FDR 4.39e-9), while lowering Gls (-0.498, FDR 1.17e-12), Cck (-0.597, FDR 4.64e-8), Grin2c (-0.588, FDR 1.92e-8), and Chgb (-0.460, FDR 1.45e-6). Ctnnb1 perturbation produced 383 FDR genes, including Ctnnb1 -0.593 (FDR 1.83e-10), Ramp3 -1.367 (FDR 3.00e-21), Gabra4 -0.758 (FDR 7.85e-22), and Chgb +0.696 (FDR 1.76e-13). Tcf7l2 perturbation yielded 106 FDR genes: among the 29 genes shared with Apc, 27 changed oppositely; Cck rose +0.706 (FDR 3.47e-12) while Spock3 fell -0.690 (FDR 1.04e-11), opposite Apc. Tcf7l2 self RNA was not FDR-depleted, so protein loss is unverified; the endpoint also cannot distinguish direct TCF occupancy from secondary neuronal-state change.
Research community: Wnt-signaling, thalamic-development, and neuronal-excitability researchers would find the signed map useful. Matched APC, CTNNB1, and TCF7L2 perturbations with β-catenin activity reporters, TCF7L2 occupancy, and electrophysiology would separate canonical Wnt feedback from mature-neuronal identity control. Confirmation would support a focused program on how an established Wnt node maintains adult thalamic physiology.

Title: PTEN perturbation recovers the expected TSC–mTOR polarity while remodeling neuronal AKT and mitochondrial transcripts
Core Idea: In the atlas, Pten perturbation produced a signed thalamic and midbrain response and aligned on restricted signatures with Tsc1, Tsc2, and Depdc5 while opposing Mtor, Rptor, and Pik3ca. The biological interpretation is a transcriptomic recovery of established PI3K–AKT–TSC–mTOR pathway ordering, not direct evidence of altered PIP3, AKT phosphorylation, or mTORC1 activity.
Significance: This is a useful positive-control epistasis pattern showing that acute postnatal neuronal perturbations can preserve known pathway polarity. Its main value is to calibrate more novel relational findings and nominate a compact RNA readout for follow-up, rather than to establish a new mTOR research direction.
Prior expectation: Established biology already placed [PTEN upstream of AKT signaling](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-199418), showed that [AKT inhibits TSC2](https://doi.org/10.1038/ncb839), and defined the [TSC1–TSC2–TBC1D7 complex as an mTORC1 regulator](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22795129/). Alignment of Pten loss with Tsc1/Tsc2 loss was therefore expected before viewing the atlas.
Support: Pten perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut lowered Ypel3 (log2FC -0.652, FDR 9.51e-7), Scn2b (-0.437, FDR 0.00236), Akt3 (-0.414, FDR 0.0144), and Pink1 (-0.485, FDR 0.0495). In 155 MB Glut it lowered Pcsk1n (-0.497, FDR 2.00e-5) and raised Epha10 (+0.569, FDR 0.00435). Restricted-signature comparisons placed Pten with Tsc1/Tsc2 and against Mtor/Rptor/Pik3ca, but those comparator perturbations are not epistasis or rescue experiments; phospho-AKT and phospho-S6 were not measured.
Research community: Neuronal mTOR and tuberous-sclerosis researchers could use this as an assay-calibration result. Matched editing of PTEN, TSC1, TSC2, MTOR, and RPTOR followed by phospho-AKT/phospho-S6 measurements and rescue with pathway-selective inhibitors would test whether the RNA polarity tracks biochemical pathway state. If it does, the markers could serve as a compact transcriptomic pharmacodynamic panel rather than seed a new standalone program.

Title: MYEF2 perturbation repeatedly induces a DZIP3–VBP1–IST1 marker triad in subcortical neurons
Core Idea: Myef2 perturbation ranked first across the surveyed perturbations for the combined Dzip3, Vbp1, and Ist1 response, with the full triad reproduced in thalamic, midbrain glutamatergic, and posterior GABAergic neurons. The atlas observation establishes an unusually specific marker relationship; the interpretation that these genes form a functional MYEF2-controlled module remains unresolved.
Significance: The exact triad appears highly unexpected and could reveal an unrecognized output of a poorly characterized transcriptional repressor. At Priority 3, it is best treated as a compact entry point for defining MYEF2 function, not as evidence for a mature pathway or disease mechanism.
Prior expectation: MYEF2 was identified as a sequence-specific nuclear repressor at the [mouse Mbp promoter](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/7539003/) and later studied as a [context-dependent corepressor](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3457535/). Neither prior predicted coordinated Dzip3, Vbp1, and Ist1 induction.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Myef2 perturbation increased Vbp1 (log2FC +0.7218, FDR 6.92e-11), Dzip3 (+0.5808, FDR 6.88e-8), and Ist1 (+0.5233, FDR 1.57e-7). In 155 MB Glut it increased Dzip3 (+0.8002, FDR 7.06e-14) and Vbp1 (+0.9352, FDR 4.97e-12), and Ist1 rose in 191 MB P MY GABA (+0.5720, FDR 0.00503). Partial recurrence occurred in additional classes, but some had small perturbation groups; the data do not show whether MYEF2 directly binds these loci or whether the triad reports a secondary state.
Research community: Transcriptional-repressor and neuronal gene-regulation researchers would find this most useful. MYEF2 CUT&RUN paired with nascent RNA after independently validated editing, followed by wild-type MYEF2 rescue, would distinguish direct repression from a downstream stress marker. Direct occupancy and rescue would justify a modest program mapping MYEF2 cofactors and the cellular function of the triad.

Title: ANKRD11 and SETD5 perturbations converge on a thalamic synaptic-receptor marker module
Core Idea: Ankrd11 and Setd5 perturbations both induced Lrrc7, Prkcd, Rgs4, and related membrane or synaptic transcripts in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, while Med23 and Wdr26 projected oppositely on parts of the restricted marker set. The observation supports reporter-level convergence between two haploinsufficiency genes; it does not establish direct cooperation or broad antagonism with MED23 or WDR26.
Significance: If reproducible, the shared state could connect KBG syndrome and SETD5-related neurodevelopmental disease through a mature-neuron output not obvious from developmental studies. Its current scope supports comparative follow-up and biomarker development rather than a field-scale mechanistic program.
Prior expectation: [ANKRD11 mutations cause KBG syndrome with central nervous system involvement](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21782149/), while Setd5 haploinsufficiency was known to alter [developmental gene expression and cognition](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-018-0266-2) and [neuronal wiring](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2019.07.013). A shared mature thalamic Lrrc7/Prkcd/Rgs4 output was not an established expectation.

Setd5 perturbation increased Adarb1 (+0.3504, FDR 2.20e-6), Prkcd (+0.3412, FDR 1.82e-5), and Lrrc7 (+0.2628, FDR 1.82e-5). Setd5 mRNA was not FDR-depleted, and the cross-target relationship rests on a restricted module with modest whole-signature similarity.
Research community: KBG, SETD5-disorder, and neuronal chromatin researchers are the natural audience. Matched ANKRD11 and SETD5 editing in the same neuronal class, followed by single-cell multiome profiling and reciprocal rescue, would test whether the shared markers arise from common chromatin occupancy or convergent secondary state changes. A confirmed common regulon would enable cross-syndrome modifier and therapeutic-response studies.

Title: MIB1 perturbation induces a recurrent neuronal sterol response that tested Notch perturbations do not reproduce
Core Idea: Mib1 perturbation strongly induced an all-up SREBP/cholesterol-synthesis signature across thalamic, midbrain, and posterior GABAergic neurons. Dll1, Notch1–4, Adam10, and Psen1 did not reproduce that signature in the available comparisons, making sterol regulation the dominant atlas observation but not proving that it is independent of Notch signaling.
Significance: A validated MIB1-to-sterol connection would extend an E3 ligase best known for Notch-ligand signaling into neuronal lipid homeostasis. Because the negative Notch comparators are not securely engaged, the immediate value is a focused mechanistic test rather than a conclusion that MIB1 acts through a new Notch-independent pathway.
Prior expectation: MIB1 was expected to control [mammalian Notch-ligand signaling](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0001221), including Delta–Notch signaling in the [developing central nervous system](https://doi.org/10.1074/jbc.M112.398263), whereas [SREBP feedback](https://doi.org/10.1194/jlr.R800054-JLR200) was expected to regulate sterol-synthesis genes. The literature did not predict that Mib1 perturbation itself would elicit a large neuronal SREBP signature.
Support: Mib1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut increased Insig1 (log2FC +1.5277, FDR 1.25e-19), Fdft1 (+1.6080, FDR 4.97e-17), Msmo1 (+1.3900, FDR 5.81e-14), and Ldlr (+2.0957, FDR 1.73e-15). Insig1 also rose in 155 MB Glut (+1.6280, FDR 3.17e-16) and 191 MB P MY GABA (+1.7458, FDR 5.29e-15). Dll1 and Notch-family perturbations were sterol-silent in the tested signatures, but their weak target-transcript evidence prevents interpreting those nulls as genetic epistasis.
Research community: Notch, ubiquitin-ligase, and neuronal lipid-homeostasis researchers would be positioned to test this. The decisive experiment is matched MIB1 and DLL1/NOTCH editing with NICD target readouts, sterol lipidomics, SREBP2 cleavage, and rescue by wild-type versus ligase-dead MIB1. If the sterol response persists after Notch activity is experimentally normalized, it would support a bounded MIB1 substrate-discovery program.

Title: KANSL1 perturbation recurrently represses an endolysosomal-associated posterior neuronal module
Core Idea: Kansl1 perturbation repeatedly lowered Ttc4, Nsmce2, Commd4, Ctsa, and Man2b2 while increasing Dgkz in several posterior neuronal classes. This supports a KANSL1-associated endolysosomal and transport state, but the atlas does not show autophagic flux or reproduce the published STX17 transcript mechanism.
Significance: The result provides a neuronal RNA phenotype that could connect Koolen-de Vries syndrome to organelle homeostasis in mature neurons. It is a moderate-priority refinement because the exact relationship to autophagosome–lysosome fusion remains indirect.
Prior expectation: KANSL1 haploinsufficiency was already known to cause [Koolen-de Vries syndrome](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22544363/), and mouse work linked Kansl1 dosage to [STX17-dependent autophagosome–lysosome fusion](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35177641/). The expectation was therefore an autophagy-related defect, but not this recurrent Ttc4/Nsmce2/Commd4/Dgkz transcript pattern.

Ttc4 and Nsmce2 were also reduced and Dgkz induced in 191 MB P MY GABA and 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA. Kansl1 mRNA was unchanged in the quoted 155 row, and neither STX17 RNA nor lysosomal flux was measured, so an indirect stress state remains a major alternative.
Research community: Koolen-de Vries, NSL-complex, and neuronal autophagy researchers would benefit most. Live-cell autophagosome–lysosome fusion assays after validated Kansl1 editing, combined with KANSL1 rescue and STX17 rescue, would distinguish a fusion defect from broader transcriptional or organelle stress. A verified fusion defect would direct a focused search for mature-neuron organelle liabilities in KANSL1 disease.

Title: BPTF and CHD4 perturbations drive opposite directions on a diagnostic posterior-neuronal marker set
Core Idea: Bptf perturbation induced Zcwpw2 and Grk4 while repressing Resp18, Abhd14b, and Stxbp2 across thalamic and midbrain-linked neurons; Chd4 was the strongest negative neighbor when projected onto the Bptf marker set. The atlas therefore resolves opposite outputs from BPTF/NURF and CHD4/NuRD on selected genes, not a direct biochemical antagonism between the remodelers.
Significance: This contrast could sharpen how distinct ATP-dependent chromatin-remodeling systems maintain mature neuronal expression states. At present it offers a diagnostic comparative system for chromatin studies rather than evidence for a new antagonistic complex axis.
Prior expectation: BPTF was established as the NURF subunit that reads [H3K4me3 through its PHD finger](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature04815), whereas CHD4 belongs to the NuRD remodeling complex, as reviewed in [chromatin-remodeler literature](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27235397/). Those distinct complex memberships made non-equivalence plausible but did not predict opposite regulation of the observed marker set.
Support: Bptf perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut increased Zcwpw2 (log2FC +1.738, FDR 1.55e-22) and Grk4 (+2.519, FDR 1.07e-5) while lowering Resp18 (-1.377, FDR 3.99e-19). In 155 MB Glut it increased Zcwpw2 (+1.263, FDR 3.17e-10) and lowered Abhd14b (-1.173, FDR 1.43e-9). Chd4 perturbation moved Stxbp2 oppositely upward in 151 (+0.584, FDR 3.88e-4). The opposition comes from restricted-signature projection, many tail genes used FDR<0.1, and no chromatin occupancy or remodeling activity was measured.
Research community: NURF, NuRD, and neuronal chromatin researchers are the relevant audience. Parallel BPTF and CHD4 depletion with CUT&RUN/ATAC-seq at Zcwpw2, Resp18, and Stxbp2, plus perturbation rescue, would determine whether the opposite RNA outputs reflect direct locus control or distinct secondary states. A direct result would motivate a modest study of remodeler-specific maintenance circuits in mature neurons.

Title: THAP11 and THAP1 perturbations produce non-interchangeable subcortical transcriptional states
Core Idea: Thap11 perturbation caused a large recurrent state with Rabac1, Gdap1, Sdccag8, Mdh1, and Ndufa3 repression and Prkn and Ttc41 induction, whereas Thap1 perturbation produced a much smaller Prepl/Cuedc2-down, Fam117a-up module. The biological interpretation is paralog divergence between a THAP11 metabolic/core-gene state and a THAP1 dystonia-linked state with only partial Ogt overlap.
Significance: This separation cautions against treating THAP-domain proteins as exchangeable and gives THAP1 disease work a marker set distinct from the much broader THAP11 response. The finding is valuable for comparative mechanism studies, but unequal signature size and unresolved protein editing constrain its impact.
Prior expectation: THAP11/Ronin was known to regulate [embryonic pluripotency](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18585351/) and [mitochondrial genes in developing retina](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26876175/), while THAP1 mutations were established causes of [DYT6 dystonia](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19182804/) and THAP1 was known to associate with [HCF-1 and OGT](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20200153/). Related DNA-binding domains did not imply matching neuronal transcriptomes.
Support: For Thap11 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Rabac1 fell (log2FC -1.3960, FDR 1.52e-52), Gdap1 fell (-1.8084, FDR 1.08e-48), and Prkn rose (+1.0237, FDR 1.95e-34); in 155 MB Glut, Rabac1 fell (-1.6945, FDR 2.39e-62) and Ttc41 rose (+3.9843, FDR 2.16e-27). Thap1 perturbation instead lowered Prepl in 151 (-0.3358, FDR 3.79e-6) and 155 (-0.4115, FDR 6.50e-5), lowered Cuedc2 in both, and raised Fam117a in 151 (+3.2295, FDR 0.00573) and 155 (+2.4678, FDR 0.0204). Thap11 mRNA lacked FDR depletion, and the large disparity in response size could reflect unequal functional perturbation.
Research community: Dystonia, THAP-family, and transcriptional-metabolism researchers would be most interested. Matched protein-level depletion of THAP1 and THAP11 followed by occupancy mapping, long-read RNA profiling, and OGT perturbation or rescue would test whether the two programs are intrinsically distinct. Confirmation would support paralog-specific disease models and a focused THAP1–OGT regulatory study.

Title: GATAD1 and PHF12 perturbations share a sparse organelle/RNA-processing output not reproduced by nearby chromatin regulators
Core Idea: Gatad1 perturbation induced Atp6v0b and Chpf2 and increased Phf12, while Phf12 perturbation strongly induced Ptcd3 and repressed Gls in the same thalamic context; Kmt2e reproduced only part of this marker neighborhood. Kdm5b, Kdm5c, Sin3a, and Gatad2b did not match the restricted signature, suggesting a selective GATAD1–PHF12 neighborhood rather than a generic chromatin-loss state.

It did not predict this Atp6v0b/Chpf2/Ptcd3 neuronal relationship.

Phf12 perturbation in 151 strongly increased Ptcd3 (+0.7328, FDR 3.83e-21) and lowered Gls (-0.2920, FDR 9.59e-8), while Kmt2e also increased Ptcd3 (+0.4840, FDR 3.04e-4). Gatad1 self-RNA did not pass FDR, the Kmt2e extension is sparse, and restricted similarity does not establish a shared protein complex.

Endogenous co-complex proteomics plus matched GATAD1 and PHF12 depletion with CUT&RUN and rescue would distinguish physical cooperation from convergent secondary stress. Direct co-occupancy would support a focused study of this regulator pair at organelle and synaptic loci.

The atlas establishes a replicated signed contrast at one transcript; whether CSF2RA marks direct chromatin regulation or a secondary neuronal state is unknown.

Title: PRDM10 perturbation repeatedly represses a MADD–CADM3–PTOV1 neuronal module

This atlas observation defines a broad recurrent neuronal module distinct from the Eif3b-centered embryonic PRDM10 program; it does not establish direct transcriptional activation by PRDM10.

The same genes fell in 155 MB Glut (Madd -1.2683, FDR 2.97e-29; Cadm3 -0.9839, FDR 3.32e-25; Ptov1 -1.3337, FDR 1.83e-28) and 191 MB P MY GABA (Madd -1.3263, FDR 3.45e-18; Cadm3 -1.2275, FDR 1.12e-20; Ptov1 -1.6685, FDR 5.57e-20). PRDM10 occupancy and acute protein degradation, paired with nascent transcription and rescue, would test direct maintenance of Madd, Cadm3, and Ptov1; neurite and synapse assays would establish consequence. Direct regulation with a neuronal phenotype would make the mature-neuron PRDM10 regulon worth mapping.

Title: Cytosolic MDH1 and GOT1 perturbations separate from mitochondrial malate–aspartate-shuttle components
Core Idea: Mdh1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut induced Gls while lowering synaptic-adhesion transcripts, and Got1 showed a weaker Asns/Gls-positive tail. Got2, Mdh2, and Slc25a12 were depleted at their own transcripts but did not reproduce those RNA outputs, suggesting a cytosolic-side distinction within the malate–aspartate shuttle.
Significance: If metabolic measurements confirm the split, it would refine how different shuttle components affect neuronal carbon, redox, and synaptic states. The present result warrants a focused metabolic study but cannot establish privileged cytosolic flux from RNA alone.
Prior expectation: Reactome places MDH1 and GOT1 on the cytosolic side and MDH2, GOT2, and SLC25A12 on the mitochondrial side of the [malate–aspartate shuttle](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-9856872). Human disease already linked [GOT2 deficiency to shuttle-related encephalopathy](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31422819/) and [MDH1 deficiency to severe encephalopathy](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31538237/), but not the observed signed neuronal RNA split.
Support: Mdh1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut reduced Mdh1 (log2FC -1.0586, FDR 1.59e-22), increased Gls (+0.3196, FDR 1.83e-5), and lowered Nrxn1 (-0.2644), Cadm3 (-0.2603), Lrrtm1 (-0.3493), and Grin2b (-0.1739), each at FDR 0.0123. Got1 itself fell (-0.9529, FDR 5.52e-11), while Asns increased only at FDR 0.0763. Got2, Mdh2, and Slc25a12 self-rows were significant, yet Gls and the selected synaptic markers had FDR 1.0 in their quoted comparisons. Metabolites, NADH/NAD+ balance, and shuttle flux were not measured, and the Got1 arm is only near-threshold.
Research community: Neuro-metabolism and inborn-error researchers would be most interested. Isotope tracing of malate, aspartate, and glutamate with compartment-resolved redox sensors after matched MDH1, GOT1, MDH2, GOT2, and SLC25A12 editing would test whether the RNA split reflects distinct flux failure. Confirmation would enable a bounded study of cytosolic versus mitochondrial shuttle liabilities in neurons.

Title: NatB and NatA perturbations produce separable neuronal states rather than a common acetyltransferase response

Naa15 perturbation instead increased Ldhd (+1.2117, FDR 5.66e-15), Actr1b (+0.5065, FDR 4.57e-7), and Matk (+0.7706, FDR 4.63e-6) while lowering Rtn3 (-0.4610, FDR 5.15e-10) in 151, with similar directions in 155. Quantitative N-terminomics after matched NAA20, NAA15, and NAA10 protein depletion, combined with catalytic rescue, would determine whether the divergent RNA states follow distinct substrate acetylomes. Confirmation would support a comparative NatA/NatB neuronal-substrate program.

ZBTB7A occupancy and chromatin-accessibility mapping with matched ZBTB18 perturbation and rescue would test whether the divergence is direct and identify coherent target subsets. Confirmation would justify a focused comparison of ZBTB paralog regulons rather than a broad disease program.

Title: SETD1A perturbation links recurrent organelle-gene repression to a modest TSC-associated signature neighborhood
Core Idea: Setd1a perturbation repeatedly repressed Fbxw5, Itfg1, Nprl3, Coq2, Ufl1, Foxred1, and other organelle-associated transcripts across thalamic, midbrain, and posterior neurons. These modest correlations do not establish a shared mechanism, and neither mTORC1 phosphorylation nor organelle function was measured.

Matched Setd1a and Tsc1/Tsc2 perturbations with H3K4me3 profiling, mitochondrial respiration, and phospho-S6 readouts, followed by SETD1A rescue, would separate a direct chromatin/organelle defect from mTOR-mediated secondary remodeling. USP48 activity-probe proteomics and ubiquitin-remnant profiling after validated editing, paired with wild-type and catalytic-dead rescue, would identify whether ABHD12B or CYP46A1 changes track a specific substrate. A recovered substrate chain would support a focused neuronal USP48 mechanism study.

Small-RNA sequencing and nascent Pol III transcription after matched POLR3B and BDP1 protein depletion, combined with Rap activity assays and rescue, would test whether the two RNA states reflect distinct primary lesions. Confirmation would justify a focused core-versus-initiation comparison in neuronal Pol III disease.

Title: SKI perturbation reveals a mature cortical marker state rather than a canonical SMAD response
Core Idea: Ski perturbation generated a 14-gene mixed state in 007 L2-3 IT CTX Glut, including Wscd2, Chd3, Rfx3, Pde4a, Plk5, Ttyh3, and Acsl3. Matched SKI degradation and TGF-beta stimulation with phospho-SMAD, SKI/SMAD occupancy, and rescue in L2/3 neurons would distinguish a direct noncanonical regulon from unequal perturbation or baseline signaling. A rescued noncanonical regulon would add a bounded mature-neuron arm to SKI disease biology.

Matched acute degradation of DHX9, TPR, TNPO3, NUP93, and RANBP2 followed by nuclear/cytoplasmic RNA sequencing, long-read splicing analysis, and live export imaging would test whether the neighborhood reflects shared transport failure or distinct secondary stresses. Confirmation would support a comparative, subcomplex-resolved neuronal RNA-trafficking program.

Title: AHDC1 perturbation represses a neuronal surface and synaptic cassette without canonical Wnt activation
Core Idea: Ahdc1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut lowered Ptprz1, Spock3, Lrp1b, Gria2, Cdh18, Ache, Calb1, and Wls. Axin2 and Nkd1 were not induced, so the observed state is better described as surface and synaptic transcript loss than as an APC-like Wnt response; its restriction to one resolved context is not evidence of intrinsic specificity.
Significance: The signature gives AHDC1-related Xia-Gibbs syndrome research a concrete mature-neuronal phenotype to test. It remains moderate priority because it is one-context, guide-defined, and lacks functional synaptic or chromatin validation.
Prior expectation: AHDC1 truncating variants were known to cause [Xia-Gibbs syndrome with developmental delay, hypotonia, and sleep apnea](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24791903/), while AHDC1 expression is broadly detected across brain regions in the [Human Protein Atlas](https://www.proteinatlas.org/ENSG00000126705-AHDC1). Neither source predicted this neuronal surface/synaptic cassette.

Axin2 and Nkd1 had FDR 1.0. The main state contains only 13 FDR genes, Ahdc1 self-RNA was not depleted, and absence outside 151 is not interpreted as specificity because context power and protein engagement are unresolved.
Research community: Xia-Gibbs syndrome, synapse, and neuronal-surface biology researchers would be most likely to use the finding. Validated AHDC1 protein depletion with surface proteomics, synaptic physiology, chromatin profiling, and rescue would distinguish direct maintenance of the cassette from a secondary state. Confirmation would provide a compact disease-model assay and a focused study of AHDC1-dependent neuronal maintenance.

Title: VEZF1 perturbation produces a compact HR–PRKCG–RASA4-down and TLE2–ABI2-up neuronal state

VEZF1 CUT&RUN and methylome profiling after validated protein depletion, with rescue and RASA4/RAS activity measurements, would distinguish direct locus control from a secondary guide-associated state. Title: CACNA1G perturbation produces a thalamic RNA state not reproduced by other calcium-channel perturbations
Core Idea: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut neurons, Cacna1g perturbation produced a 12-gene FDR-supported response with Ptprn and Iyd increased and Stmn4, Cpne7, Jun, Zdhhc22, and Lrrc7 decreased. Cacna1c, Cacna2d1, and Cacnb4 perturbations in the same class showed target-transcript loss but did not reproduce that downstream pattern. The observation therefore distinguishes a Cav3.1-associated abundance state from those of the assayed L-type and auxiliary calcium-channel components; whether it reflects unique T-type-channel physiology remains untested.
Significance: This provides a tractable transcriptional correlate of Cav3.1 disruption in a neuronal population where T-type currents are already biologically important. Confirmation would refine calcium-channel classification by showing that related channel perturbations need not converge at the RNA endpoint, but the result presently supports a focused physiology study rather than a broad new calcium-signaling model.
Prior expectation: Cav3.1/CACNA1G was already expected to be important in thalamic firing: its thalamic expression was mapped by [Talley and colleagues](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10066243/), and knockout mice lack normal thalamocortical burst firing and resist absence seizures in the [Cav3.1 loss study](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11498049/). What was not predicted was the specific Ptprn/Iyd-positive, Jun/Stmn4-negative RNA state or its separation from the other assayed calcium-channel perturbations.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Cacna1g itself decreased by log2FC -0.341 (FDR 2.37e-8). Ptprn increased by +0.311 (FDR 1.60e-8) and Iyd by +0.867 (FDR 3.74e-4), whereas Stmn4 decreased by -0.308 (FDR 2.50e-7), Cpne7 by -0.347 (FDR 6.48e-7), Jun by -0.657 (FDR 3.50e-4), and Zdhhc22 by -0.367 (FDR 3.50e-4). In the same class, Cacna1c, Cacna2d1, and Cacnb4 each had an FDR-significant self-transcript decrease but only a self-transcript FDR hit, and their genome-wide score correlations with the Cacna1c profile were small. Equivalent loss of channel protein or current was not measured, so non-phenocopying cannot yet be assigned wholly to channel biology.
Research community: Thalamic electrophysiologists and calcium-channel researchers could test whether the RNA state tracks loss of low-threshold bursting rather than generic calcium stress. The decisive experiment is matched editing of Cacna1g, Cacna1c, Cacna2d1, and Cacnb4 in molecularly identified Prkcd/Grin2c neurons, followed by Cav-current and burst-firing measurements alongside targeted RNA profiling. If the Cacna1g state follows a uniquely altered T-type current, it would motivate a bounded program connecting channel biophysics to long-term transcriptional adaptation.

Title: Zbtb18 perturbation induces an Endou-Pxdn-Clstn2-Man2a2 module in thalamic neurons
Core Idea: Zbtb18 perturbation increased Endou, Pxdn, Clstn2, Man2a2, and Zbtb18 itself in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut neurons, and Pxdn also increased in 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut. This is an adult neuronal output associated with Zbtb18 guide assignment, but the rise in Zbtb18 RNA means it cannot yet be interpreted as a simple ZBTB18 loss-of-function regulon.
Significance: The module supplies concrete candidate outputs for studying ZBTB18 beyond its established developmental-repressor role. Because the perturbation direction is unresolved and the affected genes do not yet define a functional pathway, its main value is as a compact, testable regulatory lead.
Prior expectation: ZBTB18/RP58 was expected to act as a transcriptional repressor in neurodevelopment, based on its original characterization as a sequence-specific repressor in the [EMBO Journal study](https://doi.org/10.1093/emboj/20.10.2536), and more recent work connects it to neuronal maturation and psychiatric disease in [Molecular Psychiatry](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41380-023-01941-3). Those precedents did not predict Endou, Pxdn, Clstn2, and Man2a2 as a shared postnatal neuronal output.

The corresponding four genes all had FDR 1.0 after Dnmt3a or Ctbp1 perturbation in 151, but those comparators did not show FDR-supported target-transcript depletion. The upward Zbtb18 row and uncertain comparator engagement leave guide effects, altered isoforms, and indirect stress among the alternatives.

The separating experiment is amplicon-level editing and ZBTB18 protein measurement coupled to CUT&RUN at Endou, Pxdn, Clstn2, and Man2a2, with independent guides and a CRISPR-resistant rescue. Direct occupancy plus rescue would justify studying how the module affects mature thalamic connectivity; failure would reclassify it as a guide-associated marker state.

Title: CUL3 perturbation lowers a compact RNA-processing module without a KLHL7 phenocopy
Core Idea: Cul3 perturbation in 155 MB Glut lowered Rsrp1, Snhg11, and Srsf5 while increasing Akap11 and Trim28; Pnisr was the sole FDR-supported thalamic output. Klhl7 perturbation did not align with either Cul3 response. The result suggests that reduced CUL3 scaffold activity can couple to nuclear RNA-processing abundance, but it does not identify the relevant substrate adaptor or establish direct regulation.
Significance: The finding narrows a potentially useful neuronal output of CUL3 dysfunction and cautions against assuming that one disease-linked CUL3 adaptor reproduces the scaffold phenotype. Its modest size makes it most valuable as a route to adaptor discovery, not as evidence for a broad RNA-processing mechanism.
Prior expectation: CUL3 deficiency was already known to alter neuronal excitation-inhibition balance and translation, as shown in the [Cul3 mouse-neuron study](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31780330/). KLHL7 is a documented CUL3 substrate adaptor from [biochemical work on the CUL3-KLHL7 ligase](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21828050/). A selective Rsrp1/Snhg11/Srsf5 decrease was not an established consequence of either relationship.

In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Pnisr decreased by -0.330 (FDR 0.0483). Klhl7-Cul3 signature correlations were near zero in both classes, but Klhl7 itself was not FDR-depleted in the displayed comparisons. The small number of corrected hits and unresolved adaptor therefore limit mechanism.
Research community: CUL3 neurodevelopmental-disease and ubiquitin-ligase researchers could use the module to search for the responsible adaptor-substrate pair. A decisive study would compare acute CUL3 degradation with a panel of neuronally expressed BTB/Kelch adaptors, measuring ubiquitin remodelling and Pnisr/Rsrp1/Srsf5 RNA, followed by rescue with ligase-competent versus ligase-dead CUL3. Confirmation would support a focused screen for the substrate linking CUL3 activity to RNA-processing homeostasis.

Title: Creb1 perturbation recurrently induces Crem without recreating an activity-induced CREB program
Core Idea: Across several neuronal classes, Creb1 perturbation was dominated by increased Crem rather than induction of canonical immediate-early CREB targets. The 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut response added a mixed set of metabolic and membrane-associated decreases. The observation is consistent with compensation within the CREB/ATF family, although the assay cannot distinguish activating CREM isoforms from the repressive ICER isoforms.
Significance: This recovers a known compensatory relationship at cell-class resolution and shows that basal Creb1 perturbation is not equivalent to an acute phosphorylated-CREB activation state. The atlas was therefore expected to differ from an acute stimulation experiment, but the recurrence and magnitude of Crem induction across neuronal classes were not predetermined.

In 151, Tmem181a fell by -0.915 (FDR 3.55e-4), Txndc12 by -1.069 (FDR 0.00742), and Chkb by -0.636 (FDR 0.00869). Across 294 tested rows for 14 canonical activity-responsive genes, none reached FDR 0.05. Because this was an unsynchronized basal endpoint and Crem isoforms were unresolved, absence of immediate-early genes does not exclude transient CREB signaling.

Long-read isoform profiling and CREM/ICER protein measurements after independently validated Creb1 editing, with a time course before and after depolarization, would distinguish family compensation from altered activity history. If ICER predominates, a proportionate next step is to test whether it prolongs repression of CRE-responsive genes after stimulation.

Gramicidin-perforated patch recordings and calcium imaging in edited Prkcd/Grin2c neurons, paired with KCC2 protein localization and targeted RNA measurements, would distinguish altered chloride physiology from a nonspecific stress response. Confirmation would support a focused comparison of KCC2-restoring interventions across molecularly defined thalamic and non-thalamic neurons.

Title: Manf perturbation recurrently induces an Hspa5-centered ER-chaperone response

Induction of ER chaperones after MANF insufficiency was therefore plausible; recurrence across these neuronal classes and the exact Hspa5/Sdf2l1/Dnajb9 combination provide the new resolution.

Atf6 did not reproduce the response, but its target depletion was only nominal, and neither ATF6 cleavage nor Xbp1 splicing was measured.

A decisive experiment would combine confirmed Manf editing with recombinant MANF or AAV-MANF rescue, measuring BiP/HSPA5 protein, XBP1 splicing, ATF6 cleavage, and neuronal survival under controlled ER stress. Rescue of both the transcriptional state and stress sensitivity would justify a targeted MANF-augmentation study in the most responsive neuronal populations.

Title: Pde10a perturbation produces a narrow Scg2-positive state in striatal-lineage GABA neurons
Core Idea: In 059 CNU-LGE LSX GABA, Pde10a perturbation decreased Pde10a and increased Scg2 at FDR 0.05, while Crem and Vgf rose only nominally and classic immediate-early or neuropeptide markers did not. The observation is compatible with a chronic genetic cAMP-state adaptation that differs from acute PDE10A-inhibitor responses. Because the broad cell label does not distinguish D1 from D2 medium spiny neurons, the responsible striatal population remains unresolved.

Acute PDE10A inhibition induces broader striatal transcription in [pharmacological studies](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19765598/) and can elevate c-Fos in [striatal neurons](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26256420/), making the narrow chronic endpoint noteworthy but not mechanistically surprising.

Crem and Vgf moved upward only nominally, while Fos, Arc, Egr1, Penk, and Tac1 were not FDR-significant. This is just one corrected downstream gene in one broad class; transcriptomics alone does not establish altered cAMP, cGMP, or PDE10A protein activity.

The key experiment is D1- versus D2-resolved editing and longitudinal cAMP imaging, followed by acute inhibitor challenge and Scg2 measurement. If Scg2 is specific to sustained PDE10A deficiency, it could become a modest biomarker for separating chronic adaptation from acute target engagement.

Title: Atp2a2 and Elp2 perturbations converge on an amino-acid and ER-stress state
Core Idea: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Atp2a2 and Elp2 perturbations independently induced Asns, Chac1, Ddit3, Herpud1, Mthfd2, Shmt2, and related amino-acid-stress genes. A shared ATF4-like stress endpoint is plausible, but ATF4 activity, ER calcium, and tRNA modifications were not measured.

It offers a practical way to test whether restoring amino-acid balance or translational homeostasis can rescue both perturbations, while remaining within an established integrated-stress framework.

Convergence on the same neuronal amino-acid-stress genes was biologically plausible but not directly predicted.

The shared markers robustly establish state convergence, but not a common upstream mechanism.

Matched edited neurons should be assayed for ER calcium, tRNA modifications, eIF2α phosphorylation, ATF4 occupancy, and amino-acid flux, with ISRIB or genetic ATF4 attenuation as a branch test. Rescue of both signatures through the same downstream intervention would support a shared vulnerability module rather than superficial marker overlap.

Title: Rora perturbation represses a thalamic excitability and synaptic-output state
Core Idea: Rora perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut reduced Prkcg, Adgrb1, Grin2c, Kcnc2, Gabra4, Gabrd, Gria2, Sema7a, Nr1d1, and Cry2 while increasing a smaller set of genes. Prkcg decreased by log2FC -0.764 (FDR 9.68e-18), Adgrb1 by -0.702 (FDR 1.35e-17), Grin2c by -0.626 (FDR 1.72e-6), Kcnc2 by -0.355 (FDR 3.72e-10), Gabra4 by -0.291 (FDR 1.87e-4), and Nr1d1 by -0.848 (FDR 5.32e-9). Rora itself decreased only nominally (-0.342, FDR 0.218). Rorb and Rorc had no FDR hits in the same class, but Rorb self-RNA was only nominal and Rorc self-RNA was unchanged; their nulls therefore cannot establish non-interchangeability. Silence in other classes is also not used to infer specificity.

The decisive design is orthogonal Rora editing with protein verification and rescue, followed by patch clamp and targeted proteomics for KCNC2, GRIN2C, and GABA receptors; Rorb/Rorc comparisons require matched protein loss. Confirmation would support a focused study of RORA-dependent maintenance of mature thalamic excitability.

Title: Wac perturbation couples loss of mature neuronal effectors to increased RNA-processing transcripts
Core Idea: Wac perturbation repeatedly lowered neuronal and synaptic transcripts such as Eno2, Ache, Lgi3, Septin5, and C1qtnf4, while increasing Clk1 and, in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, multiple RNA-processing genes. Those functions suggested transcriptional consequences, but not the specific opposition between synaptic-effectors and RNA-processing genes seen here.

Wac RNA trended downward in most classes but never reached FDR, and the assay did not measure protein, H2Bub1, or splice isoforms.

Independent Wac editing with WAC protein and H2Bub1 measurements, nascent RNA sequencing, long-read splicing, and rescue by wild-type versus RNF20/40-binding-deficient WAC would be decisive. A direct dependence on H2B ubiquitination would justify mapping the affected mature-neuron genes and testing whether their restoration improves synaptic physiology.

Title: Vps37a perturbation increases V-ATPase transcripts while reducing synaptic membrane genes
Core Idea: Vps37a perturbation produced a large 155 MB Glut response and a smaller 191 MB P MY GABA response in which Atp6v0b or Atp6v1h increased while glutamate-receptor, ion-channel, and adhesion transcripts decreased. This is consistent with an endolysosomal acidification-stress response accompanying erosion of neuronal membrane identity. The RNA pattern does not demonstrate altered lysosomal pH, ESCRT function, or direct regulation by VPS37A.
Significance: The paired acidification and synaptic arms provide a measurable neuronal consequence of disrupting an ESCRT-I component. If validated functionally, they could help connect endosomal sorting defects to loss of excitability-related membrane proteins, but the current result remains an extension of established endolysosomal biology.
Prior expectation: VPS37A was expected to function in ESCRT-I and late-endosomal sorting from its curated [UniProt record](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q8NEZ2). Increased V-ATPase subunits and concomitant loss of specific synaptic-membrane transcripts were reasonable stress adaptations, but not an established neuronal VPS37A signature.

In 191 MB P MY GABA, Atp6v1h increased by +0.898 (FDR 0.00375) and Atp6v0b by +0.485 (FDR 0.0323), while Gria4 decreased by -0.925 (FDR 0.0288), Asic1 by -1.429 (FDR 0.0288), and Gria3 by -1.300 (FDR 0.0314). Vps37a RNA itself was not FDR-depleted, and altered organelle acidification was not measured.

Edited neurons should undergo VPS37A protein validation, lysosomal-pH imaging, endosomal cargo tracking, and surface proteomics for AMPA receptors and ASIC1, with a CRISPR-resistant rescue. Concordant restoration of pH, surface proteins, and RNA would support a focused ESCRT-to-synapse mechanism.

Title: Autophagy-initiation perturbations reduce ER and endomembrane transcripts in selected neuronal states
Core Idea: Rb1cc1 perturbation decreased Calr, Hspa5, Pdia6, Psap, Atp1a3, or Nrxn1 across 151 TH, 155 MB, and 191 MB P MY GABA neurons; Becn1 and Atg5 produced overlapping but narrower 155 MB effects. The relationship is a partial convergence of distinct autophagy-initiation and conjugation perturbations on ER/endomembrane RNA abundance, not proof that all three perturbations create the same autophagic defect. Autophagic flux and lysosomal clearance were not assayed.
Significance: The result identifies an early RNA consequence of disrupting autophagy machinery in mature neurons that is not a broad DDIT3/XBP1 alarm. It may help separate loss of basal membrane-homeostasis support from later unfolded-protein stress, but the modest and unequal perturbation responses keep it a moderate follow-up.
Prior expectation: RB1CC1/FIP200 is required for autophagosome formation in [mammalian genetic studies](https://doi.org/10.1083/jcb.200712064), and suppression of basal autophagy in neural cells causes degeneration in the [Atg-deficient mouse study](https://doi.org/10.1038/nature04724). BECN1 is part of distinct PI3-kinase complexes described by [Itakura and colleagues](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18843052/). A shared early reduction of ER-lumen and membrane-homeostasis transcripts was not a canonical endpoint.

In 155 MB Glut, Calr fell by -0.945 (FDR 0.00559), Psap by -0.365 (FDR 0.00559), and Atp1a3 by -0.327 (FDR 0.0163). Becn1 perturbation in 155 reduced Psma5 by -0.879 (FDR 0.00767), Calr by -0.687 (FDR 0.0299), and Ctsb by -0.404 (FDR 0.0453); Atg5 reduced Calr by -0.896 and Rtn1 by -0.308 (both FDR 0.0410). Matched Rb1cc1, Becn1, and Atg5 editing should be followed by LC3 turnover, p62 accumulation, lysosomal flux, ER morphology, and Calr/Atp1a3 protein measurements, with stage-specific rescue constructs. If the same flux defect predicts the shared RNA arm, the markers could support a focused study of early neuronal autophagy failure.

Title: Atp2b1 loss induces a thalamic activity-like state not shared by PMCA2 or PMCA3 loss
Core Idea: Atp2b1 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut induced Fos, Nr4a1, Vgf, Cck, Ptprn, and related neurosecretory genes. Atp2b2 and Atp2b3 were efficiently depleted in the same class but did not reproduce the module. The within-context contrast supports a PMCA1-associated activity state distinct from other PMCA paralogs, although intracellular calcium and firing were not measured.
Significance: This is a relatively clean paralog-boundary result and suggests that PMCA1 has a nonredundant role in sustaining calcium homeostasis in these neurons. It could refine which calcium-extrusion component is most relevant to activity-dependent transcription, but the biological consequence remains within established calcium-signaling expectations.
Prior expectation: PMCAs were already known as high-affinity neuronal calcium-extrusion pumps with isoform-dependent functions, summarized in the [nervous-system PMCA review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21537478/), and calcium-dependent neuronal transcription was established by [West and colleagues](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.191352298). An activity-like response after PMCA1 loss was plausible; failure of strongly depleted PMCA2 and PMCA3 to reproduce it was the sharper observation.

Chml increased by +0.674 (FDR 5.67e-5), Fos by +1.026 (FDR 9.33e-4), Cck by +0.326 (FDR 0.00248), Vgf by +0.571 (FDR 0.00248), and Nr4a1 by +0.646 (FDR 0.00356). Atp2b2 decreased by -1.019 (FDR 8.94e-35) and Atp2b3 by -0.649 (FDR 5.31e-6), yet each had only its self-transcript at FDR 0.05; score correlations with the Atp2b1 state were 0.085 and 0.0002. These RNA contrasts are strong, but calcium load, PMCA protein, and activity were not directly observed.
Research community: Calcium-homeostasis and thalamic-circuit researchers could directly test PMCA paralog nonredundancy. Equivalent Atp2b1, Atp2b2, and Atp2b3 protein depletion should be paired with calcium-decay kinetics, spontaneous firing, and activity-gene profiling, followed by isoform-swapped rescue. Failure of PMCA2 or PMCA3 to rescue the Atp2b1 phenotype would justify a targeted program on PMCA1-specific localization or interaction partners.

Title: Mecp2 perturbation yields sparse, context-dependent excitability and membrane-gene changes
Core Idea: Mecp2 perturbation produced a mixed 151 TH membrane and ion-channel response, recurrent Cacna1g repression in 151 and 155, and an isolated Auts2 increase in 052 Pvalb Gaba. The atlas does not show a universal Rett-like transcriptome at this postnatal endpoint. The sparse pattern may reflect cell-state-dependent MECP2 output, variable functional editing, or both.
Significance: The result is useful primarily as a constraint: acute mosaic postnatal MECP2 perturbation should not be assumed to reproduce developmental Rett signatures uniformly. Cacna1g and Auts2 offer candidate readouts for focused validation, but the finding does not yet identify a general rescue mechanism.
Prior expectation: MECP2 loss was expected to model Rett syndrome based on the discovery of pathogenic variants by [Amir and colleagues](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10508514/), and MECP2 is understood as a methyl-DNA-associated chromatin regulator, including through work on its neuronal transcriptional effects in [Molecular Cell](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31784358/). A sparse acute postnatal response was plausible because this design differs from developmental, germline, and chronic disease models.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut after Mecp2 perturbation, Ache decreased by log2FC -0.693 (FDR 4.61e-6), Fxyd7 by -0.795 (FDR 0.00689), and Cacna1g by -0.203 (FDR 0.0175), while Kcnh5 increased by +0.450 (FDR 0.0371). Cacna1g also decreased in 155 MB Glut by -0.645 (FDR 1.18e-4). In 052 Pvalb Gaba, Auts2 increased by +0.527 (FDR 9.21e-5). Mecp2 self-RNA was not FDR-depleted, which is not a valid editing gate in this ORF-indel assay but leaves protein engagement unmeasured; no behavior, physiology, or developmental trajectory was assessed.
Research community: Rett-syndrome and MECP2 chromatin researchers could test whether Cacna1g and Auts2 are reproducible acute outputs rather than guide- or state-dependent markers. Independent guides, amplicon sequencing, MECP2 protein quantification, and cell-class-resolved rescue should be combined with electrophysiology and a developmental time course. Confirmation would support a limited study of acute versus developmental MECP2 dependencies rather than a universal Rett expression signature.

Title: Eif4e suppression induces an Rnpc3-Amy1-high, Rsrp1-low abundance state
Core Idea: Eif4e perturbation produced a large 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut response and recurrent echoes in 155 MB Glut and 145 MH-LH TH Glut, with Rnpc3 and Amy1 increased and Rsrp1, Fkbp2, or Pcsk1n decreased. The observation shows that reduced EIF4E dosage is transcriptionally visible, but it does not identify the translated proteins that cause the response. It should not be equated with MNK inhibition, which changes EIF4E phosphorylation rather than EIF4E abundance.
Significance: The recurrent marker combination could serve as a pharmacodynamic comparator for genetic EIF4E suppression and may expose feedback between translation initiation and RNA processing. Its disease relevance is moderate because the causal translation changes and relationship to existing EIF4E-directed therapies remain unmeasured.
Prior expectation: EIF4E-dependent translation was already implicated in autism-related circuitry, including cell-type-specific 4E-BP2 effects in [inhibitory neurons](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31427534/). This made a neuronal response to Eif4e suppression expected, whereas the Rnpc3/Amy1/Rsrp1 composition and its recurrence across these classes were not established.

In 155 MB Glut, Rnpc3 rose by +0.530 (FDR 3.76e-5) and Amy1 by +0.707 (FDR 9.80e-5), while Rsrp1 fell by -0.277 (FDR 0.00406) and Pcsk1n by -0.579 (FDR 0.00406). In 145 MH-LH TH Glut, Amy1, Rsrp1, Fkbp2, and Pcsk1n were also FDR-significant. Ribosome occupancy and protein synthesis were not measured.

Ribosome profiling and quantitative proteomics after independently validated Eif4e editing, compared with 4E-BP activation and MNK inhibition, would separate abundance loss from phosphorylation blockade. If the Rnpc3/Amy1/Rsrp1 state tracks only reduced EIF4E dosage, it could support a modest genetic-versus-pharmacologic response map.

Title: Cltc loss couples Jun-Stmn4 stress induction to loss of synaptic membrane transcripts
Core Idea: Cltc perturbation recurrently increased Jun and Stmn4 while reducing glutamate receptors, sodium channels, and vesicle-release genes in several neuronal classes. The strongest target-engaged responses in 155 MB Glut and 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA support an endomembrane/proteostasis stress state with synaptic liability. Similarity to Vps, V-ATPase, proteasome, and Sec31a perturbations indicates state convergence, not a single clathrin-controlled pathway.
Significance: The result links a core endocytic coat component to a reproducible neuronal stress-versus-synapse tradeoff. It supplies markers for studying CLTC neurodevelopmental disease and synaptic maintenance, but the direction is broadly compatible with known clathrin failure.
Prior expectation: Clathrin heavy-chain disruption was expected to impair synaptic-vesicle recycling from the [clathrin inactivation study](https://doi.org/10.1083/jcb.200804162), and a pathogenic CLTC variant was shown to reduce transferrin uptake and synaptic transmission in [patient-linked functional work](https://doi.org/10.3389/fnmol.2023.1170061). Stress induction alongside reduced synaptic transcripts is therefore plausible; recurrence of Jun and Stmn4 is the more specific atlas contribution.

In 066 CNU-HYa HY GABA, Cltc fell by -1.112 (FDR 0.0122), Jun rose by +1.963 (FDR 0.0175), Stmn4 by +1.484 (FDR 0.00109), and Unc13a fell by -1.010 (FDR 0.0227). Additional FDR-supported reductions included Gabrd in 052 Pvalb Gaba and Gria3, Grm3, Snap25, and Rph3a in 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, although Cltc RNA depletion was not detected in the latter class. These are RNA endpoints, not direct measurements of endocytosis or synaptic release.
Research community: Synaptic-vesicle, endocytosis, and CLTC-disease researchers could determine whether the Jun/Stmn4 state is caused by failed recycling or a general proteostasis burden. Live synaptic-vesicle recycling, transferrin uptake, surface-receptor proteomics, and electrophysiology should be measured after confirmed Cltc editing, with clathrin rescue and comparison to VPS or proteasome perturbation. A rescue that restores trafficking before normalizing the RNA state would establish the causal order.

Title: Ntrk2 loss lowers a Vgf-Atp2a2 branch in thalamic neurons and Akap5 in Pvalb neurons

Adult TrkB function in PV interneurons is supported by [cell-type-specific TrkB studies](https://doi.org/10.1523/JNEUROSCI.1848-20.2021), making the direction plausible but not the exact split between Vgf/Atp2a2 and Akap5.

Confirmed Ntrk2 editing with BDNF stimulation, phospho-ERK/AKT measurements, VGF secretion, AKAP5 protein localization, and cell-class-specific rescue would separate impaired trophic signaling from secondary activity changes. Title: TBL1XR1 and GSK3B perturbations do not collapse onto a canonical beta-catenin state
Core Idea: Tbl1xr1 perturbation yielded a compact Vangl1/Prkcg/Rasa4-down, Kctd16-up response in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut that was nearly uncorrelated with the much larger Ctnnb1 and Tcf7l2 states. Gsk3b was depleted in the same class without the Axin2/Lef1 output seen after Apc perturbation. These two divergences constrain a simple Wnt-neighbor interpretation, but they do not establish a shared noncanonical pathway between TBL1XR1 and GSK3B.

Ctnnb1 and Tcf7l2 produced much larger signatures in the same class but had near-zero Pearson correlations with Tbl1xr1. Gsk3b target RNA was depleted without canonical Axin2/Lef1 induction, unlike Apc perturbation. Gsk3a was not assayed, so redundancy is a major alternative, and the two sparse states do not identify a common pathway.

TBL1XR1 complex proteomics and GSK3A/GSK3B double perturbation, alongside APC and CTNNB1 controls, would distinguish corepressor-specific output from masked Wnt signaling. A positive alternative mechanism would justify follow-up; a negative result would mainly refine annotation of these perturbations.

Title: Thra loss represses Hr and Adgrb1 in thalamic Prkcd-Grin2c neurons
Core Idea: Thra perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut reduced Hr and Adgrb1, with Hcn2 decreasing only at FDR 0.10. Ncoa1 perturbation did not reproduce the branch, but Ncoa1 engagement was weak. Hr repression after Thra loss is therefore strongly precedented; coupling to Adgrb1 in this postnatal neuronal class is the newer element.

Hcn2 decreased by -0.269 at FDR 0.0736 and Rogdi increased by +0.258 at FDR 0.0980, so both remain FDR-0.10 components. Ncoa1 perturbation left Hr and Adgrb1 at FDR 1.0, but Ncoa1 itself also had FDR 1.0; this is not a decisive coactivator test. The experiment did not measure thyroid-hormone occupancy, receptor protein, or ligand state.

THRA CUT&RUN and T3-response time courses after confirmed Thra editing, with wild-type and DNA-binding-deficient rescue, would distinguish direct receptor regulation from secondary state change. If Adgrb1 is directly occupied and hormone-responsive, a focused follow-up could examine its contribution to THRA-dependent excitability.

Title: Atxn10 and Eef1a2 perturbations share selected synaptic and cytoskeletal liabilities without broad phenocopy
Core Idea: Atxn10 perturbation reduced Atp1a3, Snap25, and tubulin-associated transcripts in responsive neuronal classes, while Eef1a2 perturbation reduced App, Aplp2, Atp1a3, Tubb3, and Tuba1a most clearly in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut. It is also distinct from SCA10's usual toxic-repeat mechanism because this experiment lowered total Atxn10.

Matched edits in the same neuronal class, live axonal transport imaging, microtubule stability assays, and rescue with ATXN10 or EEF1A2 would distinguish functional convergence from coincident RNA loss. A shared transport phenotype would support a focused cross-disease vulnerability study without implying that SCA10 and EEF1A2 disorders are mechanistically identical.

Title: Spliceosome and TREX perturbations repeatedly generate posterior RNA-processing abundance states
Core Idea: Cdc40, Rnpc3, Thoc1, and Thoc2 perturbations each altered RNA-processing-related transcript abundance in selected posterior, thalamic, or inhibitory neuronal classes, but their specific outputs were not interchangeable. Cdc40 lowered Srrm2/Rsrp1/Srsf6 while increasing Pde4a; Thoc1 repeatedly lowered Snhg11/Rsrp1 while inducing genes such as Anxa6, Dgkz, and Gtf2i; Rnpc3 and Thoc2 produced distinct branches. The shared biological interpretation is vulnerability to partial RNA-machinery disruption, not a single common regulon.
Significance: The repeated but nonidentical states show that perturbing constitutive RNA machinery does not simply produce uniform housekeeping collapse in neurons. This may help prioritize cell-state and disease studies for individual spliceosome or TREX genes, although the merged relationship is too heterogeneous to support one mechanism.
Prior expectation: CDC40/PRP17 mutations were already connected to pontocerebellar hypoplasia in [human and model work](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33220177/), and the THO complex was shown to coordinate synaptic transcripts and dopamine-neuron survival in [Cell](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30146163/). THOC2 dysfunction also causes R-loop accumulation and neurodevelopmental damage in [Nature Communications](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38331934/). Neuronal vulnerability was expected; the partially recurring abundance states and their divergence were the atlas-level observation.
Support: Cdc40 perturbation produced 624 FDR genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, including Srrm2 log2FC -0.448 (FDR 1.27e-10), Ogt -0.401 (FDR 1.27e-10), and Pde4a +0.789 (FDR 1.81e-8), and 431 in 155 MB Glut, including Rsrp1 -0.691 (FDR 4.05e-13), Srsf6 -0.643 (FDR 1.07e-7), and Pde4a +1.083 (FDR 7.36e-21). Thoc1 perturbation yielded 303 FDR genes in 151, with Snhg11 -0.413 (FDR 3.92e-13), Anxa6 +1.299 (FDR 1.16e-12), and Dgkz +0.622 (FDR 6.65e-12), and related Snhg11/Rsrp1 decreases in 155, 191, and 066. Thoc2 had only eight FDR genes in 052 Pvalb Gaba and no FDR self-transcript decrease. The assay measured abundance rather than exon usage, R-loops, export, or spliceosome assembly, and the perturbations are mechanistically non-equivalent.
Research community: Spliceosome, mRNA-export, and neurodevelopmental-disease researchers could compare these perturbations using the same molecular readouts rather than treating them as one state. Long-read RNA sequencing, nascent-RNA export imaging, R-loop mapping, and spliceosome-complex biochemistry after verified editing would identify which defects are shared and which are target-specific. A reproducible shared defect could motivate a limited cross-gene vulnerability framework; otherwise the result should remain a map of distinct RNA-machinery lesions.

Title: Multiple mitochondrial-maintenance perturbations converge on an amino-acid stress state
Core Idea: Lonp1 perturbation induced Ddit3 and Slc7a1 in 217 P MY Pineal Glut, while Dnm1l, Opa1, and Hspa9 perturbations produced stronger Asns, Chac1, Cars, and Shmt2 responses in other neuronal classes. The relationship is a many-to-one convergence of mitochondrial proteostasis and dynamics lesions on an ATF4/ISR-like endpoint. Lonp1 is the weakest arm, and the atlas does not establish that all perturbations activate the same upstream sensor.
Significance: The cross-target convergence reinforces amino-acid and one-carbon transcription as a common neuronal response to mitochondrial disruption. It is scientifically useful for comparing mitochondrial disease mechanisms and rescue strategies, but the general ISR connection is established and the focal Lonp1 evidence is limited.
Prior expectation: LONP1 loss was already reported to trigger a mitochondrial integrated stress response in [human-cell work](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30061372/), and ATF4 was identified as a central mammalian mitochondrial-stress regulator by [Quirós and colleagues](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28566324/). ATF5 participation in mammalian mitochondrial UPR signaling was shown in [Current Biology](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cub.2016.06.002). Thus, convergence on DDIT3 and amino-acid genes was expected in broad terms.
Support: After Lonp1 perturbation in 217 P MY Pineal Glut, Ddit3 increased by log2FC +1.578 (FDR 0.00763) and Slc7a1 by +1.648 (FDR 0.0401). Dnm1l perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut increased Asns by +1.017 (FDR 1.72e-14) and Chac1 by +2.604 (FDR 7.01e-11). Opa1 perturbation in 155 MB Glut increased Cars by +0.910 (FDR 2.06e-19) and Asns by +0.916 (FDR 1.85e-10), while Hspa9 perturbation in 151 increased Shmt2 by +2.597 (FDR 2.99e-16). The shared direction supports state convergence, but Lonp1 target engagement was not cleanly measured and metabolic flux, organelle morphology, and ISR sensor activation were absent.
Research community: Mitochondrial-disease and integrated-stress researchers could identify whether the same ISR branch is necessary across these lesions. Matched Lonp1, Dnm1l, Opa1, and Hspa9 perturbations should be combined with mitochondrial morphology, proteostasis, amino-acid flux, phospho-eIF2α, and ATF4 occupancy, plus sensor-specific genetic suppression. A shared rescue would justify using the transcript state as a cross-lesion biomarker; divergent rescue would instead separate distinct mitochondrial stress routes.

Title: Dhdds perturbation induces ER and amino-acid stress while reducing neuronal effector transcripts
Core Idea: Dhdds perturbation increased Chac1, Ddit3, Herpud1, Hsp90b1, Asns, and Creld2 in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut while reducing Atp1a3, Chga, Gabbr2, Gabrd, and Grin2b; a narrower chaperone response recurred in 155 MB Glut. The observation is consistent with impaired dolichol-phosphate-dependent glycosylation causing ER stress. Because Dhdds RNA was not FDR-depleted in the responsive classes, the result remains a guide-associated DHDDS-pathway state rather than a confirmed dose-response.
Significance: The paired stress and neuronal-effector arms offer a molecular phenotype for DHDDS-related neurologic disease and a way to test whether glycosylation support rescues neuronal function. The pathway connection is expected, so the main contribution is the resolved neuronal response rather than a new mechanism.
Prior expectation: DHDDS was already known as an enzyme in dolichol-linked protein glycosylation from its curated [UniProt record](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q86SQ9) and the [Reactome dolichol pathway](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-4419978). Human DHDDS mutations were linked to retinal disease in [genetic studies](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21572394/), making secretory and ER stress after perturbation plausible.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Dhdds perturbation increased Chac1 by log2FC +2.573 (FDR 1.30e-5), Ddit3 by +1.268 (FDR 0.00241), Herpud1 by +0.618 (FDR 0.00386), Hsp90b1 by +0.501 (FDR 0.00263), Asns by +0.669 (FDR 0.0107), and Creld2 by +1.006 (FDR 0.0111). Atp1a3 decreased by -0.255 (FDR 0.0164), Gabrd by -0.357 (FDR 0.0251), and Grin2b by -0.212 (FDR 0.0422). In 155 MB Glut, Hsp90b1 rose by +0.602 (FDR 0.00190), Herpud1 by +0.642 (FDR 0.00479), and Dnajb11 by +0.742 (FDR 0.0107), while Atp1a3 fell by -0.383 (FDR 7.81e-6). Dhdds target RNA and glycosylation flux were not FDR-confirmed.
Research community: Congenital-glycosylation, dolichol-metabolism, and neuronal ER-stress researchers could test whether the RNA state follows loss of dolichol-phosphate synthesis. Independent editing with DHDDS protein, dolichol species, and glycoprotein occupancy measurements, followed by wild-type rescue or pathway-substrate supplementation, would distinguish DHDDS-dependent glycosylation failure from nonspecific editing stress. Confirmation would support a focused search for the neuronal glycoproteins whose loss drives the effector-gene arm.

Title: Hspa5 perturbation induces a BiP-centered ER response with Hspa9-like features
Core Idea: Hspa5 perturbation increased Hsp90b1, Nfe2l1, Pdia4, and Derl3 in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut. The score state resembled Hspa9 and, more weakly, Uba5 and Ufc1 perturbations. The direct observation is an ER-chaperone response after Hspa5 guide assignment; overlap with mitochondrial Hsp70 and UFMylation perturbations is correlative and does not establish organelle crosstalk or epistasis.
Significance: The response supplies a compact BiP-insufficiency readout and suggests that distinct proteostasis lesions can enter related neuronal states. Because BiP/UPR biology is established and the cross-pathway relationships are based on similarity, the result is best used to design discriminating stress assays.
Prior expectation: HSPA5/BiP was already established as an ER-lumenal chaperone and regulator of UPR sensors, summarized in the authoritative [UPR review by Walter and Ron](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1209038) and its curated [mouse UniProt entry](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P20029). Induction of ER chaperones after Hspa5 perturbation was therefore expected; resemblance to Hspa9 and UFMylation states is the exploratory component.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut after Hspa5 perturbation, Hsp90b1 increased by log2FC +1.078 (FDR 0.00879), Nfe2l1 by +0.751 (FDR 0.00879), Pdia4 by +1.480 (FDR 0.0130), and Derl3 by +4.880 (FDR 0.0343). In the same class, Hspa9 perturbation strongly induced Shmt2 (+2.597, FDR 2.99e-16) and Chac1 (+3.126, FDR 7.44e-12), contributing to state similarity. Hspa5 self-RNA was not FDR-depleted, which does not disprove editing but leaves protein engagement unresolved; Uba5/Ufc1 support consists of signature similarity rather than direct interaction.
Research community: ER-stress, mitochondrial-proteostasis, and UFMylation researchers could distinguish a shared integrated-stress endpoint from direct cross-organelle coupling. Matched perturbations should be profiled for BiP and mortalin protein, UFM1 conjugates, compartment-specific proteostasis reporters, and ISR activation, with pathway-specific rescue. If only the downstream ISR is shared, the finding remains a stress-state comparator; selective cross-rescue would motivate a more substantive organelle-coupling study.

Title: Hnrnpc perturbation repeatedly shifts RNA-processing gene abundance across posterior neuronal classes
Core Idea: Hnrnpc perturbation produced recurrent increases in Txnl1, Mterf3, Zbed4, Leng8, and Slc25a36 and decreases in Luc7l2 across several thalamic, midbrain, hindbrain, hypothalamic, and Pvalb classes. The atlas resolves a repeated RNA-abundance state associated with HNRNPC perturbation. It does not measure the alternative-splicing defects expected from HNRNPC biology, and the response distribution cannot be separated cleanly from editing and power differences.
Significance: The recurrent module provides candidate abundance biomarkers for HNRNPC insufficiency and complements the field's focus on exon usage. Its scientific value is moderate until the abundance state is linked to specific splice defects or neuronal dysfunction.
Prior expectation: hnRNP proteins were expected to control tissue-specific RNA processing and to contribute to brain disorders, as summarized in a recent [hnRNP neurobiology review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39086926/). HNRNPC haploinsufficiency was directly shown to alter splicing of intellectual-disability genes and cause neurodevelopmental disease in the [2023 human study](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ajhg.2023.07.005). The recurrent Txnl1/Mterf3/Zbed4/Luc7l2 abundance module was not previously established.
Support: Hnrnpc perturbation yielded 122 FDR genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, including Txnl1 log2FC +0.812 (FDR 7.38e-27), Tspan33 +0.725 (FDR 4.95e-14), Rbm25 -0.513 (FDR 4.95e-14), and Luc7l2 -0.360 (FDR 5.20e-11). In 155 MB Glut, Mterf3 increased by +0.822 (FDR 8.56e-11), Zbed4 by +1.376 (FDR 1.53e-7), Txnl1 by +0.615 (FDR 3.52e-7), and Luc7l2 decreased by -0.417 (FDR 6.83e-6). Smaller fragments recurred in 191, 217, 066, 145, 110, and 052. Hnrnpc self-RNA depletion was weak, and total-gene abundance cannot establish altered splicing or nuclear-speckle organization.
Research community: RNA-splicing and HNRNPC neurodevelopmental-disease researchers could connect the abundance module to the actual splice defect. Independent editing with HNRNPC protein validation, long-read isoform sequencing, eCLIP, and nuclear-speckle imaging across a high-response and a low-response class would distinguish direct RNA-processing vulnerability from unequal perturbation. A recurring set of HNRNPC-bound, mis-spliced neuronal genes would justify a focused disease-mechanism study.

Title: Kdm5c, Nsd1, and Zmynd8 perturbations converge on a Ptgds-Capn3 thalamic response
Core Idea: Kdm5c, Nsd1, and Zmynd8 perturbations each increased Ptgds and Capn3 in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, while retaining distinct additional outputs. This many-to-one relationship defines a shared chromatin-perturbation response hotspot rather than a single pathway or direct regulatory complex. Kdm5c self-RNA was only nominally reduced and Zmynd8 RNA increased, so the state cannot be treated uniformly as loss of each factor.
Significance: The Ptgds-Capn3 convergence may expose a common mature-neuron response to otherwise different chromatin lesions and offers a way to compare disease genes on a shared functional axis. The broad mechanistic alternatives and one-context concentration limit it to a moderate chromatin-state hypothesis.
Prior expectation: NSD1 was known to deposit H3K36me2 and pattern neuronal non-CG methylation in [Molecular Cell](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.molcel.2023.04.001), while ZMYND8 is a chromatin reader associated with NuRD recruitment as shown in [Cell Reports](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27732854/). KDM5C, NSD1, and ZMYND8 are all neurodevelopmentally relevant chromatin regulators, but a shared Ptgds-Capn3 abundance response was not a known common function.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Kdm5c perturbation increased Ptgds by log2FC +1.570 (FDR 8.54e-14) and Capn3 by +1.687 (FDR 4.01e-6), with Kdm5c itself at -0.292 and FDR 1.0. Nsd1 perturbation increased Ptgds by +0.814 (FDR 4.96e-5) and Capn3 by +1.552 (FDR 2.81e-5), while Nsd1 decreased by -0.341 (FDR 0.0276). Zmynd8 perturbation increased Ptgds by +1.735 (FDR 3.09e-19) and Capn3 by +2.045 (FDR 1.03e-15), but Zmynd8 itself increased by +0.496 (FDR 3.34e-5). The repeated downstream direction is real; direct chromatin occupancy, histone marks, and equivalently signed target perturbations are not established.
Research community: Neuronal epigenetics and chromatin-disorder researchers could determine whether Ptgds and Capn3 mark a common chromatin transition or a generic response to chromatin stress. CUT&Tag for H3K4 and H3K36 methylation, ZMYND8 occupancy, and chromatin accessibility after independently validated edits, coupled to target-specific rescue, would separate shared downstream state from direct co-regulation. Confirmation could support a modest cross-disorder biomarker study.

Title: Brd4 perturbation opposes synaptic-membrane and RNA-processing transcripts in thalamic neurons
Core Idea: Brd4 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut reduced Omg, Plch2, Sparcl1, Gria4, Gabrg2, Dpp6, and Mapk10 while increasing Pabpn1, Leng8, Gabbr1, Gabbr2, Snap25, and Vamp2. Yy1 perturbation showed moderate similarity to Brd4, Chd8, and Trrap states. The direct finding is a mixed synaptic-membrane versus nuclear-RNA response after BRD4 perturbation; the YY1 relationship is state similarity rather than physical enhancer cooperation.
Significance: The result provides a mature-neuron view of BET-protein perturbation and nominates a specific set of membrane genes that may be sensitive to BRD4 dosage. It could guide targeted enhancer studies, but established BRD4 transcription biology and unmeasured occupancy keep the expected impact moderate.
Prior expectation: BRD4 was already known to activate neuronal transcription, and BET inhibition impaired memory in the [Korb and colleagues study](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26301327/). YY1 was established as a structural regulator of enhancer-promoter loops in [Cell](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29224777/). These precedents make a neuronal chromatin response plausible but do not establish that the altered membrane genes are direct BRD4 or YY1 targets.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Brd4 perturbation produced 311 FDR-significant genes. Omg decreased by log2FC -1.142 (FDR 1.50e-10), Plch2 by -1.283 (FDR 4.06e-10), Sparcl1 by -1.278 (FDR 1.25e-8), Gria4 by -0.618 (FDR 2.90e-5), and Gabrg2 by -0.838 (FDR 5.84e-5), while Pabpn1 increased by +0.425 (FDR 4.06e-10) and Leng8 by +0.391 (FDR 1.85e-8). Across the top 250 YY1-responsive genes, Brd4 correlations ranged from 0.321 to 0.412 in several classes, alongside Chd8 and Trrap. Brd4 self-RNA was not FDR-depleted, and neither occupancy nor enhancer-promoter contacts were measured.
Research community: BET-chromatin and neuronal enhancer researchers could test whether the membrane-gene arm is a direct BRD4 dependency. BRD4 degradation and independent editing should be compared by PRO-seq, BRD4/YY1 CUT&RUN, and enhancer-contact mapping, with rescue by wild-type or bromodomain-defective BRD4. Direct occupancy and rapid nascent-RNA loss would support a focused enhancer study; delayed changes would favor a secondary neuronal-state explanation.

Title: Rbfox1 perturbation lowers an axonal and excitability transcript module
Core Idea: Rbfox1 perturbation reduced Adgrb1, Dync1i2, Grin1, Vamp1, Scn1a, Nat8l, and related axonal or excitability transcripts, most strongly in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut and 155 MB Glut. The atlas therefore identifies gene-abundance outputs of RBFOX1 perturbation, distinct from the splice-isoform changes for which RBFOX1 is best known. Whether the abundance state is direct, secondary to mis-splicing, or shaped by unequal functional editing is unresolved.

Prior expectation: RBFOX1 was already expected to control neuronal splicing and cortical development, including cell-type-specific programs described by [Wamsley and colleagues](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2018.09.026), and its broad brain expression is documented by the [Human Protein Atlas](https://www.proteinatlas.org/ENSG00000078328-RBFOX1). Reduced axonal and ion-channel transcript abundance was plausible but not sufficient to identify the causal splice events.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut after Rbfox1 perturbation, Adgrb1 decreased by log2FC -0.513 (FDR 3.97e-17), Dync1i2 by -0.467 (FDR 2.64e-9), Grin1 by -0.246 (FDR 3.04e-4), Vamp1 by -0.530 (FDR 6.81e-4), and Scn1a by -0.320 (FDR 6.81e-4). In 155 MB Glut, Nat8l decreased by -0.468 (FDR 0.00175), and in 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut Adgrb1 decreased by -0.295 (FDR 0.0495). Rbfox1 self-RNA was not FDR-depleted, which leaves protein engagement unmeasured, and gene-level differential expression cannot reveal the anticipated splice defects.
Research community: Neuronal splicing, epilepsy, and axonal-physiology researchers could connect these abundance changes to specific RBFOX1-dependent isoforms. Long-read RNA sequencing and RBFOX1 eCLIP after verified editing, followed by isoform-specific rescue of candidate channels or transport genes and electrophysiology, would distinguish direct splice-mediated loss from a secondary state. A causal isoform would justify a focused therapeutic-rescue study.

Title: Sec63 perturbation induces an ER-chaperone response distinct from selected secretory-pathway perturbations
Core Idea: Sec63 perturbation increased Hspa5, Canx, Hyou1, and Chac1 in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, with an Hspa5 echo in 155 MB Glut. Perturbations of Ssr4, Stt3b, Sec23a, Sar1b, Ergic1, and Edem3 showed different expression profiles from this chaperone combination. The pattern defines a SEC63-associated ER import and folding-stress response for comparison with neighboring secretory-pathway perturbations.
Significance: The compact response offers a neuronal assay for SEC63 insufficiency and separates it from several neighboring secretory-pathway perturbations at the RNA endpoint. It is a useful mechanistic refinement of established ER-import biology, with limited broader impact until client proteins and functional consequences are identified.
Prior expectation: SEC63 was expected to assist SEC61-mediated ER import based on mammalian transport experiments by [Lang and colleagues](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22375059/), and its disease relevance includes impaired polycystin biogenesis in [genetic interaction studies](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21685914/). CHAC1 is a known ATF4-ATF3-CHOP output from [UPR studies](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19109178/), so chaperone and Chac1 induction were consistent with import stress.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut after Sec63 perturbation, Hspa5 increased by log2FC +0.924 (FDR 6.31e-4), Canx by +0.723 (FDR 0.00607), Hyou1 by +0.633 (FDR 0.00876), and Chac1 by +2.030 (FDR 0.0179). Hspa5 also increased in 155 MB Glut by +1.028 (FDR 0.0298). In 151, Ssr4, Stt3b, and Ergic1 each showed strong FDR-supported self-transcript depletion (-0.640, FDR 5.65e-5; -0.619, FDR 3.69e-4; and -0.820, FDR 2.56e-17, respectively) without the reported Sec63 chaperone module, but equivalent protein-level disruption and client load were not measured. Sec63 itself lacked an FDR self-row in the available evidence.
Research community: ER-translocation and SEC63-disease researchers could determine whether the response reflects failure of a restricted client set. Confirmed SEC63 editing should be followed by translocation reporters with diverse signal peptides, ER proteomics, BiP occupancy, and rescue by wild-type versus J-domain-defective SEC63, alongside matched Ssr4 and Stt3b controls. Identification of the clients whose import failure precedes the chaperone state would support a focused neuronal SEC63 mechanism.
