Title: Distinct endomembrane perturbations converge on a proteostasis-associated neighborhood with node-specific outputs
Core Idea: The atlas places ATP6V0C, CAML, PI4KA, DHDDS, VPS53, and VPS54 perturbations near endomembrane or proteostasis states, with node-specific leading outputs: ATP6V0C lowers Atp6v0a1, CAML raises Stmn4, and PI4KA lowers Slc12a5. These results define a many-to-one neighborhood in which acidification, membrane insertion, phosphoinositide synthesis, glycosylation, and tethering converge on related RNA states. The shared neighborhood and its node-specific outputs provide a framework for separating common organelle responses from pathway-specific effects.
Significance: This view could help endomembrane researchers select shared stress readouts while preserving the distinct initiating lesions. Because the group is heterogeneous and supported partly by modest correlations, it currently refines experimental organization more than it establishes a new pathway.
Prior expectation: [ATP6V0C is a V-ATPase V0 pore component](https://www.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/P63082), [PI4KA maintains plasma-membrane PI4P and PI(4,5)P2 pools](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24415756/), [DHDDS supplies dolichyl-phosphate for glycosylation](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-4419978), and [VPS53 participates in endocytic recycling](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25799061/). Convergence at a broad endomembrane-stress level was therefore plausible, whereas a single shared neuronal mechanism was not.
Support: ATP6V0C perturbation reduced Atp6v0a1 in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut (log2FC -0.577, FDR 2.39e-19), 155 MB Glut (-0.438, FDR 6.82e-13), and 052 Pvalb Gaba (-0.450, FDR 1.29e-7). CAML perturbation increased Stmn4 in 191 MB P MY GABA (+1.068, FDR 1.83e-4) and 052 Pvalb Gaba (+0.833, FDR 0.00363); PI4KA perturbation reduced Slc12a5 in 191 MB P MY GABA (-0.670, FDR 0.0136). The broader neighborhood rests on correlation and low-count marker findings, and the PI4KA pineal branch is low-n; shared RNA proximity cannot establish common flux, organelle physiology, or causal ordering.
Research community: Neuronal membrane-trafficking, lysosome, and proteostasis groups could test whether the shared neighborhood reflects a common organelle state. Parallel perturbations with live endolysosomal pH, PI4P, glycosylation, and cargo-trafficking measurements would determine whether the RNA overlap tracks one physiological variable or several. The proportionate outcome would be a cross-node phenotyping framework and a small number of mechanistically coherent subgroups.

Title: KLF9 perturbation defines a Wsb1/Rasgrp2/Pld3 thalamic module distinct from KLF7 and KLF13 outputs
Core Idea: Klf9-assigned cells in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut showed 19 FDR genes, including increased Wsb1, Rasgrp2, Pld3, Dzip1, Maged2, Hcn3, and Aplp1. Klf7 and Klf13 perturbations showed different leading marker directions in the same context. Together, these results define a KLF9-associated mature-thalamic state that distinguishes this perturbation from the selected KLF paralogs.
Significance: The result could identify a few downstream markers for comparing hormone-responsive KLF paralogs in mature neurons. Its mechanism and consequence are presently too narrow for more than a low-priority validation study.
Prior expectation: [Klf9 is directly induced by thyroid hormone in the postnatal brain](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19359381/) and [is required for late neuronal maturation](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19657039/). [KLF paralogs can differ in their effects on CNS axon growth](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19815778/), so paralog non-equivalence was plausible even though this marker set was unexpected.

Klf9 itself changed by -0.138 with FDR 1.0. In Klf7 and Klf13 perturbations, Wsb1, Rasgrp2, and Pld3 were not FDR-supported; however, unequal functional editing could still explain the apparent paralog boundary, and the weak SIN3A similarity does not establish a corepressor mechanism.

KLF9 occupancy at Wsb1, Rasgrp2, and Pld3 would distinguish direct regulation from a secondary state. If confirmed, this would support a focused mature-neuron KLF9 regulon study.

Title: ZMYM2 perturbation induces a Notum/Echdc2/F11r/Atp2a3 thalamic signature
Core Idea: Zmym2 guides yielded 22 FDR genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, with strong induction of Echdc2, F11r, Atp2a3, Ebf4, and Notum. This marker state links ZMYM2 perturbation to Notum signaling and Atp2a3-associated calcium handling in mature thalamic neurons. The implicated genes provide defined targets for testing ZMYM2-dependent chromatin regulation.

Without recurrence, occupancy, or functional consequence, it remains an incremental, low-priority extension of ZMYM2 chromatin biology.
Prior expectation: [ZMYM2 participates in chromatin corepressor complexes](https://elifesciences.org/articles/86669), and [heterozygous loss-of-function variants cause a syndromic congenital disorder](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ajhg.2020.08.013). Confirmation would justify a narrow study of this mature-neuronal output rather than a broad disease program.

Title: SP4 perturbation defines a Tsga10/Abhd11/Svip module in posterior glutamatergic neurons
Core Idea: The atlas reports corrected responses for SP4 perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut and 155 MB Glut, with Tsga10 and Abhd11 induction and supporting Svip, Strada, and Dnajc4 changes. This posterior glutamatergic marker program is distinct from developmental SP4–NMDAR biology. It provides an adult-neuron context for testing SP4 regulation of Tsga10, Abhd11, and the associated genes.

That inconsistency, together with increased rather than decreased Sp4 RNA, prevents a stronger biological conclusion.

Title: ZFP148 perturbation lowers spliceosomal and synaptic-marker transcripts in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons
Core Idea: Zfp148 perturbation produced its corrected response in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, where Srrm2, Srsf9, Cacnb3, Slitrk5, Ptprn, and Rasgrp2 decreased. The resulting marker state connects ZFP148 perturbation with spliceosome-associated and synaptic transcripts. Prior expectation: [Reactome places SRSF9, SRRM2, and LUC7L2 in mRNA-splicing machinery](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-72163), while [ZNF148 paralog cooperation is known to be context dependent](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37948511/). These priors make the marker identities interpretable but do not predict direct ZFP148 control of them.
Support: Within 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Zfp148 perturbation decreased Srrm2 by log2FC -0.288 (FDR 0.00164), Cacnb3 by -0.939 (FDR 0.00255), Srsf9 by -0.569 (FDR 0.0103), Slitrk5 by -0.762 (FDR 0.0295), and Ptprn and Rasgrp2 by -0.350 and -0.948 (both FDR 0.0450). Luc7l2 was just outside threshold (-0.249, FDR 0.0543), and Zfp148 itself was unchanged (-0.044, FDR 1.0). The major gap is that abundance changes in splice factors do not demonstrate altered splicing or direct ZFP148 regulation.

A reproducible isoform consequence would turn the marker panel into a defined ZFP148–splicing study; without it, the result should remain a small observational lead.

Title: NR2F1 perturbation couples Sorl1 induction to Sema7a repression in thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons
Core Idea: Nr2f1 perturbation increased Sorl1 and decreased Sema7a at FDR significance in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut. Cortical L5-6 IT and CTX-CGE GABA populations exhibited different downstream profiles. Together, the results define an adult NR2F1-associated thalamic response centered on opposing membrane-trafficking and guidance genes.

Nr2f1 itself was only nominally lower (-0.225, FDR 1.0); Chst1, Igsf21, Lrrtm4, Ncan, and Plxdc1 were also nominal rather than FDR-supported. Occupancy at Sorl1 and Sema7a, followed by neurite or synaptic phenotyping, would decide whether this is direct regulation with a functional consequence. Confirmation would motivate a compact adult-NR2F1 study.

Title: KLF7 perturbation links Tuba1a repression to a thalamic cytoskeletal transcript pattern
Core Idea: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Klf7 perturbation decreased Tuba1a, accompanied by directionally aligned changes in Coro1a, Sptan1, Stmn2, Map1b, Map6, and Dctn1. This pattern defines a cytoskeletal readout for studying KLF7-dependent projection maintenance in mature thalamic neurons. It also connects the adult perturbation response to KLF7's established developmental role in neuronal morphogenesis.
Significance: The result can nominate Tuba1a as a sensitive marker for testing KLF7 function in mature neurons. Its sparse support and lack of the expected Ntrk1/CDKN/Rac3 outputs make it incremental and low priority.
Prior expectation: [KLF7 is expressed during neurogenesis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11336497/) and [mouse genetics established roles in neuronal morphogenesis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15964824/). Those studies predicted axonal or cytoskeletal consequences, so the broad direction is not surprising even though the adult thalamic marker is new.

Coro1a (-0.432, FDR 0.0526), Sptan1 (-0.135, FDR 0.0902), Stmn2 (-0.248, FDR 0.180), Map1b (-0.193, FDR 0.464), Map6 (-0.220, FDR 0.690), and Dctn1 (-0.139, FDR 0.748) did not clear 5% FDR. Only if the weaker cytoskeletal genes and a cellular phenotype replicate would a larger mature-neuron KLF7 project be justified.

Title: GABRD perturbation links Cttnbp2 induction to thalamic Prkcd/Grin2c neurons
Core Idea: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Gabrd perturbation strongly reduced Gabrd RNA and increased Cttnbp2 as an FDR-supported non-target response. Gabrd RNA was also reduced in 052 Pvalb Gaba, which showed a different downstream profile. This population contrast defines Cttnbp2 as a focused reporter for testing how delta-GABA-A perturbation interfaces with synaptic-scaffold regulation.
Significance: Cttnbp2 may provide a simple molecular readout linking tonic-inhibition perturbation to synaptic-scaffold biology, but no direct connection is known and the effect is small. This warrants a targeted physiology-plus-expression experiment rather than a larger program.

Those priors predict electrophysiological consequences more directly than a Cttnbp2 RNA response.

Transcript loss supports the assay direction but does not by itself prove functional receptor disruption; Cttnbp2 has no validated causal link to delta-receptor physiology, and differences between the two populations may reflect power or state.

Gabrd rescue and direct manipulation of Cttnbp2 would determine whether it is causal, compensatory, or incidental. A positive result would support a narrow receptor-to-scaffold mechanism.

Title: ACSL4 perturbation increases Srebf2 alongside sterol-gene shifts in superficial cortex
Core Idea: Acsl4 perturbation increased Srebf2 in 007 L2-3 IT CTX Glut, with directionally aligned changes in several sterol genes and a pattern opposite to direct Srebf2 loss. Acsl4 RNA was strongly reduced in 151 TH and 155 MB, where the Srebf2 response differed from that in superficial cortex. This regional contrast frames a testable ACSL4–SREBF2 lipid-feedback relationship in mature forebrain neurons.
Significance: If validated, an ACSL4-dependent neuronal lipid-feedback circuit separated from the dominant thalamic and midbrain contexts could connect phospholipid remodeling, ferroptosis susceptibility, and cholesterol homeostasis and merit high-priority follow-up. The potentially large consequence explains Priority 4, while Support 2 reflects that the current atlas evidence is far below what that interpretation requires.
Prior expectation: [ACSL4 is an established determinant of ferroptosis-sensitive lipid composition](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27842070/), whereas [SREBP feedback governs sterol-homeostasis transcription](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tibs.2018.01.005). Direct ACSL4-to-SREBF2 compensation in mature forebrain neurons was not an established expectation.

In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Acsl4 was -0.995 (FDR 3.83e-16) but Srebf2 was +0.111 (FDR 1.0); in 155 MB Glut, Acsl4 was -1.042 (FDR 7.89e-6). Direct Srebf2 perturbation in 155 reduced Srebf2 (-1.652, FDR 3.98e-27), Dhcr24 (-0.951, FDR 9.22e-6), and Insig1 (-1.063, FDR 1.26e-5). One corrected Acsl4 downstream row cannot establish a sterol program, lipid feedback, or regional restriction, and no lipidomics or ferroptosis phenotype was measured.

ACSL4 rescue and SREBF2 epistasis would distinguish feedback from a coincidental transcript. Only a replicated biochemical axis would justify the larger cross-field program implied by the priority score.

Title: TRRAP perturbation represses an imprinted and noncoding RNA module distinct from the MYC perturbation profile
Core Idea: Trrap perturbation produced a large, recurrent state in 151 TH, 155 MB, and 191 MB P MY GABA, including repression of Snrpn, Snhg11, Peg3, Mest, and Nap1l5 and induction of Leng8, Rnpc3, and Kpna1. Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Trrap guides decreased Snrpn by log2FC -0.485 (FDR 4.94e-23), Snhg11 by -0.474 (FDR 7.48e-23), Peg3 by -0.720 (FDR 4.46e-22), and Mest by -1.442 (FDR 8.46e-13), while increasing Leng8 by +0.312 (FDR 2.30e-10), Rnpc3 by +0.525 (FDR 4.79e-9), and Kpna1 by +0.456 (FDR 3.51e-8). Snrpn (-0.649, FDR 7.28e-18), Snhg11 (-0.430, FDR 3.50e-14), and Nap1l5 (-0.763, FDR 7.54e-11) also decreased in 155 MB Glut. Trrap RNA itself was unchanged, and Myc RNA and the four queried module genes all had FDR 1.0 under Myc guides; that null cannot establish equivalent MYC disruption, while RNA abundance cannot establish imprinting, methylation, or allele-specific control.

Title: GABRB2 and ATP2B1 perturbations oppose the ANK3 thalamic activity-associated RNA state
Core Idea: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Ank3 perturbation lowered Nr4a1, Vgf, Opn3, and calcium/synaptic transcripts, whereas Gabrb2 and Atp2b1 perturbations induced several of those activity-associated genes. This signed transcriptional contrast links GABA-A receptor, calcium-pump, and ANK3 perturbations to a shared excitability-associated gene set for physiological testing.

These functions make opposing activity states plausible but do not predict direct coupling to ANK3.

Gabrb2 perturbation increased Nr4a1 by +0.804 (FDR 2.51e-4) and Vgf by +0.573 (FDR 0.00203); direct Atp2b1 perturbation was reported to increase Opn3 (+0.621, FDR 0.00127), Vgf (+0.571, FDR 0.00248), Nr4a1 (+0.646, FDR 0.00356), and Fos (+1.026, FDR 9.33e-4). Yet the full-score anticorrelations with Ank3 were only r=-0.129 for Gabrb2 and r=-0.116 for Atp2b1. Gabrb1 nulls are not equivalent perturbation controls, and shared activity genes could reflect generic changes in firing rather than molecular interaction.

ANK3 rescue plus pharmacologic normalization of GABAergic current or calcium extrusion would determine whether the RNA reversal follows excitability or a specific molecular pathway. A reproducible physiological mediator would justify a larger cross-system study.

Title: Rab7, Slc17a5, and Tsc1 perturbations converge on lysosomal and sterol RNA outputs in neuronal populations

Title: A lone Vegfa response after TFRC perturbation weakly suggests a neuronal low-iron compensation state
Core Idea: Tfrc guides produced an FDR-supported Vegfa increase in 145 MH-LH TH Glut and nominal glycolytic changes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut. Ireb2 perturbation nominally lowered Tfrc in several populations but did not produce a broad corrected iron-response program. A low-iron or HIF-like compensatory state is plausible, but the atlas evidence is too sparse and target perturbation too uncertain for an eligible mechanistic conclusion.
Significance: If confirmed with iron measurements, the result could reveal how selected neurons transcriptionally respond to impaired transferrin uptake and merit moderate investigation. At present it is a one-gene lead, not evidence of glycolytic rewiring, ferroptosis, or an IRP2–TFRC program.
Prior expectation: [TFRC is required for nervous-system development](https://doi.org/10.1038/7727), [Irp2-deficient mice show reduced transferrin receptor and CNS iron defects](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15831703/), and [biallelic human IREB2 loss causes neurodegeneration with functional iron deficiency](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30915432/). A compensatory hypoxia-like response was plausible but not directly established by these priors.
Support: In 145 MH-LH TH Glut, Tfrc perturbation increased Vegfa by log2FC +0.940 (FDR 0.0328), while Tfrc itself was -0.294 (FDR 1.0) and Aldoa, Ldha, Pfkl, Pfkp, and Pkm all had FDR 0.868-1.0. In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Vegfa (+0.541, FDR 0.250), Ldha (+0.766, FDR 0.370), Pfkp (+0.346, FDR 0.0523), and the other glycolytic genes were not significant. Ireb2 perturbation nominally lowered Tfrc in 052 Pvalb Gaba (-0.483), 151 (-0.417), and 155 MB Glut (-0.468), but all had FDR 1.0. Without verified editing, intracellular iron, HIF stabilization, or a multi-gene corrected response, the low-iron interpretation remains weak.
Research community: Neuronal iron and ferroptosis researchers could repeat TFRC and IREB2 editing with labile-iron sensors, HIF protein assays, metabolic flux, and rescue by iron delivery. A matched-power comparison would determine whether Vegfa is reproducible or a lone outlier. Only biochemical confirmation should trigger a broader neuronal iron-compensation project.

Novelty Score: 4

Priority Score: 2
Title: FTO guides repeatedly lower Rsrp1, but a mechanistic bridge to spliceosome perturbations is unproven
Core Idea: Fto perturbation produced only ten FDR rows atlas-wide, with recurrent Rsrp1 repression in 151 TH, 155 MB, and cortical 008 neurons and Dazap1 induction in 191 MB P MY GABA. Restricted signature comparisons linked the state to PRPF6 and DDX23 and opposed it to FUS. The recurrent markers are real observations, but the proposed FTO-to-spliceosome mechanism rests on sparse output, correlation, and unverified FTO disruption.
Significance: Rsrp1 is a compact, reproducible reporter worth checking in an RNA-modification context. Without m6A, splicing, or protein-level evidence, the finding is a low-priority hypothesis rather than a new connection between FTO biology and spliceosome control.
Prior expectation: [FTO can demethylate m6A in RNA](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22002720/), while [RSRP1 is associated with spliceosomal function](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34042961/). Those facts make the bridge conceivable but do not establish direct regulation.
Support: Under Fto perturbation, Rsrp1 decreased in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut by log2FC -0.391 (FDR 3.32e-5), in 155 MB Glut by -0.605 (FDR 1.79e-9), and in 008 L2-3 IT ENT PPP RSP Glut by -0.614 (FDR 0.0211). Dazap1 increased in 191 MB P MY GABA by +0.612 (FDR 0.0132), and Amy1 increased in 151 by +0.452 (FDR 0.00399). Every measured Fto self-transcript row had FDR 1.0; no m6A, splice-junction, or direct-target assay was performed, and modest signature correlations cannot supply that missing mechanism.
Research community: Epitranscriptomics and neuronal splicing groups could validate independent FTO editing, quantify m6A, and test whether Rsrp1 abundance and splice-junction usage are rescued by wild-type but not catalytic-dead FTO. A catalytic-dependence result would elevate the lead; absent that, Rsrp1 should remain an empirical reporter only.

Priority Score: 2
Title: WDFY3 guides suppress Jun, Stmn4, and synaptic transcripts in thalamic neurons, but the autophagy interpretation is unresolved
Core Idea: Wdfy3 perturbation produced 17 FDR genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, led by decreases in Jun, Stmn4, Gpm6a, and Syt11, plus one Stmn4 decrease in 155 MB Glut. The observation is a thalamic-heavy activity/synaptic RNA state. Interpreting it as a WDFY3-dependent autophagy, AP-1/JNK, or connectivity mechanism is speculative because Wdfy3 RNA did not clear FDR and no autophagic flux was measured.
Significance: The state could provide molecular markers for testing postnatal WDFY3 function, but the relationship largely extends known WDFY3 roles in neuronal connectivity and proteostasis. Its mechanistic ambiguity and context imbalance make it low priority.
Prior expectation: [WDFY3/ALFY is required for aggregate-selective autophagy](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20417604/), [mouse Wdfy3 loss disrupts neuronal connectivity](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27648578/), and [WDFY3 haploinsufficiency affects synaptic and mitochondrial phenotypes](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34187232/). Synaptic or stress-associated transcription was therefore plausible.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Wdfy3 perturbation reduced Stmn4 by log2FC -0.389 and Jun by -1.090 (both FDR 3.24e-9), Gpm6a by -0.346 (FDR 5.36e-4), and Syt11 by -0.171 (FDR 0.00896), while increasing Gls by +0.211 (FDR 7.14e-4). Stmn4 also decreased in 155 MB Glut (-0.663, FDR 3.73e-4). Wdfy3 itself was -0.209 with FDR 0.107, Jund and Mapk8 were not significant, and full-transcriptome neighbor correlations were modest. These data support a guide-associated RNA state but not loss of autophagy, AP-1/JNK signaling, or intrinsic thalamic specificity.
Research community: Selective-autophagy and neurodevelopment researchers could verify WDFY3 protein disruption and rescue, then measure aggregate clearance, autophagic flux, and neuronal activity alongside Jun/Stmn4. If the transcript state tracks a rescued autophagy defect, a focused postnatal WDFY3 study would be justified; otherwise it should remain a marker observation.

Title: KMT5B guides induce a small Ache/Syngap1/Grik1 thalamic signature despite no evidence of target-transcript loss
Core Idea: Kmt5b guides produced a corrected response in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, including increased Ache, Syngap1, and Grik1, with several translation-associated transcripts only nominal. Kmt5b RNA trended upward rather than downward in that population. The defensible observation is therefore guide association; a KMT5B-loss synaptic program is not established.
Significance: Because KMT5B is a neurodevelopmental-disease gene, the marker set is worth a compact replication attempt. The lack of molecular engagement, chromatin readouts, and physiology makes the result low priority and evidence-ineligible as a KMT5B mechanism.
Prior expectation: [KMT5B/SUV420H1 deposits H4K20 methylation](https://reactome.org/content/detail/R-HSA-5651654), [KMT5B variants cause developmental delay and neurodevelopmental phenotypes](https://doi.org/10.3389/fped.2022.844845), and [prefrontal Kmt5b deficiency can impair synaptic function](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41386-021-01029-y). A synaptic consequence was thus plausible, though not this exact signature.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Kmt5b perturbation increased Ache by log2FC +0.469 (FDR 0.00121), Syngap1 by +0.294 (FDR 0.0225), and Grik1 by +0.559 (FDR 0.0321); Nlgn2 was +0.316 at FDR 0.0516. Rab3a, Rab3gap2, Eif4e, Eif4g3, and Eif2a had FDR 0.177-0.532. Kmt5b itself increased by +0.269 (FDR 1.0), and H4K20 methylation, KMT5B protein, and synaptic physiology were not measured. The signature therefore cannot be assigned to KMT5B loss without independent validation.
Research community: KMT5B syndrome and neuronal chromatin groups could use independent guides, DNA/protein assays, and wild-type rescue, followed by H4K20 methylation and synaptic physiology. Reproduction of the Ache/Syngap1/Grik1 state only under verified KMT5B loss would warrant a narrow mechanistic follow-up.

Title: PROX1 guides create a Tmem145/Vgf-high, Rreb1/Kcnd2-low thalamic state without demonstrating PROX1 loss
Core Idea: Prox1 guides in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut induced Tmem145 and Vgf while repressing Rreb1, Nexn, Snx25, and Kcnd2. The measured Prox1 transcript increased, and endothelial/lymphatic partners such as Flt4 and Sox18 were inert in this neuronal context. The atlas thus identifies a guide-associated neuronal state, not a validated PROX1-loss regulon or meaningful uncoupling from lymphatic circuitry.
Significance: The signature could be useful for discovering an adult neuronal PROX1 role if it survives orthogonal validation. At present the direction of the target transcript and off-context comparators make it a low-priority, evidence-ineligible lead.
Prior expectation: [PROX1 is embedded in established VEGFC/FLT4 developmental circuitry](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-018-05039-1), but those lymphatic relationships are not expected to be active in mature neurons. The atlas marker pattern is therefore distinct from the canonical context, without yet showing that PROX1 caused it.
Support: The Prox1-guide comparison within 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut showed Tmem145 up by log2FC +0.833 (FDR 2.73e-15) and Vgf by +0.689 (FDR 1.53e-10), with Rreb1 down by -0.953 (FDR 4.45e-9), Nexn by -0.514 (FDR 0.00378), Snx25 by -0.462 (FDR 0.00510), and Kcnd2 by -0.327 (FDR 0.0139). Prox1 RNA increased rather than decreased, and Flt4 and Sox18 had FDR 1.0 in a neuronal population where their lymphatic roles are off-context. The response is statistically real but cannot be interpreted as PROX1 loss without DNA or protein validation.
Research community: Adult neuronal transcription-factor researchers could repeat PROX1 editing with allele and protein assays, rescue, and occupancy profiling at Tmem145, Vgf, and Rreb1. A verified loss-of-function response would justify functional tests of excitability or neuronal identity; otherwise the state should be treated as guide-associated.

Title: CREBBP guides produce an eight-gene thalamic state that EP300 does not reproduce, but paralog nonredundancy is not resolved
Core Idea: Crebbp perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut induced Stxbp2, Leng8, and Cfap298 while repressing Rtn4rl1, Dgkz, Ccdc141, Rbfox2, and Stmn4. Ep300 perturbation did not yield a corrected state in the same population, and the two score vectors were nearly uncorrelated. This is an apparent KAT3-paralog divergence, but neither perturbation had convincing target-transcript depletion, so unequal functional editing remains a major alternative.
Significance: CBP/p300 nonredundancy in adult neurons is already precedented, making this mainly a cell-state extension. The eight-gene signature could be useful, but the unresolved perturbation comparison keeps it low priority.
Prior expectation: [CBP is a transcriptional coactivator and lysine acetyltransferase](https://rest.uniprot.org/uniprotkb/Q92793), and [adult-brain work found CBP, but not p300, necessary for some adaptive gene programs](https://www.jneurosci.org/content/42/42/7984). A divergence was therefore expected more than surprising.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Crebbp perturbation increased Stxbp2 by log2FC +1.060 (FDR 5.01e-4), Leng8 by +0.248 (FDR 0.0472), and Cfap298 by +0.924 (FDR 0.0472), while reducing Rtn4rl1 by -0.617 (FDR 0.0240), Dgkz by -0.460, Ccdc141 by -1.355, Rbfox2 by -0.471, and Stmn4 by -0.439 (each FDR 0.0472). Ep300 had 155 perturbed cells and no gene at FDR below 0.10, versus 61 Crebbp-assigned cells and eight FDR genes, but both targets had weak RNA-level evidence of disruption. A null Ep300 signature cannot therefore prove biological nonredundancy.
Research community: CBP/p300 and neuronal plasticity researchers could perform matched-efficiency protein depletion and reciprocal rescue, then compare acetylation and enhancer activity at the eight loci. That experiment would distinguish true paralog specialization from guide performance. Even if confirmed, the likely outcome is a focused refinement of established CBP/p300 biology.

Title: HNRNPU, U2AF2, and NAA15 signatures share modest spliceosome–proteasome neighbors without a common marker-defined mechanism
Core Idea: Restricted signature analyses for Hnrnpu, U2af2, and Naa15 repeatedly nominated spliceosome perturbations such as Prpf6, Cdc40, Ddx23, and Snrnp70 and proteasome perturbations such as Pomp, Psmc1, Psmb4, and Psmc5. Their exact leading genes, however, differ substantially. The observation is a correlation neighborhood among stressed post-transcriptional states, not evidence for a shared spliceosome–proteasome pathway.
Significance: The neighborhood may help organize follow-up on RNA-processing stress and protein quality control, but it is too correlation-driven and heterogeneous to support more than a low-priority exploratory study.
Prior expectation: [HNRNPU has established RNA-processing, splicing, and chromatin functions](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/QuickGO/annotations?geneProductId=UniProtKB:Q00839), [U2AF2 recruits PRPF19 during cotranscriptional splicing](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21536736/), and [NAA15 is an auxiliary NatA subunit](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29754825/). Broad proteostasis consequences are plausible, but a single shared mechanism across these distinct factors was not expected.
Support: Hnrnpu perturbation in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut increased Dmtf1 by log2FC +1.418 (FDR 5.17e-30), Macf1 by +0.506 (FDR 9.93e-20), and Atxn2l by +0.468 (FDR 1.23e-12), while decreasing Rsrp1 by -0.417 (FDR 9.99e-24). Naa15 perturbation in the same population increased Ring1 (+0.898, FDR 8.75e-6) and Actr1b (+0.584, FDR 7.48e-5) while decreasing Thy1 (-0.420, FDR 9.72e-6) and Atp1b1 (-0.388, FDR 1.39e-5). U2af2 comparator genes were mostly not significant: Ddx23 +0.247 at FDR 0.0502, Cdc40 +0.261 at FDR 0.181, Prpf6 FDR 1.0, and Snrnp70 FDR 0.540. Whole-signature correlations were modest, including Naa15-to-Prpf6 r=0.206 and Naa15-to-Hnrnpu r=0.189 in 151; restricted Hnrnpu correlations were larger but selection-conditioned. These data do not establish a shared causal state.
Research community: RNA-splicing and proteostasis groups could repeat the perturbations under matched conditions and measure splice-junction usage, proteasome activity, ubiquitinated proteins, and stress signaling. Cross-rescue or epistasis would be needed to show that one physiological defect mediates the correlations. Without a shared functional readout, the neighborhood should remain an exploratory map.

Title: GABRG2 perturbation weakly associates with a Bdnf/Vgf activity response in glutamatergic neurons
Core Idea: Gabrg2 RNA was strongly reduced in cortical 005 L4-5 IT and 155 MB Glut. Bdnf was near the FDR threshold in cortex, whereas Vgf was FDR-supported in midbrain; Kcnb1 perturbation produced a clearer cortical Bdnf/Vgf/Nptx2 state and Gphn did not. The interpretation that reduced gamma-2-containing GABA-A signaling causes a shared disinhibition program is plausible but not supported by a strong, recurrent multi-gene Gabrg2 signature.
Significance: The result may guide a small experiment connecting inhibitory-receptor perturbation to activity-dependent trophic transcription. It is low priority because the strongest cortical genes are nominal, the cross-perturbation comparison is weak, and direct physiology is absent.
Prior expectation: [Gamma-2-containing GABA-A receptors and gephyrin organize inhibitory synapses](<https://www.cell.com/cell-reports/fulltext/S2211-1247(21)00958-7>), while [VGF is an activity- and BDNF-responsive neuronal gene](https://www.jneurosci.org/content/23/34/10800). A trophic response to disinhibition was therefore broadly expected.
Support: In 005 L4-5 IT CTX Glut, Gabrg2 perturbation reduced Gabrg2 by log2FC -0.795 (FDR 2.40e-10), while Bdnf increased by +1.321 at FDR 0.0632, Fosl2 by +1.292 at FDR 0.176, Nptx2 by +1.007 at FDR 0.487, and Vgf by +0.249 at FDR 1.0. In 155 MB Glut, Gabrg2 decreased by -1.299 (FDR 1.76e-23) and Vgf increased by +1.076 (FDR 0.00229), but Bdnf had FDR 1.0. Kcnb1 perturbation in cortex induced Bdnf (+1.063, FDR 0.00144), Vgf (+0.510, FDR 0.00144), and Nptx2 (+0.818, FDR 0.0393), whereas Gphn supplied only Cbarp as a corrected row. Target-RNA loss does not prove equivalent receptor physiology, and the Gabrg2 evidence is too sparse to infer a shared mechanism.
Research community: Inhibitory-synapse and activity-dependent transcription groups could combine Gabrg2 editing with patch clamp, calcium imaging, and time-resolved Bdnf/Vgf measurements. Restoring gamma-2 receptor function or normalizing firing should reverse the RNA response if disinhibition is causal. That focused test is proportionate to the evidence.

Title: LRP6 guides produce a weak Chgb/Nrbp2 thalamic response that does not establish canonical Wnt signaling or LRP5 divergence
Core Idea: Lrp6 perturbation yielded four FDR genes in 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, including Chgb up and Nrbp2 down, with Lef1 decreasing only nominally. Lrp5, Kremen1, and Alx4 did not reproduce those rows, and the state was described as aligned with Ctnnb1 and opposed to Apc. The exact evidence supports a small LRP6-associated signature, not a beta-catenin mechanism or receptor-paralog divergence.
Significance: A genuine adult neuronal LRP6 output could be relevant to synapse and neurodegeneration biology, but this particular signature is too weak for more than a low-priority replication. The Wnt interpretation depends on nominal or comparator evidence rather than a corrected canonical module.
Prior expectation: [LRP6 recruits canonical Wnt machinery upstream of beta-catenin and TCF/LEF transcription](https://reactome.org/content/query?q=LRP6%20WNT%20AXIN), [AXIN2 is a canonical beta-catenin feedback target](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11809809/), and [LRP5 and LRP6 have overlapping but nonidentical regulation](https://doi.org/10.3389/fcell.2021.670960). A Wnt-linked response was expected, but the observed core does not strongly recover it.
Support: In 151 TH Prkcd Grin2c Glut, Lrp6 perturbation increased Chgb by log2FC +0.352 (FDR 0.0462) and decreased Nrbp2 by -0.366 (FDR 0.0406); Lef1 decreased by -0.282 but had FDR 0.572, and Lrp6 itself was -0.230 with FDR 1.0. Under Lrp5, Kremen1, and Alx4 perturbations, the displayed target and comparator rows all had FDR 1.0. Thus the negative comparators are not validated matched perturbations, Axin2 is not supported by an exact corrected row here, and four FDR genes do not establish canonical Wnt output.
Research community: Neuronal Wnt researchers could repeat LRP6 and LRP5 editing with verified protein loss, beta-catenin localization, TCF reporter activity, and Axin2/Lef1 measurements. Rescue and matched perturbation strength are required before claiming paralog divergence. Unless a canonical response emerges, the Chgb/Nrbp2 pair should remain a small empirical lead.
